````javascript
128:           enabled: koDiagnosticsEnabled,
129:           bands: koDiagnosticBands,
130:         })
131:       : {};
132: 
133:     const endingMonotony = lang === 'ko' ? koreanEndingMonotony(sentences) : null;
134:     // KO uniform plain-다 register: low burstiness AND -다 dominance. Unlike the
135:     // standard burstiness trigger (cvBand) this does NOT require the 3-sentence
136:     // minimum, so it catches short AI Korean the band gate skips, while the -다
137:     // conjuncts keep formal/conversational human Korean out (DEFAULT_KO_ENDING_MONOTONY).
138:     const endingMonotonyHot = Boolean(
139:       endingMonotony &&
140:       allTokens.length >= koEndingMonotonyBands.minTokens &&
141:       cv != null &&
142:       cv < burstinessBands.low &&
143:       endingMonotony.daRatio != null &&
144:       endingMonotony.daRatio >= koEndingMonotonyBands.minDaRatio &&
145:       endingMonotony.daCount >= koEndingMonotonyBands.minDaCount
146:     );
147: 
148:     const lexiconHot = classifyLexiconHot(lex, {
149:       lang,
150:       densityThreshold: lexiconDensityThreshold,
151:       minHotMatches: lexiconMinHotMatches,
152:     });
153:     // Discourse-tell attribution (#391): this paragraph carries a tell AND the
154:     // document-level density gate fired (>=2 candor openers / >=3 breaks).
155:     const candorHot = discourseTells.fakeCandor.hot && paraCandorCounts[idx] >= 1;
156:     const thematicBreakHot = discourseTells.thematicBreaks.hot && paraThematicBreakCounts[idx] >= 1;
157:     const hot =
158:       cvBand === 'low' ||
159:       mattrBand === 'low' ||
160:       lexiconHot ||
161:       Boolean(koSignals.koDiagnostics?.hot) ||
162:       candorHot ||
163:       thematicBreakHot ||
164:       endingMonotonyHot;
165: 
166:     return {
167:       id: `P${idx + 1}`,
168:       sentenceCount: sentences.length,
169:       tokenCount: allTokens.length,
170:       burstiness: { cv, band: cvBand },
171:       mattr: { value: mattrValue, band: mattrBand },
172:       lexicon: { ...lex, hot: lexiconHot },
173:       ...koSignals,
174:       candorHot,
175:       candorCount: paraCandorCounts[idx],
176:       thematicBreakHot,
177:       thematicBreakCount: paraThematicBreakCounts[idx],
178:       endingMonotony,
179:       endingMonotonyHot,
180:       // Divider-only pseudo-paragraph (a bare `---` line between blank lines).
181:       // Hot attribution still applies (the divider itself is rewrite scope),
182:       // but prose gates use this to keep their ratios on actual prose.
183:       thematicBreakOnly: isThematicBreakOnly(paragraph),
184:       hot,
185:     };
186:   });
187: 
188:   return {
189:     lang,
190:     skipped: Boolean(skipReason),
191:     skipReason,
192:     paragraphs: analyzed,
193:     markupLeakage,
194:     discourseTells,
195:     translationese,
196:     koPostEditese,
197:     // No `discourseTells.hot` disjunct here: whenever the document-level density
198:     // gate fires, at least one paragraph carries the tell (candor regexes cannot
199:     // span paragraph breaks; thematic breaks are whole lines), so the
200:     // per-paragraph attribution above already makes some paragraph hot.
201:     hot: markupLeakage.leaked || structuralClassifier.hot === true || analyzed.some((p) => p.hot),
202:     structuralClassifier,
203:   };
204: }
205: 
206: export {
207:   splitParagraphs,
208:   splitSentences,
209:   splitProseSentences,
210:   tokenize,
211:   burstinessCV,
212:   mattr,
213:   classifyBurstiness,
214:   classifyMattr,
215:   classifyKoreanDiagnostics,
216:   koreanDiagnostics,
217:   commaDensity,
218:   koreanPosDiversityProxy,
219:   koreanSpacingFeatures,
220:   koreanPostEditeseFeatures,
221:   koreanEndingMonotony,
222:   detectKoreanRegister,
223:   loadLexicon,
224:   computeDensity,
225:   extractStructuralFeatures,
226:   structuralFeatureRecord,
227:   STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES,
228:   applyScaler,
229:   fitScaler,
230:   normalizeStructuralModel,
231:   predictStructuralScore,
232:   structuralModelVerdict,
233:   thresholdForMaxFpr,
234:   trainLogReg,
235:   loadStructuralModel,
236:   resolveStructuralModelPath,
237: };
````

## File: src/features/lexicon-core.js
````javascript
  1: // Browser-pure AI-lexicon parsing and density helpers per core/stylometry.md §16.
  2: 
  3: export const DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD = 3.0;
  4: export const DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES = {
  5:   default: 1,
  6:   ko: 2,
  7:   zh: 2,
  8:   ja: 2,
  9: };
 10: 
 11: // Parses the two well-known sections out of a lexicon markdown file.
 12: // Returns { strict: string[], phrases: string[] }.
 13: export function parseLexiconBody(body, { skipUnderscore = false } = {}) {
 14:   const strict = [];
 15:   const phrases = [];
 16:   let mode = null;
 17:   for (const rawLine of body.split('\n')) {
 18:     const line = rawLine.trim();
 19:     if (line.startsWith('## ')) {
 20:       const heading = line.toLowerCase();
 21:       if (heading.includes('strict matches')) mode = 'strict';
 22:       else if (heading.includes('multi-word phrases')) mode = 'phrases';
 23:       else mode = null;
 24:       continue;
 25:     }
 26:     if (mode && line.startsWith('- ')) {
 27:       // Normalize to NFC so visually identical entries don't fail to match
 28:       // tokens that arrive in a different normalization form.
 29:       const entry = line.slice(2).trim().normalize('NFC');
 30:       if (entry && (!skipUnderscore || !entry.startsWith('_'))) {
 31:         (mode === 'strict' ? strict : phrases).push(entry);
 32:       }
 33:     }
 34:   }
 35:   return { strict, phrases };
 36: }
 37: 
 38: // Phrases may include `~` as a wildcard standing in for up to 40 chars.
 39: // Inter-word whitespace becomes `\s+` and the wildcard becomes `[\s\S]{0,40}`
 40: // so a phrase hard-wrapped across a single newline inside a paragraph (which
 41: // only blank lines split) still matches instead of silently undercounting.
 42: export function phraseToRegex(phrase) {
 43:   // Collapse runs of consecutive wildcards (optionally whitespace-separated)
 44:   // into a single `~`. Adjacent `[\s\S]{0,40}` / `\s+` quantifiers overlap and
 45:   // backtrack exponentially on long inputs — a ReDoS reachable only via a
 46:   // hand-authored custom lexicon, but cheap to neutralize here (#527 H12).
 47:   const collapsed = String(phrase).replace(/~(?:\s*~)+/g, '~');
 48:   const escaped = collapsed
 49:     .toLowerCase()
 50:     .replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')
 51:     .replace(/\s+/g, '\\s+');
 52:   const withWildcard = escaped.replace(/~/g, '[\\s\\S]{0,40}');
 53:   return new RegExp(withWildcard);
 54: }
 55: 
 56: // Whole-word match for a multi-token non-CJK strict entry (contains a space,
 57: // hyphen, or apostrophe). Anchored on Unicode letter/digit boundaries so it
 58: // cannot hit inside a larger token, and whitespace tolerates soft line wraps.
 59: function strictEntryRegex(lowerEntry) {
 60:   const escaped = lowerEntry
 61:     .replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')
 62:     .replace(/\s+/g, '\\s+');
 63:   return new RegExp(`(?<![\\p{L}\\p{N}])${escaped}(?![\\p{L}\\p{N}])`, 'u');
 64: }
 65: 
 66: // Per-lexicon regex caches keyed by the lexicon object identity. Phrase and
 67: // strict-entry regexes depend only on the lexicon's entry text, never on the
 68: // paragraph under test, so they are compiled once per (lexicon, entry) pair
 69: // and reused across every computeDensity() call. WeakMaps keep the cache OFF
 70: // the lexicon object — Object.keys(lexicon) and JSON.stringify(lexicon) are
 71: // unchanged, and a lexicon's cache is garbage-collected with it. The compiled
 72: // regexes are non-global and non-sticky (no /g or /y flags), so repeated
 73: // .test() calls carry no lastIndex state and are safe to share. Entries are
 74: // keyed by string, so any later-added phrase/entry is compiled lazily.
 75: const phraseRegexCache = new WeakMap();
 76: const strictEntryRegexCache = new WeakMap();
 77: 
 78: function memoizedRegex(cache, lexicon, key, build) {
 79:   let byKey = cache.get(lexicon);
 80:   if (!byKey) {
 81:     byKey = new Map();
 82:     cache.set(lexicon, byKey);
 83:   }
 84:   let regex = byKey.get(key);
 85:   if (!regex) {
 86:     regex = build(key);
 87:     byKey.set(key, regex);
 88:   }
 89:   return regex;
 90: }
 91: 
 92: // Counts paragraph-level lexicon hits. Strict entries match whole-word
 93: // (Unicode-aware boundaries via \p{L}\p{N}); phrases match as substrings
 94: // with `~` wildcard support. Each entry counts at most once per paragraph.
 95: export function computeDensity(paragraphText, tokens, lexicon) {
 96:   const lowerText = paragraphText.toLowerCase();
 97:   const hits = [];
 98:   const tokenSet = new Set(tokens.map((t) => t.toLowerCase()));
 99: 
100:   // §16: English strict entries match whole-word; CJK strict entries are
101:   // approximated by substring. Korean inflection and zh/ja character fallback
102:   // mean `자리매김`, `可以说`, or `まとめると` may not survive as whole tokens, so
103:   // CJK keeps bare substring matching. A non-CJK strict entry that is not a
104:   // single whole token (it carries a space/hyphen/apostrophe, so tokenization
105:   // split or edge-stripped it) falls back to a whole-word boundary-anchored
106:   // match — never bare substring, which would hit inside a larger token and
107:   // break the documented whole-word contract (lexicon/ai-en.md).
108:   const cjkSubstring = ['ko', 'zh', 'ja'].includes(lexicon.lang);
109:   for (const entry of lexicon.strict) {
110:     const lowerEntry = entry.toLowerCase();
111:     if (tokenSet.has(lowerEntry)) {
112:       hits.push(entry);
113:       continue;
114:     }
115:     // lexicon/ai-en.md documents "A trailing `s` or `ing` form counts as the
116:     // same entry" for English strict entries, but only the exact token was
117:     // ever matched (#566). Accept the two documented inflections for non-CJK
118:     // single-token entries; CJK strict matching is substring-based below and
119:     // Korean inflections are absorbed by phrase matching per the same doc.
120:     if (!cjkSubstring && (tokenSet.has(`${lowerEntry}s`) || tokenSet.has(`${lowerEntry}ing`))) {
121:       hits.push(entry);
122:       continue;
123:     }
124:     const isMultiToken = /[^\p{L}\p{N}]/u.test(lowerEntry);
125:     if (cjkSubstring) {
126:       if (lowerText.includes(lowerEntry)) hits.push(entry);
127:     } else if (isMultiToken && memoizedRegex(strictEntryRegexCache, lexicon, lowerEntry, strictEntryRegex).test(lowerText)) {
128:       hits.push(entry);
129:     }
130:   }
131:   for (const phrase of lexicon.phrases) {
132:     if (memoizedRegex(phraseRegexCache, lexicon, phrase, phraseToRegex).test(lowerText)) hits.push(phrase);
133:   }
134: 
135:   const density = tokens.length > 0 ? (hits.length / tokens.length) * 1000 : 0;
136:   return { matches: hits.length, density, hits };
137: }
138: 
139: /**
140:  * @param {{ matches?: number, density?: number }} [lexiconStats]
141:  * @param {{ lang?: string, densityThreshold?: number, minHotMatches?: (number|Record<string, number>) }} [options]
142:  */
143: export function classifyLexiconHot(
144:   lexiconStats,
145:   {
146:     lang,
147:     densityThreshold = DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD,
148:     minHotMatches = DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES,
149:   } = {}
150: ) {
151:   const matches = lexiconStats?.matches ?? 0;
152:   const density = lexiconStats?.density ?? 0;
153:   const minMatches = resolveMinHotMatches(lang, minHotMatches);
154:   return matches >= minMatches && density > densityThreshold;
155: }
156: 
157: export function resolveMinHotMatches(lang, minHotMatches) {
158:   if (typeof minHotMatches === 'number' && Number.isFinite(minHotMatches)) {
159:     return Math.max(1, minHotMatches);
160:   }
161:   const normalized = typeof lang === 'string' ? lang.toLowerCase() : 'default';
162:   const value = minHotMatches?.[normalized] ?? minHotMatches?.default;
163:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.max(1, value) : 1;
164: }
````

## File: src/features/lexicon.js
````javascript
 1: // AI-lexicon loading per core/stylometry.md §16.
 2: // Loads the markdown lexicon at lexicon/ai-{lang}.md. Custom lexicons under
 3: // custom/lexicon/ take precedence when present.
 4: 
 5: import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
 6: import { resolve } from 'node:path';
 7: import { parseLexiconBody } from './lexicon-core.js';
 8: 
 9: export {
10:   DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD,
11:   DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES,
12:   parseLexiconBody,
13:   phraseToRegex,
14:   computeDensity,
15:   classifyLexiconHot,
16:   resolveMinHotMatches,
17: } from './lexicon-core.js';
18: 
19: export function loadLexicon(lang, repoRoot) {
20:   const candidates = [
21:     resolve(repoRoot, 'custom', 'lexicon', `ai-${lang}.md`),
22:     resolve(repoRoot, 'lexicon', `ai-${lang}.md`),
23:   ];
24:   for (const path of candidates) {
25:     if (existsSync(path)) {
26:       const raw = readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
27:       const body = raw.replace(/^---[\s\S]*?---\s*/, '');
28:       return { lang, path, ...parseLexiconBody(body) };
29:     }
30:   }
31:   return { lang, path: null, strict: [], phrases: [] };
32: }
````

## File: src/features/markup-leakage.js
````javascript
  1: // Deterministic detection of model-output *leakage* artifacts: tokens that LLM
  2: // web-search / tooling inject and that essentially never appear in human-written
  3: // prose. Unlike the stylometry/lexicon signals (which are probabilistic and
  4: // fire on clusters), a single hit here is near-proof-grade evidence of pasted
  5: // model output, so it fires hard at the document level. See issue #332.
  6: //
  7: // Language-agnostic literal token set — applies to ko/en/zh/ja alike.
  8: //
  9: // Self-scan caveat: patina's own docs, fixtures, and issues that *discuss* these
 10: // tokens will match. That is correct behavior (the text genuinely contains
 11: // them); callers scanning the repo's own meta-content should expect hits.
 12: 
 13: /**
 14:  * Score floor applied when deterministic markup-leakage is detected.
 15:  *
 16:  * Model-output leakage (issue #332) is near-proof-grade: a single token that
 17:  * LLM tooling injects and humans never type. Unlike the stylometric/lexical
 18:  * signals it is decisive on its own, so any hit short-circuits the deterministic
 19:  * `overall` into the 'heavily AI' band (>70) regardless of the per-paragraph
 20:  * hot ratio. It is a floor, not a hard 100, because the surrounding prose may
 21:  * still be genuinely human and we avoid claiming absolute proof.
 22:  *
 23:  * Lives here — the browser-pure module that owns leakage detection — so
 24:  * src/scoring.js re-exports the same constant
 25:  * (threshold parity gate: tests/unit/threshold-parity.test.js).
 26:  *
 27:  * @type {number}
 28:  */
 29: export const LEAKAGE_SCORE_FLOOR = 90;
 30: 
 31: const OBJECT_REPLACEMENT_CHAR = '￼';
 32: 
 33: // Each entry: { id, label, build() => fresh RegExp }. We build a fresh regex per
 34: // scan so the shared module is reentrant (no leaking lastIndex across calls).
 35: const MARKUP_RULES = [
 36:   {
 37:     id: 'oai-citation-markup',
 38:     label: 'OpenAI citation markup',
 39:     build: () => /:contentReference|oaicite|oai_citation/gi,
 40:   },
 41:   {
 42:     id: 'model-tool-token',
 43:     label: 'Model tool token',
 44:     build: () => /\bturn\d+(?:search|view|news|image|forecast|finance|fetch)\d*\b|\bgrok_card\b/gi,
 45:   },
 46:   {
 47:     id: 'object-replacement-char',
 48:     label: 'Object-replacement character (￼)',
 49:     build: () => new RegExp(OBJECT_REPLACEMENT_CHAR, 'g'),
 50:   },
 51:   {
 52:     id: 'ai-tracking-param',
 53:     label: 'AI-tool tracking parameter in URL',
 54:     build: () => /utm_source=(?:chatgpt\.com|openai\.com|perplexity\.ai|claude\.ai|gemini\.google\.com)|[?&](?:ref|utm_source)=chatgpt/gi,
 55:   },
 56:   {
 57:     id: 'explicit-self-identification',
 58:     label: 'Explicit AI self-identification',
 59:     // Near-proof-grade, so precision beats recall (issue #435): a single hit
 60:     // forces LEAKAGE_SCORE_FLOOR. Human bio/ML prose must not fire:
 61:     // - "I am an AI researcher" / "I'm an AI safety engineer": \b alone sits
 62:     //   between "AI" and the next noun, so the bare phrase needs a guard. The
 63:     //   "I am/I'm an AI" alternant (optionally with an AI-role noun:
 64:     //   assistant/chatbot/model) only matches when the phrase ends the clause
 65:     //   (punctuation/EOL) or continues with refusal-boilerplate provenance
 66:     //   ("created/trained/developed ... by"), and never before "-"/"/"
 67:     //   compounds like "AI-powered" or "AI/ML". Human job titles such as
 68:     //   "AI assistant manager" stay cold.
 69:     // - "BERT functions as a language model": the bare alternant now requires
 70:     //   the first-person continuation ("as a language model, I ...") that
 71:     //   real refusal boilerplate uses.
 72:     build: () => /\bas an? (?:AI|artificial intelligence) language model\b|\bas a large language model\b|\bas a language model,? I\b|\bas an AI assistant\b|\bI(?: am|'?m) an AI(?:\s+(?:assistant|chatbot|language model|model))?(?:\s+(?:created|developed|trained|designed|built|made)\s+by\b|(?![-/]|\s+\w))/gi,
 73:   },
 74:   {
 75:     // `navlist` alone is a plausible human identifier (CSS class/id, variable
 76:     // name) in frontend docs/code, so it is NOT near-proof on its own (#442).
 77:     // It only counts as leakage when an independent near-proof token in the
 78:     // same text corroborates that this is pasted model output.
 79:     id: 'model-nav-token',
 80:     label: 'Model navlist token',
 81:     corroborated: true,
 82:     build: () => /\bnavlist\b/gi,
 83:   },
 84: ];
 85: 
 86: /**
 87:  * Scan raw text for model-output leakage artifacts.
 88:  * @param {string} text
 89:  * @returns {{ leaked: boolean, hits: Array<{id:string,label:string,count:number,samples:string[]}> }}
 90:  */
 91: export function detectMarkupLeakage(text) {
 92:   const str = typeof text === 'string' ? text : '';
 93:   const hits = [];
 94:   if (!str) return { leaked: false, hits };
 95: 
 96:   const corroborated = [];
 97:   let hasIndependentHit = false;
 98:   for (const rule of MARKUP_RULES) {
 99:     const matches = str.match(rule.build());
100:     if (!matches || matches.length === 0) continue;
101:     const hit = {
102:       id: rule.id,
103:       label: rule.label,
104:       count: matches.length,
105:       samples: [...new Set(matches.map((m) => m.trim()).filter(Boolean))].slice(0, 3),
106:     };
107:     if (rule.corroborated) {
108:       corroborated.push(hit);
109:     } else {
110:       hits.push(hit);
111:       hasIndependentHit = true;
112:     }
113:   }
114:   // Corroboration-required tokens (navlist) only escalate to leakage when an
115:   // independent near-proof token also fired; otherwise they are dropped so a
116:   // human identifier cannot force the LEAKAGE_SCORE_FLOOR on its own (#442).
117:   if (hasIndependentHit) hits.push(...corroborated);
118:   return { leaked: hits.length > 0, hits };
119: }
120: 
121: export { MARKUP_RULES, OBJECT_REPLACEMENT_CHAR };
````

## File: src/features/meaning-proxy.js
````javascript
  1: // patina-lane: A asset for Lane B — deterministic meaning-floor proxy; LLM-free
  2: // by construction. See docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.
  3: //
  4: // This is NOT a semantic-similarity score (we cannot compute real MPS without a
  5: // model and must not pretend to). It is a conjunction of high-precision
  6: // invariants that ANY meaning-preserving rewrite must satisfy, each built only
  7: // from tokenize/normalization/counting primitives in `./index.js`. No imports
  8: // from backends/api/scoring — enforced by tests/unit/meaning-proxy.test.js.
  9: //
 10: // Phase A (v6.2): signals 2–4 (rare-token recall, negation delta, length ratio)
 11: // ship ADVISORY — surfaced in the JSON report only, never a CLI warning and
 12: // never an enforced exit. Only signal 1 (dropped numbers) is separately
 13: // enforced by the existing persona safety gate. Promotion of fail-severity to
 14: // enforcing happens later via the formal 2-round calibration (see ARCHITECTURE).
 15: import { tokenize } from './index.js';
 16: 
 17: const NUMBER_RE = /\d[\d.,]*/g;
 18: // Valid thousands grouping only (1,200 / 1,234,567 / 1,234.56). Non-standard
 19: // grouping like 1,2 or 3,14 is intentionally NOT stripped so it never collapses
 20: // onto 12 / 314 and masks a genuinely dropped number.
 21: const GROUPED_THOUSANDS_RE = /^\d{1,3}(,\d{3})+(\.\d+)?$/;
 22: 
 23: // Own, Lane-A-pure numeric extraction (mirrors verify.js#numbersIn so this module
 24: // imports nothing from Lane B). Normalizes grouping commas (1,200 === 1200).
 25: function numbersIn(text) {
 26:   const out = new Set();
 27:   for (const m of String(text ?? '').matchAll(NUMBER_RE)) {
 28:     const raw = m[0].replace(/\.+$/, '');
 29:     const normalized = GROUPED_THOUSANDS_RE.test(raw) ? raw.replace(/,/g, '') : raw;
 30:     if (normalized) out.add(normalized);
 31:   }
 32:   return out;
 33: }
 34: 
 35: /**
 36:  * Source numbers that vanish from the rewrite. Deterministic, LLM-free.
 37:  *
 38:  * @param {string} original
 39:  * @param {string} rewrite
 40:  * @returns {string[]}
 41:  */
 42: export function droppedNumbers(original, rewrite) {
 43:   const oNums = numbersIn(original);
 44:   const rNums = numbersIn(rewrite);
 45:   return [...oNums].filter((n) => !rNums.has(n));
 46: }
 47: 
 48: const CJK_RE = /[\p{Script=Han}\p{Script=Hiragana}\p{Script=Katakana}]/u;
 49: 
 50: // Content tokens for rare-token recall: NFC + lowercased, keeping latin tokens of
 51: // length >= 2 and any CJK token. freq<=2 filtering already excludes high-frequency
 52: // function words, so no separate stopword list is needed.
 53: function contentTokens(text, lang) {
 54:   return tokenize(String(text ?? ''), { lang })
 55:     .map((t) => t.normalize('NFC').toLowerCase())
 56:     .filter((t) => t.length >= 2 || CJK_RE.test(t));
 57: }
 58: 
 59: /**
 60:  * Fraction of the original's low-frequency (freq<=2) content tokens that survive
 61:  * in the rewrite, matched by exact token OR normalized substring containment.
 62:  * Rare content tokens approximate the load-bearing nouns/entities/terms of a
 63:  * claim; a humanizer rephrases connectives, not terminology. INACTIVE below 3
 64:  * rare tokens (N=1/2 volatility would false-alarm).
 65:  *
 66:  * @returns {{active: boolean, recall: number|null, rareCount: number, survived: number|null}}
 67:  */
 68: export function rareTokenRecall(original, rewrite, lang = 'ko') {
 69:   const freq = new Map();
 70:   for (const t of contentTokens(original, lang)) freq.set(t, (freq.get(t) ?? 0) + 1);
 71:   const rare = [...freq.entries()].filter(([, c]) => c <= 2).map(([t]) => t);
 72:   if (rare.length < 3) return { active: false, recall: null, rareCount: rare.length, survived: null };
 73:   const rSet = new Set(contentTokens(rewrite, lang));
 74:   const rJoined = String(rewrite ?? '').normalize('NFC').toLowerCase();
 75:   let survived = 0;
 76:   for (const t of rare) {
 77:     // Substring fallback only for CJK or long (>=5) Latin tokens. Short Latin
 78:     // rare tokens (us / art / ai / go) would false-survive inside business /
 79:     // chair / ongoing, masking a dropped entity/term.
 80:     const allowSubstring = CJK_RE.test(t) || t.length >= 5;
 81:     if (rSet.has(t) || (allowSubstring && rJoined.includes(t))) survived += 1;
 82:   }
 83:   return { active: true, recall: survived / rare.length, rareCount: rare.length, survived };
 84: }
 85: 
 86: const EN_NEGATIONS = new Set([
 87:   'no', 'not', 'never', 'none', 'neither', 'nor', 'cannot', 'without', 'nobody', 'nothing', 'nowhere',
 88: ]);
 89: // ja/zh negation markers are morphemes with no whitespace boundary, so they are
 90: // matched as documented COARSE substrings (advisory-only; can over-count inside
 91: // compounds like 不错 / 案内). en/ko are matched on word/token boundaries per the
 92: // review (avoids substring false hits like "cannot"/"notable"/안전/안내).
 93: const JA_NEG_RE = /ない|ません|ではない|なかった|ぬ(?=[。、！？\s]|$)|ず(?=[。、！？\s]|$)/g;
 94: const ZH_NEG_SET = new Set(['不', '没', '無', '无', '非', '未', '別', '别']);
 95: const KO_NEG_WORD = /(^(안|못)$)|않|없|아니/u;
 96: 
 97: /**
 98:  * Count negation markers on token/word boundaries per language. A large delta
 99:  * between original and rewrite flags a possible silent polarity inversion.
100:  *
101:  * @returns {number}
102:  */
103: export function countNegations(text, lang = 'ko') {
104:   const t = String(text ?? '');
105:   if (lang === 'ja') return (t.match(JA_NEG_RE) ?? []).length;
106:   if (lang === 'zh') return tokenize(t, { lang: 'zh' }).filter((tok) => ZH_NEG_SET.has(tok)).length;
107:   if (lang === 'ko') {
108:     return tokenize(t, { lang: 'ko' }).filter((w) => KO_NEG_WORD.test(w)).length;
109:   }
110:   // en/default: whole-word match + n't contractions.
111:   const words = t.toLowerCase().match(/[a-z]+(?:'[a-z]+)?/g) ?? [];
112:   return words.filter((w) => EN_NEGATIONS.has(w) || w.endsWith("n't")).length;
113: }
114: 
115: /**
116:  * Deterministic, LLM-free meaning-floor proxy. Returns a severity plus per-signal
117:  * measurements and human-readable reasons. Never throws.
118:  *
119:  * @param {object} input
120:  * @param {string} input.original
121:  * @param {string} input.rewrite
122:  * @param {string} [input.lang]
123:  * @returns {{ok: boolean, severity: 'pass'|'warn'|'fail', signals: object, reasons: string[]}}
124:  */
125: export function evaluateMeaningProxy({ original, rewrite, lang = 'ko' } = {}) {
126:   const reasons = [];
127:   const order = { pass: 0, warn: 1, fail: 2 };
128:   let severity = 'pass';
129:   const bump = (s) => { if (order[s] > order[severity]) severity = s; };
130: 
131:   // Signal 1 — numeric preservation (informational here; the persona safety gate
132:   // enforces dropped numbers separately).
133:   const dropped = droppedNumbers(original, rewrite);
134:   if (dropped.length > 0) {
135:     reasons.push(`dropped ${dropped.length} source number(s): ${dropped.slice(0, 6).join(', ')}${dropped.length > 6 ? '…' : ''}`);
136:     bump('warn');
137:   }
138: 
139:   // Signal 2 — rare-content-token recall (active only with >=3 rare tokens).
140:   const rare = rareTokenRecall(original, rewrite, lang);
141:   if (rare.active) {
142:     if (rare.recall < 0.3) {
143:       reasons.push(`rare-content-token recall ${(rare.recall * 100).toFixed(0)}% (${rare.survived}/${rare.rareCount}) — likely dropped terms/entities`);
144:       bump('fail');
145:     } else if (rare.recall < 0.5) {
146:       reasons.push(`low rare-content-token recall ${(rare.recall * 100).toFixed(0)}% (${rare.survived}/${rare.rareCount})`);
147:       bump('warn');
148:     }
149:   }
150: 
151:   // Signal 3 — negation-polarity delta (coarse; advisory).
152:   const negOriginal = countNegations(original, lang);
153:   const negRewrite = countNegations(rewrite, lang);
154:   const negationDelta = Math.abs(negOriginal - negRewrite);
155:   if (negationDelta >= 2) {
156:     reasons.push(`negation-marker count changed ${negOriginal}→${negRewrite} — possible polarity inversion`);
157:     bump('fail');
158:   } else if (negationDelta === 1) {
159:     reasons.push(`negation-marker count changed ${negOriginal}→${negRewrite}`);
160:     bump('warn');
161:   }
162: 
163:   // Signal 4 — length-ratio EXTREME bounds (truncation / hallucinated expansion).
164:   const oTokens = tokenize(String(original ?? ''), { lang }).length;
165:   const rTokens = tokenize(String(rewrite ?? ''), { lang }).length;
166:   // Empty original + non-empty rewrite is closer to hallucinated expansion than a
167:   // meaning-preserving rewrite, so it must fail the extreme-bounds check rather
168:   // than record a benign ratio of 1.
169:   const lengthRatio = oTokens === 0 ? (rTokens === 0 ? 1 : Infinity) : rTokens / oTokens;
170:   if (lengthRatio < 0.4 || lengthRatio > 2.5) {
171:     reasons.push(`length ratio ${lengthRatio.toFixed(2)} outside [0.4, 2.5] — truncation or expansion`);
172:     bump('fail');
173:   }
174: 
175:   return {
176:     ok: severity !== 'fail',
177:     severity,
178:     signals: {
179:       droppedNumbers: dropped,
180:       rareTokenRecall: rare.active ? Number(rare.recall.toFixed(3)) : null,
181:       rareTokenActive: rare.active,
182:       rareTokenCount: rare.rareCount,
183:       negationDelta,
184:       negationOriginal: negOriginal,
185:       negationRewrite: negRewrite,
186:       lengthRatio: Number(lengthRatio.toFixed(3)),
187:     },
188:     reasons,
189:   };
190: }
````

## File: src/features/persona-match.js
````javascript
  1: // patina-lane: A asset for Lane B — deterministic persona-match scorer; LLM-free
  2: // by construction, consumed by the Lane B persona gate. See docs/ARCHITECTURE.md.
  3: import {
  4:   analyzeText,
  5:   splitProseSentences,
  6:   tokenize,
  7:   commaDensity,
  8:   koreanPosDiversityProxy,
  9:   koreanPostEditeseFeatures,
 10:   detectKoreanRegister,
 11: } from './index.js';
 12: import { editChurn } from '../personas/gates.js';
 13: 
 14: function clamp(value, min, max) {
 15:   return Math.max(min, Math.min(max, value));
 16: }
 17: 
 18: function mean(values) {
 19:   const nums = values.filter((value) => typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value));
 20:   return nums.length > 0 ? nums.reduce((sum, value) => sum + value, 0) / nums.length : null;
 21: }
 22: 
 23: function countPhrase(text, phrase) {
 24:   if (!phrase) return 0;
 25:   let count = 0;
 26:   let index = 0;
 27:   while (index <= text.length) {
 28:     const found = text.indexOf(phrase, index);
 29:     if (found === -1) break;
 30:     count += 1;
 31:     index = found + Math.max(phrase.length, 1);
 32:   }
 33:   return count;
 34: }
 35: 
 36: function lexiconDensity(text, phrases, tokenCount) {
 37:   if (!Array.isArray(phrases) || phrases.length === 0 || tokenCount === 0) return 0;
 38:   const normalized = String(text ?? '').normalize('NFC');
 39:   const matches = phrases.reduce((sum, phrase) => sum + countPhrase(normalized, String(phrase).normalize('NFC')), 0);
 40:   return (matches / tokenCount) * 1000;
 41: }
 42: 
 43: function openerDiversity(sentences) {
 44:   if (sentences.length === 0) return 0;
 45:   const openers = sentences
 46:     .map((sentence) => String(sentence).trim().split(/\s+/u)[0])
 47:     .filter(Boolean);
 48:   return openers.length > 0 ? new Set(openers).size / openers.length : 0;
 49: }
 50: 
 51: function localRegisterRatios(sentences) {
 52:   const counts = { plain: 0, polite: 0 };
 53:   for (const sentence of sentences) {
 54:     const final = String(sentence).trim().replace(/[.!?…"'」』)\]]+$/g, '').split(/\s+/u).at(-1) ?? '';
 55:     if (/[요죠]$/.test(final) || /(?:니다|니까)$/.test(final)) counts.polite += 1;
 56:     else if (/다$/.test(final)) counts.plain += 1;
 57:   }
 58:   const total = counts.plain + counts.polite;
 59:   return {
 60:     plain: total > 0 ? counts.plain / total : 0,
 61:     polite: total > 0 ? counts.polite / total : 0,
 62:   };
 63: }
 64: 
 65: function registerRatios(text, sentences) {
 66:   const detected = detectKoreanRegister(text);
 67:   if (detected?.shares) {
 68:     return {
 69:       plain: detected.shares.plain ?? 0,
 70:       polite: (detected.shares.polite ?? 0) + (detected.shares.formal ?? 0),
 71:     };
 72:   }
 73:   return localRegisterRatios(sentences);
 74: }
 75: 
 76: function featureValue(vector, key) {
 77:   const value = vector[key];
 78:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : 0;
 79: }
 80: 
 81: function cosineWeighted(vector, targets) {
 82:   let dot = 0;
 83:   let magX = 0;
 84:   let magT = 0;
 85:   for (const [name, spec] of Object.entries(targets)) {
 86:     if (name === 'overEditChurn' || spec?.target == null) continue;
 87:     const weight = spec.weight ?? 0;
 88:     if (weight <= 0) continue;
 89:     const wx = featureValue(vector, name) * weight;
 90:     const wt = spec.target * weight;
 91:     dot += wx * wt;
 92:     magX += wx * wx;
 93:     magT += wt * wt;
 94:   }
 95:   if (magX === 0 || magT === 0) return 0;
 96:   return dot / (Math.sqrt(magX) * Math.sqrt(magT));
 97: }
 98: 
 99: /**
100:  * Extract deterministic document-level persona-match features.
101:  *
102:  * @param {string} text Text to analyze.
103:  * @param {object} [options] Extraction options.
104:  * @returns {object} Numeric feature vector.
105:  */
106: export function extractPersonaFeatureVector(text, { lang = 'ko', repoRoot, persona } = {}) {
107:   const normalized = String(text ?? '').normalize('NFC');
108:   const analysis = analyzeText(normalized, { lang, repoRoot });
109:   const paragraphs = analysis.paragraphs ?? [];
110:   const sentences = splitProseSentences(normalized);
111:   const allTokens = tokenize(normalized, { lang });
112:   const sentenceCount = sentences.length;
113:   const comma = commaDensity(normalized, sentenceCount);
114:   const pos = koreanPosDiversityProxy(normalized);
115:   const postEditese = koreanPostEditeseFeatures(normalized, { lang });
116:   const register = registerRatios(normalized, sentences);
117:   const words = persona?.blocks?.preferredWords ?? {};
118:   const tokenCount = Math.max(allTokens.length, 1);
119: 
120:   return {
121:     burstiness_cv: mean(paragraphs.map((paragraph) => paragraph?.burstiness?.cv)),
122:     mattr: mean(paragraphs.map((paragraph) => paragraph?.mattr?.value)),
123:     sentence_opener_diversity: openerDiversity(sentences),
124:     comma_per_sentence: comma.perSentence ?? 0,
125:     suffix_class_diversity: postEditese?.rhythm?.suffixClassDiversity ?? pos.classDiversity ?? 0,
126:     ko_register_plain_ratio: register.plain,
127:     ko_register_polite_ratio: register.polite,
128:     lexicon_density_preferred: lexiconDensity(normalized, words.allow, tokenCount),
129:     lexicon_density_avoid: lexiconDensity(normalized, words.avoid, tokenCount),
130:   };
131: }
132: 
133: /**
134:  * Compute LLM-free persona-match score against normalized persona targets.
135:  *
136:  * @param {object} input Score inputs.
137:  * @returns {{score: number, featureVector: object, deltas: object, avoidDensityPenalty: number, overEditChurn: number|null}}
138:  */
139: export function personaMatchScore({ text, persona, lang = 'ko', repoRoot, original = null }) {
140:   const featureVector = extractPersonaFeatureVector(text, { lang, repoRoot, persona });
141:   const targets = persona?.targetFeatures ?? {};
142:   const deltas = {};
143:   let weightedDistance = 0;
144:   let weightSum = 0;
145: 
146:   for (const [name, spec] of Object.entries(targets)) {
147:     if (name === 'overEditChurn') continue;
148:     if (spec?.target == null || spec?.tolerance == null || spec.tolerance <= 0 || (spec.weight ?? 0) <= 0) continue;
149:     const x = featureValue(featureVector, name);
150:     const z = clamp((x - spec.target) / spec.tolerance, -3, 3);
151:     deltas[name] = { value: x, target: spec.target, z };
152:     weightedDistance += spec.weight * z * z;
153:     weightSum += spec.weight;
154:   }
155: 
156:   const dEuclid = weightSum > 0 ? Math.sqrt(weightedDistance / weightSum) : 0;
157:   const cos = cosineWeighted(featureVector, targets);
158:   const base = (100 * (1 - Math.min(dEuclid, 3) / 3) * 0.8) + (100 * Math.max(cos, 0) * 0.2);
159:   const avoidTarget = targets.lexicon_density_avoid?.target ?? 0;
160:   const avoidTolerance = targets.lexicon_density_avoid?.tolerance ?? 1;
161:   const avoidExcess = Math.max(0, featureVector.lexicon_density_avoid - avoidTarget - avoidTolerance);
162:   const avoidDensityPenalty = clamp((avoidExcess / Math.max(avoidTolerance, 1)) * 5, 0, 20);
163:   const overEditChurn = original == null ? null : editChurn(original, text);
164:   const churnMax = targets.overEditChurn?.max ?? null;
165:   const overEditPenalty = overEditChurn != null && churnMax != null && overEditChurn > churnMax
166:     ? clamp(((overEditChurn - churnMax) / Math.max(churnMax, 0.01)) * 20, 0, 20)
167:     : 0;
168:   const score = clamp(base - avoidDensityPenalty - overEditPenalty, 0, 100);
169: 
170:   return {
171:     score,
172:     featureVector,
173:     deltas,
174:     avoidDensityPenalty,
175:     overEditChurn,
176:   };
177: }
````

## File: src/features/segment.js
````javascript
  1: // Paragraph / sentence / token splitting per core/stylometry.md §2 §3.
  2: //
  3: // Tokenization is intentionally simple: whitespace split + edge-punctuation
  4: // strip, no morphological analysis. For Chinese/Japanese prose, where normal
  5: // text often has no whitespace, use a deterministic character-token fallback
  6: // so sentence-length and lexical-diversity signals are not collapsed to
  7: // "one token per sentence." Sentence splitting is regex-only and accepts known
  8: // false splits on abbreviations / decimals (documented limit).
  9: //
 10: // A CJK sentence terminator (。！？) is treated as a boundary only when it is
 11: // NOT immediately followed by a closing quote/bracket, so quote-internal
 12: // terminators (彼は「やめろ。」と言った。) do not split mid-quote and a trailing
 13: // closer (…と言った。」) is never stranded as its own zero-token "sentence".
 14: // U+2026 (…) is intentionally NOT a hard terminator: in en/ko it is usually
 15: // intra-sentence ("Well… maybe not."), so splitting on it would perturb token
 16: // counts on exactly the human prose the tool must not flag. It is still
 17: // stripped from sentence ends below.
 18: const SENTENCE_CLOSERS = '」』】〕）》〉｝"\'”’';
 19: const SENTENCE_SPLIT_RE = new RegExp(
 20:   `[.!?]+\\s+|(?<=[。！？])(?![${SENTENCE_CLOSERS}])|\\n+`,
 21:   'u'
 22: );
 23: const PARAGRAPH_SPLIT_RE = /\n\s*\n/;
 24: const LIST_LINE_RE = /^\s*(?:[-*+]\s+|\d+[.)]\s+)/u;
 25: // \W in Unicode-aware mode. Strips edge punctuation but keeps internal
 26: // hyphens / apostrophes (e.g. "don't", "좋은-도구") as a single token.
 27: const EDGE_PUNCT_RE = /^[^\p{L}\p{N}]+|[^\p{L}\p{N}]+$/gu;
 28: const CJK_TOKEN_RE = /[\p{Script=Han}\p{Script=Hiragana}\p{Script=Katakana}\u30FC]|[A-Za-z0-9]+/gu;
 29: 
 30: export function splitParagraphs(text) {
 31:   if (!text) return [];
 32:   return text
 33:     .split(PARAGRAPH_SPLIT_RE)
 34:     .map((p) => p.trim())
 35:     .filter((p) => p.length > 0);
 36: }
 37: 
 38: function stripListBlocks(paragraph) {
 39:   const lines = String(paragraph ?? '').split(/\r?\n/);
 40:   const proseLines = [];
 41:   let colonListRemaining = 0;
 42:   for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
 43:     const rawLine = lines[i];
 44:     const trimmed = rawLine.trim();
 45:     if (trimmed === '') {
 46:       colonListRemaining = 0;
 47:       proseLines.push(rawLine);
 48:       continue;
 49:     }
 50:     if (LIST_LINE_RE.test(rawLine)) continue;
 51:     if (colonListRemaining > 0) {
 52:       colonListRemaining--;
 53:       continue;
 54:     }
 55:     if (trimmed.endsWith(':')) {
 56:       colonListRemaining = countFollowingPlainListLines(lines, i + 1);
 57:     }
 58:     proseLines.push(rawLine);
 59:   }
 60:   return proseLines.join('\n');
 61: }
 62: 
 63: function countFollowingPlainListLines(lines, start) {
 64:   let count = 0;
 65:   for (let i = start; i < lines.length; i++) {
 66:     const trimmed = lines[i].trim();
 67:     if (trimmed === '') break;
 68:     if (LIST_LINE_RE.test(lines[i])) continue;
 69:     count++;
 70:   }
 71:   return count >= 2 ? count : 0;
 72: }
 73: 
 74: export function splitSentences(paragraph) {
 75:   if (!paragraph) return [];
 76:   return paragraph
 77:     .split(SENTENCE_SPLIT_RE)
 78:     .map((s) => s.trim().replace(/[.!?。！？…]+$/u, ''))
 79:     .filter((s) => s.length > 0);
 80: }
 81: 
 82: export function splitProseSentences(paragraph) {
 83:   return splitSentences(stripListBlocks(paragraph));
 84: }
 85: 
 86: function tokenizeCjk(text) {
 87:   const tokens = [];
 88:   for (const match of text.matchAll(CJK_TOKEN_RE)) {
 89:     const token = match[0].replace(EDGE_PUNCT_RE, '');
 90:     if (token) tokens.push(token);
 91:   }
 92:   return tokens;
 93: }
 94: 
 95: export function tokenize(text, opts = {}) {
 96:   if (!text) return [];
 97:   if (opts.lang === 'zh' || opts.lang === 'ja') return tokenizeCjk(text);
 98:   return text
 99:     .split(/\s+/)
100:     .map((chunk) => chunk.replace(EDGE_PUNCT_RE, ''))
101:     .filter((t) => t.length > 0);
102: }
````

## File: src/features/signal-strength.js
````javascript
  1: import { FAKE_CANDOR_MIN, THEMATIC_BREAK_MIN } from './discourse-tells.js';
  2: import { DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD } from './lexicon-core.js';
  3: import { DEFAULT_BURSTINESS_BANDS, DEFAULT_MATTR_BANDS } from './stylometry.js';
  4: 
  5: /**
  6:  * Average the strongest deterministic signal for each paragraph.
  7:  *
  8:  * This is diagnostic-only. It intentionally does not replace the existing
  9:  * hot-paragraph ratio used by gates and reconciliation.
 10:  *
 11:  * @param {object[]} [paragraphs] Analyzer paragraph payloads.
 12:  * @param {object} [options] Thresholds used by the analyzer.
 13:  * @returns {number} 0..100 average signal strength.
 14:  */
 15: export function summarizeSignalStrength(paragraphs = [], options = {}) {
 16:   if (!Array.isArray(paragraphs) || paragraphs.length === 0) return 0;
 17:   const total = paragraphs.reduce(
 18:     (sum, paragraph) => sum + paragraphSignalStrength(paragraph, options),
 19:     0
 20:   );
 21:   return total / paragraphs.length;
 22: }
 23: 
 24: /**
 25:  * Score how deep a paragraph is inside its strongest deterministic trigger.
 26:  *
 27:  * @param {object} [paragraph] Analyzer paragraph payload.
 28:  * @param {object} [options] Thresholds used by the analyzer.
 29:  * @returns {number} 0..100 paragraph signal strength.
 30:  */
 31: export function paragraphSignalStrength(paragraph = {}, options = {}) {
 32:   const burstiness = lowBandStrength(
 33:     paragraph.burstiness?.cv,
 34:     resolveLowThreshold(options.burstinessBands, DEFAULT_BURSTINESS_BANDS.low),
 35:     paragraph.burstiness?.band
 36:   );
 37:   const mattr = lowBandStrength(
 38:     paragraph.mattr?.value,
 39:     resolveLowThreshold(options.mattrBands, DEFAULT_MATTR_BANDS.low),
 40:     paragraph.mattr?.band
 41:   );
 42:   const lexicon = highThresholdStrength(
 43:     paragraph.lexicon?.density,
 44:     resolveThreshold(
 45:       options.lexiconDensityThreshold,
 46:       DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD
 47:     ),
 48:     paragraph.lexicon?.hot
 49:   );
 50:   const koDiagnostics =
 51:     paragraph.koDiagnostics?.hot &&
 52:     typeof paragraph.koDiagnostics?.strength === 'number' &&
 53:     Number.isFinite(paragraph.koDiagnostics.strength)
 54:       ? paragraph.koDiagnostics.strength
 55:       : 0;
 56:   // Discourse-tell attribution (#391): candorHot/thematicBreakHot make a
 57:   // paragraph hot, so they must carry strength here too — otherwise a
 58:   // discourse-only hot paragraph would rank at 0, contradicting the hot
 59:   // verdict. Strength is the paragraph's tell count normalized by the
 60:   // document density gate (FAKE_CANDOR_MIN / THEMATIC_BREAK_MIN): a paragraph
 61:   // carrying enough tells to clear the gate alone scores 100.
 62:   const discourse = Math.max(
 63:     discourseTellStrength(paragraph.candorHot, paragraph.candorCount, FAKE_CANDOR_MIN),
 64:     discourseTellStrength(
 65:       paragraph.thematicBreakHot,
 66:       paragraph.thematicBreakCount,
 67:       THEMATIC_BREAK_MIN
 68:     )
 69:   );
 70:   return Math.max(burstiness, mattr, lexicon, koDiagnostics, discourse);
 71: }
 72: 
 73: function discourseTellStrength(isHot, count, docThreshold) {
 74:   if (!isHot) return 0;
 75:   if (!docThreshold || docThreshold <= 0) return 0;
 76:   // Hot implies >= 1 carried tell; floor the count so older payloads without
 77:   // counts still register a nonzero strength.
 78:   const carried = Math.max(
 79:     typeof count === 'number' && Number.isFinite(count) ? count : 0,
 80:     1
 81:   );
 82:   return clampPercent((carried / docThreshold) * 100);
 83: }
 84: 
 85: function resolveLowThreshold(bands, fallback) {
 86:   return resolveThreshold(bands?.low, fallback);
 87: }
 88: 
 89: function resolveThreshold(value, fallback) {
 90:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : fallback;
 91: }
 92: 
 93: function lowBandStrength(value, threshold, band) {
 94:   if (band !== 'low' || typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) return 0;
 95:   if (!threshold || threshold <= 0) return 0;
 96:   return clampPercent((1 - value / threshold) * 100);
 97: }
 98: 
 99: function highThresholdStrength(value, threshold, isHot) {
100:   if (!isHot || typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) return 0;
101:   if (!threshold || threshold <= 0) return 0;
102:   return clampPercent(((value - threshold) / threshold) * 100);
103: }
104: 
105: function clampPercent(value) {
106:   if (!Number.isFinite(value)) return 0;
107:   return Math.max(0, Math.min(100, value));
108: }
````

````javascript
  1: // Small, dependency-free logistic classifier helpers for optional structural
  2: // stylometry models. The public package ships the code path, not trained private
  3: // weights; callers must provide a model explicitly.
  4: import { extractStructuralFeatures, STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES } from './structural-features.js';
  5: 
  6: export const DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD = 0.5;
  7: 
  8: function sigmoid(value) {
  9:   return 1 / (1 + Math.exp(-value));
 10: }
 11: 
 12: function assertMatrix(X) {
 13:   if (!Array.isArray(X) || X.length === 0 || !Array.isArray(X[0]) || X[0].length === 0) {
 14:     throw new TypeError('training features must be a non-empty matrix');
 15:   }
 16:   const width = X[0].length;
 17:   for (const row of X) {
 18:     if (!Array.isArray(row) || row.length !== width || row.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value))) {
 19:       throw new TypeError('training features must be rectangular finite-number rows');
 20:     }
 21:   }
 22: }
 23: 
 24: function assertLabels(y, expectedLength) {
 25:   if (!Array.isArray(y) || y.length !== expectedLength || y.some((value) => value !== 0 && value !== 1)) {
 26:     throw new TypeError('training labels must be 0/1 values aligned to features');
 27:   }
 28: }
 29: 
 30: export function fitScaler(X) {
 31:   assertMatrix(X);
 32:   const width = X[0].length;
 33:   const mu = new Array(width).fill(0);
 34:   const sigma = new Array(width).fill(0);
 35: 
 36:   for (const row of X) {
 37:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) mu[j] += row[j];
 38:   }
 39:   for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) mu[j] /= X.length;
 40: 
 41:   for (const row of X) {
 42:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) sigma[j] += (row[j] - mu[j]) ** 2;
 43:   }
 44:   for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) sigma[j] = Math.sqrt(sigma[j] / X.length) || 1;
 45: 
 46:   return { mu, sigma };
 47: }
 48: 
 49: export function applyScaler({ mu, sigma }, row) {
 50:   if (!Array.isArray(mu) || !Array.isArray(sigma) || !Array.isArray(row)) {
 51:     throw new TypeError('scaler and row must be arrays');
 52:   }
 53:   if (mu.length !== sigma.length || row.length !== mu.length) {
 54:     throw new TypeError('scaler dimensions must match row dimensions');
 55:   }
 56:   return row.map((value, index) => (value - mu[index]) / sigma[index]);
 57: }
 58: 
 59: export function trainLogReg(X, y, { lr = 0.1, epochs = 2000, l2 = 0.01 } = {}) {
 60:   assertMatrix(X);
 61:   assertLabels(y, X.length);
 62:   if (!Number.isFinite(lr) || lr <= 0) throw new TypeError('lr must be a positive finite number');
 63:   if (!Number.isInteger(epochs) || epochs <= 0) throw new TypeError('epochs must be a positive integer');
 64:   if (!Number.isFinite(l2) || l2 < 0) throw new TypeError('l2 must be a non-negative finite number');
 65: 
 66:   const scaler = fitScaler(X);
 67:   const Z = X.map((row) => applyScaler(scaler, row));
 68:   const width = Z[0].length;
 69:   const w = new Array(width).fill(0);
 70:   let b = 0;
 71: 
 72:   for (let epoch = 0; epoch < epochs; epoch++) {
 73:     const gradientW = new Array(width).fill(0);
 74:     let gradientB = 0;
 75: 
 76:     for (let i = 0; i < Z.length; i++) {
 77:       const probability = sigmoid(Z[i].reduce((sum, value, index) => sum + value * w[index], b));
 78:       const error = probability - y[i];
 79:       for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) gradientW[j] += error * Z[i][j];
 80:       gradientB += error;
 81:     }
 82: 
 83:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) w[j] -= lr * (gradientW[j] / Z.length + l2 * w[j]);
 84:     b -= lr * (gradientB / Z.length);
 85:   }
 86: 
 87:   // Only stamp this patina version's feature names onto models actually
 88:   // trained at that width; a toy/experimental model must not masquerade as a
 89:   // STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES-compatible model (normalizeStructuralModel
 90:   // rejects non-matching widths at load time, issue #436).
 91:   return width === STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length
 92:     ? { weights: w, bias: b, scaler, featureNames: STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES }
 93:     : { weights: w, bias: b, scaler };
 94: }
 95: 
 96: export function normalizeStructuralModel(model) {
 97:   if (!model || typeof model !== 'object') return null;
 98:   const weights = model.weights ?? model.w;
 99:   const bias = model.bias ?? model.b;
100:   const scaler = model.scaler;
101:   const threshold = model.threshold ?? DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD;
102: 
103:   if (!Array.isArray(weights) || !Number.isFinite(bias) || !scaler || !Number.isFinite(threshold)) {
104:     throw new TypeError('structural model requires weights, bias, scaler, and optional threshold');
105:   }
106:   if (!Array.isArray(scaler.mu) || !Array.isArray(scaler.sigma)) {
107:     throw new TypeError('structural model scaler requires mu and sigma arrays');
108:   }
109:   if (weights.length !== scaler.mu.length || weights.length !== scaler.sigma.length) {
110:     throw new TypeError('structural model dimensions must match scaler dimensions');
111:   }
112:   // Dimension must match THIS patina version's feature extractor, even when
113:   // the optional featureNames field is absent (issue #436). Otherwise a
114:   // self-consistent model trained against an older feature set loads cleanly
115:   // and only explodes at predict time, deep inside analyzeText — past the
116:   // call sites that handle load errors (audit aborts untyped; scoring.js
117:   // silently zeroes the whole deterministic shadow score, including the
118:   // markup-leakage floor).
119:   if (weights.length !== STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length) {
120:     throw new TypeError(
121:       `structural model expects ${weights.length} features but this patina version extracts ${STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length} (${STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.join(', ')}); retrain the model against the current feature set`,
122:     );
123:   }
124:   if (weights.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value)) || scaler.mu.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value)) || scaler.sigma.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value))) {
125:     throw new TypeError('structural model values must be finite numbers');
126:   }
127:   // Sigma must be strictly positive: a hand-written or truncated model with a
128:   // zero/negative sigma passes the finite check but makes applyScaler emit
129:   // NaN/±Infinity, which sigmoid turns into a silent NaN verdict (#443).
130:   if (scaler.sigma.some((value) => !(value > 0))) {
131:     throw new TypeError('structural model scaler sigma values must be positive');
132:   }
133:   if (Array.isArray(model.featureNames) && model.featureNames.join('\0') !== STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.join('\0')) {
134:     throw new TypeError('structural model featureNames do not match this patina version');
135:   }
136: 
137:   return {
138:     ...model,
139:     weights,
140:     bias,
141:     scaler,
142:     threshold,
143:     featureNames: STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES,
144:   };
145: }
146: 
147: export function predictStructuralScore(model, row) {
148:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
149:   if (!normalized) throw new TypeError('structural model is required');
150:   const scaled = applyScaler(normalized.scaler, row);
151:   const score = sigmoid(scaled.reduce((sum, value, index) => sum + value * normalized.weights[index], normalized.bias));
152:   return score;
153: }
154: 
155: export function thresholdForMaxFpr(model, Xtrain, ytrain, maxFpr = 0.1) {
156:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
157:   assertMatrix(Xtrain);
158:   assertLabels(ytrain, Xtrain.length);
159:   if (!Number.isFinite(maxFpr) || maxFpr < 0 || maxFpr > 1) {
160:     throw new TypeError('maxFpr must be a finite number between 0 and 1');
161:   }
162:   const negativeScores = Xtrain
163:     .filter((_, index) => ytrain[index] === 0)
164:     .map((row) => predictStructuralScore(normalized, row))
165:     .sort((a, b) => a - b);
166:   if (negativeScores.length === 0) return DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD;
167:   // ceil (not floor) so the realized FPR never exceeds maxFpr: floor would keep
168:   // an extra negative above the threshold (#443).
169:   const index = Math.min(negativeScores.length - 1, Math.ceil((1 - maxFpr) * negativeScores.length));
170:   return Math.max(DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD, negativeScores[index]);
171: }
172: 
173: export function structuralModelVerdict(text, { lang = 'ko', model = null } = {}) {
174:   if (!model) return { available: false, hot: null, score: null };
175:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
176:   // A truthy non-object model normalizes to null; treat as unavailable rather
177:   // than null-derefing normalized.lang (#443).
178:   if (!normalized || (normalized.lang && normalized.lang !== lang)) {
179:     return { available: false, hot: null, score: null };
180:   }
181:   const row = extractStructuralFeatures(text, { lang });
182:   const score = predictStructuralScore(normalized, row);
183:   return {
184:     available: true,
185:     hot: score >= normalized.threshold,
186:     score: Number(score.toFixed(4)),
187:   };
188: }
````
