 25: 
 26: const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
 27: const REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '../..');
 28: const DEFAULT_FIXTURE_DIR = resolve(REPO_ROOT, 'tests/fixtures/live-quality');
 29: const LEGACY_FIXTURE_PATH = resolve(REPO_ROOT, 'tests/quality/live-fixtures.jsonl');
 30: 
 31: export const LIVE_QUALITY_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1;
 32: export const DEFAULT_POLICY = Object.freeze({
 33:   aiAfterCeiling: 30,
 34:   mpsFloor: 70,
 35:   fidelityFloor: 70,
 36:   requireAiImprovement: true,
 37: });
 38: 
 39: const REQUIRED_FIXTURE_FIELDS = ['fixture_id', 'language', 'redistribution', 'text'];
 40: 
 41: export function loadLiveFixtures(source = DEFAULT_FIXTURE_DIR) {
 42:   const resolved = resolve(source);
 43:   if (existsSync(resolved) && statSync(resolved).isDirectory()) {
 44:     return loadMarkdownFixtureDir(resolved);
 45:   }
 46:   if (existsSync(resolved)) {
 47:     return loadJsonlFixtures(resolved);
 48:   }
 49:   return loadJsonlFixtures(LEGACY_FIXTURE_PATH);
 50: }
 51: 
 52: function loadJsonlFixtures(fixturePath) {
 53:   const body = readFileSync(fixturePath, 'utf8');
 54:   return body
 55:     .split('\n')
 56:     .map((line) => line.trim())
 57:     .filter(Boolean)
 58:     .map((line, index) => validateFixture(JSON.parse(line), `${fixturePath}:${index + 1}`));
 59: }
 60: 
 61: function loadMarkdownFixtureDir(root) {
 62:   const paths = collectMarkdownFiles(root);
 63:   if (paths.length === 0) throw new Error(`no live-quality markdown fixtures found in ${root}`);
 64:   return paths.map((path) => parseMarkdownFixture(path));
 65: }
 66: 
 67: function collectMarkdownFiles(root) {
 68:   const entries = readdirSync(root, { withFileTypes: true });
 69:   const paths = [];
 70:   for (const entry of entries) {
 71:     const path = join(root, entry.name);
 72:     if (entry.isDirectory()) paths.push(...collectMarkdownFiles(path));
 73:     else if (entry.isFile() && extname(entry.name) === '.md') paths.push(path);
 74:   }
 75:   return paths.sort();
 76: }
 77: 
 78: function parseMarkdownFixture(path) {
 79:   const raw = readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
 80:   const match = raw.match(/^---\n([\s\S]*?)\n---\s*\n([\s\S]*)$/);
 81:   if (!match) throw new Error(`${path}: missing YAML frontmatter`);
 82:   const meta = yaml.load(match[1]) || {};
 83:   return validateFixture({ ...meta, text: match[2].trim() }, path);
 84: }
 85: 
 86: function validateFixture(fixture, source) {
 87:   for (const field of REQUIRED_FIXTURE_FIELDS) {
 88:     if (!(field in fixture)) throw new Error(`missing ${field} in ${source}`);
 89:   }
 90:   if (!['en', 'ko', 'zh', 'ja'].includes(fixture.language)) {
 91:     throw new Error(`unsupported language ${fixture.language} in ${source}`);
 92:   }
 93:   const anchors = normalizeStringArray(fixture.anchors ?? fixture.facts, `${source}: anchors`);
 94:   if (anchors.length === 0) throw new Error(`anchors/facts must be a non-empty array in ${source}`);
 95:   return {
 96:     register: 'unspecified',
 97:     source_type: 'fixture',
 98:     model_family: 'fixture',
 99:     prompt_id: 'live-quality-v2',
100:     ...fixture,
101:     anchors,
102:     facts: anchors,
103:     expected_focus: normalizeStringArray(fixture.expected_focus, `${source}: expected_focus`, { required: false }),
104:   };
105: }
106: 
107: function normalizeStringArray(value, source, { required = true } = {}) {
108:   if (value === undefined || value === null) {
109:     if (required) throw new Error(`${source} must be an array`);
110:     return [];
111:   }
112:   if (!Array.isArray(value)) throw new Error(`${source} must be an array`);
113:   return value.map((item) => String(item).trim()).filter(Boolean);
114: }
115: 
116: export async function buildPatinaRewritePrompt(fixture, { repoRoot = getRepoRoot() } = {}) {
117:   const config = loadConfig();
118:   config.language = fixture.language;
119:   if (fixture.profile) config.profile = fixture.profile;
120: 
121:   const patterns = loadPatterns(repoRoot, fixture.language);
122:   const profile = loadProfile(repoRoot, config.profile || 'default');
123:   const voice = loadCoreFile(repoRoot, 'voice.md');
124: 
125:   return buildPrompt({
126:     config,
127:     patterns,
128:     profile: profile.body ? profile : null,
129:     voice: voice.body ? voice : null,
130:     scoring: null,
131:     text: fixture.text,
132:     mode: 'rewrite',
133:   });
134: }
135: 
136: export function deliveredRewrite(rawRewrite, { logger = { warn() {} } } = {}) {
137:   return formatOutput(String(rawRewrite || ''), 'rewrite', {}, { logger }).trim();
138: }
139: 
140: export function evaluateRewriteQuality(fixture, rawRewrite, { repoRoot = REPO_ROOT } = {}) {
141:   const rewrite = deliveredRewrite(rawRewrite);
142:   const before = scoreProseText(fixture.text, {
143:     file: `${fixture.fixture_id}.md`,
144:     lang: fixture.language,
145:     repoRoot,
146:   });
147:   const after = scoreProseText(rewrite, {
148:     file: `${fixture.fixture_id}.rewrite.md`,
149:     lang: fixture.language,
150:     repoRoot,
151:   });
152:   const humanizationGain = round1(before.score - after.score);
153:   const meaningSafety = round1(computeMeaningSafety(fixture, rewrite));
154:   const safeGain = round1(Math.max(0, humanizationGain) * (meaningSafety / 100));
155:   const status = classifyQuality({ afterScore: after.score, meaningSafety, safeGain });
156:   const facts = preservedFacts(fixture.facts, rewrite, fixture.language);
157: 
158:   return {
159:     fixture_id: fixture.fixture_id,
160:     language: fixture.language,
161:     register: fixture.register,
162:     mode: 'offline-candidate',
163:     before_score: round1(before.score),
164:     after_score: round1(after.score),
165:     humanization_gain: humanizationGain,
166:     meaning_safety: meaningSafety,
167:     safe_gain: safeGain,
168:     status,
169:     preserved_facts: facts.preserved,
170:     total_facts: facts.total,
171:   };
172: }
173: 
174: export async function evaluateModelGradedRewrite(fixture, rawRewrite, options = {}) {
175:   const repoRoot = options.repoRoot || REPO_ROOT;
176:   const policy = options.policy || DEFAULT_POLICY;
177:   const settings = options.settings || resolveLiveSettings(options);
178:   const rewrite = deliveredRewrite(rawRewrite, { logger: options.logger });
179:   const config = loadConfig();
180:   config.language = fixture.language;
181:   if (fixture.profile) config.profile = fixture.profile;
182:   const patterns = loadPatterns(repoRoot, fixture.language);
183:   const deadline = settings.timeoutMs ? Date.now() + settings.timeoutMs : undefined;
184:   const callLLM = createLiveCallLLM(options.callLLM || defaultCallLLM, settings);
185: 
186:   const common = {
187:     apiKey: settings.apiKey,
188:     baseURL: settings.baseURL,
189:     model: settings.model,
190:     deadline,
191:     callLLM,
192:     logger: options.logger,
193:   };
194: 
195:   const [beforeScore, afterScore, mpsResult, fidelityResult] = await Promise.all([
196:     scoreText({ text: fixture.text, config, patterns, ...common }),
197:     scoreText({ text: rewrite, config, patterns, ...common }),
198:     scoreMPS({ original: fixture.text, rewritten: rewrite, ...common }),
199:     scoreFidelity({ original: fixture.text, rewritten: rewrite, ...common }),
200:   ]);
201: 
202:   return modelGradedResult({
203:     fixture,
204:     beforeScore,
205:     afterScore,
206:     mpsResult,
207:     fidelityResult,
208:     policy,
209:   });
210: }
211: 
212: function modelGradedResult({ fixture, beforeScore, afterScore, mpsResult, fidelityResult, policy }) {
213:   const before = numberOrNull(beforeScore?.overall);
214:   const after = numberOrNull(afterScore?.overall);
215:   const mps = numberOrNull(mpsResult?.mps);
216:   const fidelity = numberOrNull(fidelityResult?.fidelity);
217:   const errors = [];
218:   const violations = [];
219: 
220:   if (before === null || beforeScore?.error) errors.push('before-score-unavailable');
221:   if (after === null || afterScore?.error) errors.push('after-score-unavailable');
222:   if (mps === null || mpsResult?.error) errors.push('mps-unavailable');
223:   if (fidelity === null || fidelityResult?.error) errors.push('fidelity-unavailable');
224: 
225:   if (mps !== null && mps < policy.mpsFloor) violations.push(`mps<${policy.mpsFloor}`);
226:   if (fidelity !== null && fidelity < policy.fidelityFloor) violations.push(`fidelity<${policy.fidelityFloor}`);
227:   if (after !== null && after > policy.aiAfterCeiling) violations.push(`ai_after>${policy.aiAfterCeiling}`);
228:   if (policy.requireAiImprovement && before !== null && after !== null && after >= before) {
229:     violations.push('ai_not_improved');
230:   }
231: 
232:   const meaningUnsafe = violations.some((item) => item.startsWith('mps<') || item.startsWith('fidelity<'));
233:   const status = errors.length > 0 || meaningUnsafe
234:     ? 'error'
235:     : violations.length > 0
236:       ? 'warn'
237:       : 'pass';
238: 
239:   return {
240:     fixture_id: fixture.fixture_id,
241:     language: fixture.language,
242:     register: fixture.register,
243:     mode: 'live-api',
244:     status,
245:     before_score: before,
246:     after_score: after,
247:     ai_delta: before !== null && after !== null ? round1(before - after) : null,
248:     mps,
249:     fidelity,
250:     policy_violations: violations,
251:     errors,
252:   };
253: }
254: 
255: function createLiveCallLLM(callLLM, settings) {
256:   return (args) => callLLM({
257:     ...args,
258:     timeout: settings.timeoutMs,
259:   });
260: }
261: 
262: export function computeMeaningSafety(fixture, rewrite) {
263:   const facts = preservedFacts(fixture.facts, rewrite, fixture.language);
264:   const factScore = facts.total ? (facts.preserved.length / facts.total) * 100 : 100;
265:   const lengthScore = lengthSafetyScore(fixture.text, rewrite);
266:   return Math.min(factScore, lengthScore);
267: }
268: 
269: export function classifyQuality({ afterScore, meaningSafety, safeGain }) {
270:   if (afterScore <= 30 && meaningSafety >= 70 && safeGain > 0) return 'pass';
271:   if (meaningSafety >= 70 && safeGain > 0) return 'warn';
272:   return 'fail';
273: }
274: 
275: export async function runLiveQuality(options = {}) {
276:   const report = await runLiveQualityReport(options);
277:   return report.results;
278: }
279: 
280: export async function runLiveQualityReport(options = {}) {
281:   const fixtures = selectFixtures(options.fixtures ?? loadLiveFixtures(options.fixturePath), options);
282:   const policy = { ...DEFAULT_POLICY, ...(options.policy || {}) };
283:   const liveRequested = shouldRunLive(options);
284:   const settings = resolveLiveSettings(options);
285:   const candidateDir = options.candidateDir ? resolve(options.candidateDir) : null;
286:   const results = [];
287: 
288:   if (fixtures.length === 0) throw new Error('no live-quality fixtures selected');
289: 
290:   for (const fixture of fixtures) {
291:     const candidate = candidateDir ? readCandidate(candidateDir, fixture.fixture_id) : null;
292:     if (!liveRequested && !candidate) {
293:       results.push(skippedResult(fixture, 'live rewrite disabled; pass --live, set PATINA_LIVE=1, or pass --candidate-dir'));
294:       continue;
295:     }
296:     if (liveRequested && !settings.hasApiKey) {
297:       results.push(failedResult(fixture, new Error('live rewrite requested but no API key was found')));
298:       continue;
299:     }
300: 
301:     try {
302:       const rawRewrite = candidate ?? await runWithApi(fixture, { ...options, settings });
303:       const result = liveRequested
304:         ? await evaluateModelGradedRewrite(fixture, rawRewrite, { ...options, settings, policy })
305:         : evaluateRewriteQuality(fixture, rawRewrite, options);
306:       results.push(result);
307:     } catch (err) {
308:       results.push(failedResult(fixture, err));
309:     }
310:   }
311: 
312:   return buildReport({ results, settings: redactSettings(settings), policy });
313: }
314: 
315: function shouldRunLive(options = {}) {
316:   if (options.dryRun) return false;
317:   if (options.live !== undefined) return Boolean(options.live);
318:   const env = options.env || process.env;
319:   return env.PATINA_LIVE === '1' ||
320:     Boolean(env.PATINA_LIVE_PROVIDER || env.PATINA_LIVE_API_KEY || env.PATINA_LIVE_MODEL || env.PATINA_LIVE_API_BASE);
321: }
322: 
323: export function resolveLiveSettings(options = {}) {
324:   const env = options.env || process.env;
325:   const providerName = options.provider ?? env.PATINA_LIVE_PROVIDER ?? env.PATINA_PROVIDER ?? null;
326:   const provider = selectProvider(providerName);
327:   const explicitApiKey = options.apiKey ?? env.PATINA_LIVE_API_KEY ?? null;
328:   const fallbackKey = explicitApiKey ? undefined : resolveOptionalApiKey(provider, env, options.apiKeyFile);
329:   const apiKey = explicitApiKey || fallbackKey?.apiKey || null;
330:   const apiKeySource = explicitApiKey
331:     ? (options.apiKey ? 'option:apiKey' : 'env:PATINA_LIVE_API_KEY')
332:     : fallbackKey?.source ?? null;
333:   const baseURL = options.baseURL ?? env.PATINA_LIVE_API_BASE ?? env.PATINA_API_BASE;
334:   const model = options.model ?? env.PATINA_LIVE_MODEL ?? env.PATINA_MODEL;
335:   const resolved = resolveProviderConfig({ provider, apiKey, baseURL, model });
336:   const timeoutMs = parsePositiveInt(options.timeoutMs ?? env.PATINA_LIVE_TIMEOUT_MS, 120000);
337: 
338:   return {
339:     provider: provider?.name ?? null,
340:     baseURL: resolved.baseURL,
341:     model: resolved.model,
342:     apiKey,
343:     hasApiKey: Boolean(apiKey),
344:     apiKeySource,
345:     baseURLSource: baseURL ? sourceLabel(options.baseURL, env.PATINA_LIVE_API_BASE, 'baseURL') : resolved.baseURLSource,
346:     modelSource: model ? sourceLabel(options.model, env.PATINA_LIVE_MODEL, 'model') : resolved.modelSource,
347:     timeoutMs,
348:   };
349: }
350: 
351: function resolveOptionalApiKey(provider, env, apiKeyFile) {
352:   try {
353:     const envVars = providerHttpKeyEnvVars(provider?.apiKeyEnv);
354:     const apiKey = resolveHttpApiKey({ apiKeyFile, env, envVars });
355:     if (!apiKey) return null;
356:     if (apiKeyFile) return { apiKey, source: 'option:apiKeyFile' };
357:     const source = envVars.find((key) => env[key]) || 'env:PATINA_API_KEY';
358:     return { apiKey, source: `env:${source}` };
359:   } catch (err) {
360:     if (apiKeyFile) throw err;
361:     return null;
362:   }
363: }
364: 
365: function sourceLabel(optionValue, liveEnvValue, name) {
366:   if (optionValue) return `option:${name}`;
367:   if (liveEnvValue) return `env:PATINA_LIVE_${name === 'baseURL' ? 'API_BASE' : 'MODEL'}`;
368:   return 'env:PATINA';
369: }
370: 
371: function redactSettings(settings) {
372:   const { apiKey: _apiKey, ...safe } = settings;
373:   return safe;
374: }
375: 
376: function buildReport({ results, settings, policy }) {
377:   const summary = {
378:     total: results.length,
379:     pass: results.filter((result) => result.status === 'pass').length,
380:     warn: results.filter((result) => result.status === 'warn').length,
381:     error: results.filter((result) => result.status === 'error' || result.status === 'fail').length,
382:     skipped: results.filter((result) => result.status === 'skipped').length,
383:   };
384:   return {
385:     schema_version: LIVE_QUALITY_SCHEMA_VERSION,
386:     settings,
387:     policy,
388:     summary,
389:     results,
390:   };
391: }
392: 
393: export function renderMarkdownReport(reportOrResults) {
394:   const report = Array.isArray(reportOrResults)
395:     ? buildReport({ results: reportOrResults, settings: { legacy: true }, policy: DEFAULT_POLICY })
396:     : reportOrResults;
397:   const lines = [
398:     '# Patina live rewrite quality',
399:     '',
400:     `schema_version: ${report.schema_version}`,
401:     `provider: ${report.settings.provider ?? 'default'}`,
402:     `model: ${report.settings.model ?? 'default'}`,
403:     `api_key: ${report.settings.hasApiKey ? `present (${report.settings.apiKeySource || 'unknown source'})` : 'missing'}`,
404:     `policy: AI-after<=${report.policy.aiAfterCeiling}, MPS>=${report.policy.mpsFloor}, fidelity>=${report.policy.fidelityFloor}`,
405:     '',
406:     '| status | fixture | lang | mode | before | after | delta | mps | fidelity | notes |',
407:     '|---|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|',
408:   ];
409: 
410:   for (const result of report.results) {
411:     const notes = [
412:       ...(result.policy_violations || []),
413:       ...(result.errors || []),
414:       result.reason,
415:     ].filter(Boolean).join('; ');
416:     const cells = [
417:       result.status,
418:       result.fixture_id,
419:       result.language,
420:       result.mode || 'offline-candidate',
421:       formatNumber(result.before_score),
422:       formatNumber(result.after_score),
423:       formatNumber(result.ai_delta ?? result.humanization_gain),
424:       formatNumber(result.mps),
425:       formatNumber(result.fidelity ?? result.meaning_safety),
426:       notes || '-',
427:     ];
428:     lines.push(`| ${cells.join(' | ')} |`);
429:   }
430: 
431:   lines.push('', `Summary: ${report.summary.pass} pass, ${report.summary.warn} warn, ${report.summary.error} error, ${report.summary.skipped} skipped.`);
432:   return `${lines.join('\n')}\n`;
433: }
434: 
435: export function parseArgs(argv = process.argv.slice(2)) {
436:   const options = {};
437:   for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i += 1) {
438:     const arg = argv[i];
439:     if (arg === '--json') options.json = true;
440:     else if (arg === '--dry-run') options.dryRun = true;
441:     else if (arg === '--live') options.live = true;
442:     else if (arg === '--fixtures') options.fixturePath = resolve(argv[++i]);
443:     else if (arg === '--candidate-dir') options.candidateDir = argv[++i];
444:     else if (arg === '--language') options.language = argv[++i];
445:     else if (arg === '--limit') options.limit = Number(argv[++i]);
446:     else if (arg === '--model') options.model = argv[++i];
447:     else if (arg === '--provider') options.provider = argv[++i];
448:     else if (arg === '--base-url') options.baseURL = argv[++i];
449:     else if (arg === '--api-key-file') options.apiKeyFile = argv[++i];
450:     else if (arg === '--timeout-ms') options.timeoutMs = Number(argv[++i]);
451:     else if (arg === '--help' || arg === '-h') options.help = true;
452:     else throw new Error(`unknown argument: ${arg}`);
453:   }
454:   return options;
455: }
456: 
457: export async function main(argv = process.argv.slice(2)) {
458:   const options = parseArgs(argv);
459:   if (options.help) {
460:     console.log(helpText());
461:     return;
462:   }
463: 
464:   const report = await runLiveQualityReport(options);
465:   if (options.json) console.log(JSON.stringify(report, null, 2));
466:   else process.stdout.write(renderMarkdownReport(report));
467: 
468:   if (report.summary.error > 0) process.exitCode = 1;
469: }
470: 
471: async function runWithApi(fixture, options = {}) {
472:   const prompt = await buildPatinaRewritePrompt(fixture, options);
473:   const settings = options.settings || resolveLiveSettings(options);
474:   const callLLM = createLiveCallLLM(options.callLLM || defaultCallLLM, settings);
475:   return callLLM({
476:     prompt,
477:     apiKey: settings.apiKey,
478:     baseURL: settings.baseURL,
479:     model: settings.model,
480:     temperature: 0.2,
481:   });
482: }
483: 
484: function selectFixtures(fixtures, { language, limit } = {}) {
485:   let selected = fixtures;
486:   if (language) selected = selected.filter((fixture) => fixture.language === language);
487:   if (Number.isFinite(limit) && limit >= 0) selected = selected.slice(0, limit);
488:   return selected;
489: }
490: 
491: function readCandidate(candidateDir, fixtureId) {
492:   const path = resolve(candidateDir, `${fixtureId}.md`);
493:   if (!existsSync(path)) throw new Error(`missing candidate rewrite: ${path}`);
494:   return readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
495: }
496: 
497: function skippedResult(fixture, reason) {
498:   const before = scoreProseText(fixture.text, {
499:     file: `${fixture.fixture_id}.md`,
500:     lang: fixture.language,
501:     repoRoot: REPO_ROOT,
502:   });
503:   return {
504:     fixture_id: fixture.fixture_id,
505:     language: fixture.language,
506:     register: fixture.register,
507:     mode: 'skipped',
508:     before_score: round1(before.score),
509:     after_score: null,
510:     humanization_gain: null,
511:     meaning_safety: null,
512:     safe_gain: null,
513:     status: 'skipped',
514:     reason,
515:   };
516: }
517: 
518: function failedResult(fixture, err) {
519:   return {
520:     fixture_id: fixture.fixture_id,
521:     language: fixture.language,
522:     register: fixture.register,
523:     mode: 'live-api',
524:     before_score: null,
525:     after_score: null,
526:     ai_delta: null,
527:     mps: null,
528:     fidelity: null,
529:     status: 'error',
530:     errors: [err.message],
531:   };
532: }
533: 
534: function preservedFacts(facts, rewrite, language = 'en') {
535:   const lowerRewrite = language === 'en' ? rewrite.toLowerCase() : rewrite;
536:   const preserved = facts.filter((fact) => {
537:     const needle = language === 'en' ? String(fact).toLowerCase() : String(fact);
538:     return lowerRewrite.includes(needle);
539:   });
540:   return { preserved, total: facts.length };
541: }
542: 
543: function lengthSafetyScore(original, rewrite) {
544:   if (!original || !rewrite) return 0;
545:   const ratio = rewrite.length / original.length;
546:   if (ratio >= 0.7 && ratio <= 1.3) return 100;
547:   if (ratio >= 0.5 && ratio <= 1.5) return 80;
548:   if (ratio >= 0.3 && ratio <= 2.0) return 60;
549:   return 30;
550: }
551: 
552: function parsePositiveInt(value, fallback) {
553:   const n = Number(value);
554:   return Number.isFinite(n) && n > 0 ? Math.floor(n) : fallback;
555: }
556: 
557: function numberOrNull(value) {
558:   const n = Number(value);
559:   return Number.isFinite(n) ? round1(n) : null;
560: }
561: 
562: function round1(n) {
563:   return Math.round(Number(n) * 10) / 10;
564: }
565: 
566: function formatNumber(n) {
567:   return Number.isFinite(n) ? Number(n).toFixed(1) : '-';
568: }
569: 
570: function helpText() {
571:   return `Usage: npm run quality:live -- [options]
572: 
573: Runs the deliberate live rewrite quality probe. By default this does not call a model; pass --live or set PATINA_LIVE=1 to use an OpenAI-compatible provider.
574: 
575: Options:
576:   --live                  Run credentialed API rewrites and model-graded checks
577:   --provider <name>       Provider preset (openai, gemini, groq, kimi, moonshot, together)
578:   --model <id>            Model id (or PATINA_LIVE_MODEL)
579:   --base-url <url>        OpenAI-compatible base URL (or PATINA_LIVE_API_BASE)
580:   --api-key-file <path>   Read API key from a file
581:   --timeout-ms <ms>       Per fixture live timeout budget (default: 120000)
582:   --fixtures <path>       Fixture directory or legacy JSONL file
583:   --candidate-dir <dir>   Score precomputed rewrites named <fixture_id>.md
584:   --language <lang>       Filter fixtures by language
585:   --limit <n>             Limit selected fixtures
586:   --json                  Emit structured JSON report
587:   --dry-run               Force skip mode even if PATINA_LIVE_* is set
588: `;
589: }
590: 
591: if (import.meta.url === `file://${process.argv[1]}`) {
592:   main().catch((err) => {
593:     console.error(err.message);
594:     process.exitCode = 1;
595:   });
596: }
````

212: test('scoreText prompt digest lists every pattern and matches the stated denominators', async () => {
213:   const patterns = loadPatterns(getRepoRoot(), 'ko');
214:   assert.ok(patterns.length > 0, 'expected real ko pattern packs');
215: 
216:   let prompt = '';
217:   await scoreText({
218:     text: 'Sample text.',
219:     config: {
220:       ...loadConfig(),
221:       language: 'ko',
222:       scoring: { deterministic: { enabled: false } },
223:     },
224:     patterns,
225:     callLLM: async (args) => {
226:       prompt = args.prompt;
227:       return '{ "overall": 12, "interpretation": "human" }';
228:     },
229:   });
230: 
231:   const countsSection = prompt
232:     .split('Pattern counts from pack frontmatter (use as pattern_count denominators):\n')[1]
233:     .split('\n\n')[0];
234:   const digestSection = prompt
235:     .split('Compact pattern catalog digest:\n')[1]
236:     .split('\n\n')[0];
237: 
238:   const statedCounts = new Map(
239:     countsSection.split('\n').map((line) => {
240:       const match = line.match(/^- (\S+): (\d+) patterns$/);
241:       assert.ok(match, `unparseable pattern-count line: ${line}`);
242:       return [match[1], Number(match[2])];
243:     })
244:   );
245:   assert.strictEqual(statedCounts.size, patterns.length);
246: 
247:   const digestLines = digestSection.split('\n');
248:   assert.strictEqual(digestLines.length, patterns.length);
249:   for (const line of digestLines) {
250:     const match = line.match(/^- (\S+): (.+)$/);
251:     assert.ok(match, `unparseable digest line: ${line}`);
252:     const [, pack, entries] = match;
253:     assert.ok(statedCounts.has(pack), `digest pack ${pack} missing from pattern counts`);
254:     // Issue #397: the digest must show as many catalog entries as the
255:     // pattern_count denominator the same prompt instructs the model to use.
256:     assert.strictEqual(
257:       entries.split('; ').length,
258:       statedCounts.get(pack),
259:       `digest entries for ${pack} must equal its stated pattern_count denominator`
260:     );
261:   }
262: });

300: test('deterministic markup-leakage short-circuits the score into the heavily-AI band', () => {
301:   const leakedText = [
302:     'I rewrote the parser this morning and it finally handles nested quotes without choking on them. The previous version tripped over one rare edge case that took the better part of two days to track down and reproduce.',
303:     'Reviewers wanted another pass on the error copy, so I split the longest messages into a short summary plus a separate hint. According to turn0search1 the phrasing still needs work, yet the overall structure holds together fine.',
304:     'We shipped it behind a flag and watched the logs over lunch. Nothing broke. The on-call engineer shrugged and went back to her coffee.',
305:   ].join('\n\n');

142: import { splitProseSentences } from '../../src/features/segment.js';
143: 
144: // Reference assembly identical to the pre-A5 buildKoreanSignals: each proxy
145: // recomputes its own eojeols. koreanDiagnostics must match it exactly while
146: // tokenizing eojeols only once.
147: function referenceKoreanSignals(paragraph, sentenceCount, { enabled = true, bands = DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS } = {}) {
148:   const spacing = koreanSpacingFeatures(paragraph);
149:   const comma = commaDensity(paragraph, sentenceCount);
150:   const posDiversity = koreanPosDiversityProxy(paragraph);
151:   const koDiagnostics = enabled
152:     ? classifyKoreanDiagnostics({ sentenceCount, spacing, comma, posDiversity }, bands)
153:     : { hot: false, strength: 0, reasons: [], thresholds: bands };
154:   return { spacing, comma, posDiversity, koDiagnostics };
155: }

159:     '이 도구는 혁신적이다. 이 도구는 효율적이고 신뢰할 수 있다. 팀은 확장성 때문에 빠르게 채택한다. 또한 측정 가능한 가치를 제공한다.',
160:     '어제 친구랑 카페 갔는데, 거기 커피가 진짜 맛있더라. 너도 한번 가봐! 분위기도 좋고, 가격도 안 비싸.',
161:     KO_COMPOSITE_TEXT,
162:     '', // empty paragraph

239: // --- A2: rolling MATTR equivalence -----------------------------------------
240: 
241: // Exact pre-refactor reference: rebuilds a Set for every window slice.
242: function refMattr(tokens, window = 50) {
243:   if (!Array.isArray(tokens) || tokens.length === 0) return null;
244:   const lower = tokens.map((t) => t.toLowerCase());
245:   if (lower.length < window) {
246:     return new Set(lower).size / lower.length;
247:   }
248:   let sum = 0;
249:   let count = 0;
250:   for (let i = 0; i + window <= lower.length; i++) {
251:     const slice = lower.slice(i, i + window);
252:     sum += new Set(slice).size / window;
253:     count++;
254:   }
255:   return sum / count;
256: }
257: 
258: test('mattr rolling implementation is bit-identical to slice+Set reference', () => {
259:   const patterns = {
260:     repeated: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, () => 'the'),
261:     alternating: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, (_, i) => (i % 2 ? 'beta' : 'alpha')),
262:     unique: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, (_, i) => `tok${i}`),
263:     mixedCase: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, (_, i) => ['The', 'the', 'THE', 'tHe', 'Cat', 'CAT'][i % 6]),
264:     unicode: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, (_, i) => ['café', 'CAFÉ', 'naïve', 'Naïve', 'Über', 'über'][i % 6]),
265:     cjk: (len) => Array.from({ length: len }, (_, i) => ['工', '具', '写', '作', '模', '型'][i % 6]),
266:   };
267: 
268:   // Required scope: windows 1/2/40/50; lengths empty/below-window/exact-window/window+1/long.
269:   const windows = [1, 2, 40, 50];
270:   for (const w of windows) {
271:     const lengths = [0, Math.max(0, w - 1), w, w + 1, w + 75];
272:     for (const [name, gen] of Object.entries(patterns)) {
273:       for (const len of lengths) {
274:         const tokens = gen(len);
275:         const got = mattr(tokens, w);
276:         const want = refMattr(tokens, w);
277:         assert.ok(
278:           Object.is(got, want),
279:           `mattr mismatch pattern=${name} window=${w} len=${len}: got ${got} want ${want}`,
280:         );
281:       }
282:     }
283:   }
284: 
285:   // Default window (50) parity, including a long input on the rolling path.
286:   const longDefault = Array.from({ length: 130 }, (_, i) => ['alpha', 'beta', 'gamma', 'alpha', 'beta'][i % 5]);
287:   assert.ok(Object.is(mattr(longDefault), refMattr(longDefault, 50)));
288:   // Empty / degenerate inputs.
289:   assert.equal(mattr([]), null);
290:   assert.equal(mattr('not-an-array'), null);
291: });
292: 
293: test('extractStructuralFeatures vectors unchanged after rolling mattr (parity)', () => {
294:   // Captured from the pre-refactor (slice+Set) implementation; the rolling
295:   // version must reproduce the full structural vector for every language.
296:   const STRUCTURAL_BASELINE = {
297:     en: [0.4065457283638481, 7.25, 2.947456530637899, 0.8275862068965517, 0.7931034482758621, 0.7916666666666666, 5.896551724137931, 0.25, 0.028409090909090908, 1, 0, 0],
298:     ko: [0.5160156871153361, 6.5, 3.3541019662496847, 0.8846153846153846, 0.8846153846153846, 0.9130434782608695, 2.769230769230769, 0, 0.05263157894736842, 0.75, 0.11538461538461539, 0],
299:     zh: [0.3197799481205415, 9.666666666666666, 3.0912061651652345, 0.8275862068965517, 0.8275862068965517, 0.7916666666666666, 1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0],
300:     ja: [0.23022081247934104, 14.333333333333334, 3.2998316455372216, 0.7209302325581395, 0.75625, 0.7096774193548387, 1, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0],
301:   };
302:   const texts = {
303:     en: 'The tool is innovative. The tool is efficient and reliable. Teams adopt it quickly because it scales.\n\nMoreover, the platform delivers measurable value across diverse organizational contexts and workflows.',
304:     ko: '이 도구는 혁신적이다. 이 도구는 효율적이고 신뢰할 수 있다. 팀은 확장성 때문에 빠르게 채택한다.\n\n또한 이 플랫폼은 다양한 조직 맥락과 업무 흐름에서 측정 가능한 가치를 제공한다.',
305:     zh: '这个工具很创新。这个工具高效可靠。团队因为可扩展性而迅速采用它。',
306:     ja: 'このツールは革新的だ。このツールは効率的で信頼できる。チームは拡張性のために素早く採用する。',
307:   };
308:   for (const [lang, t] of Object.entries(texts)) {
309:     assert.deepEqual(extractStructuralFeatures(t, { lang }), STRUCTURAL_BASELINE[lang]);
310:   }
311: });
312: 
313: // extractStructuralFeatures default MATTR window is 40; analyzer default is 50.
314: // The exhaustive mattr≡refMattr test above covers both windows on the rolling
315: // path, so the integration result is identical for every possible token array.
316: test('extractStructuralFeatures mattr element matches reference at window 40', () => {
317:   const long = `${'alpha beta gamma delta epsilon zeta eta theta iota kappa '.repeat(8)}lambda mu nu.`;
318:   const vec = extractStructuralFeatures(long, { lang: 'en', mattrWindow: 40 });
319:   // Replicate the structural-features tokenization pipeline exactly.
320:   const normalized = long.normalize('NFC');
321:   const sentences = splitParagraphs(normalized).flatMap((p) => splitProseSentences(p));
322:   const tokens = sentences.flatMap((s) => tokenize(s, { lang: 'en' }));
323:   assert.ok(tokens.length > 40, 'expected the long input to exercise the rolling path');
324:   assert.ok(Object.is(vec[4], refMattr(tokens, 40)));
325: });
````

## File: core/scoring.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: name: AI-Likeness Scoring Algorithm
  3: version: 1.0.0
  4: description: Pattern-based AI-likeness scoring reference for patina score and ouroboros modes
  5: ---
  6: 
  7: # AI-Likeness Scoring Algorithm
  8: 
  9: Pattern-based scoring that converts AI pattern detection results into a numeric 0-100 score.
 10: Used by `--score` mode and `--ouroboros` loop for termination gating.
 11: 
 12: ---
 13: 
 14: ## 1. Severity Scale (Per-Detection)
 15: 
 16: Severity is assigned **per detection** by the LLM during the pattern scan phase.
 17: It is NOT intrinsic to the pattern — the same pattern may receive different severity
 18: depending on how egregiously it appears in context.
 19: 
 20: | Level | Points | Criteria |
 21: |-------|--------|----------|
 22: | High | 3 | Pattern is pervasive — appears multiple times or is especially blatant |
 23: | Medium | 2 | Pattern present at moderate frequency or impact |
 24: | Low | 1 | Pattern barely present — isolated occurrence |
 25: | Not detected | 0 | Pattern not found in text |
 26: 
 27: ---
 28: 
 29: ## 2. Severity Assignment Rubric
 30: 
 31: To reduce variance between runs, follow these guidelines when assigning severity:
 32: 
 33: ### General Rubric (4+ paragraph text)
 34: 
 35: | Instances | Severity |
 36: |-----------|----------|
 37: | 1-2 isolated occurrences | Low (1) |
 38: | 3-5 occurrences, or concentrated in one section | Medium (2) |
 39: | 6+ occurrences, or pervasive throughout | High (3) |
 40: 
 41: ### Special Cases
 42: 
 43: - **Structure patterns (#25-28):** Assess at document level, not instance count.
 44:   A single structural issue (e.g., every paragraph follows identical template) is High.
 45: - **Communication patterns (#19-21):** A single clear chatbot expression
 46:   (e.g., "좋은 질문입니다!") may be High — these are strong AI signals regardless of count.
 47: - **Short text (1-2 paragraphs):** Adjust thresholds proportionally.
 48:   2 instances in 2 paragraphs = Medium, not Low.
 49: 
 50: ---
 51: 
 52: ## 3. Category Derivation
 53: 
 54: Categories are derived **dynamically** from pack frontmatter, not hardcoded.
 55: 
 56: ```
 57: Category = pack frontmatter `pack` field minus language prefix
 58: Example: pack: ko-content  → category: content
 59:          pack: en-style    → category: style
 60:          pack: ko-custom   → category: custom
 61: 
 62: Pattern count = pack frontmatter `patterns` field
 63: ```
 64: 
 65: If multiple packs map to the same category (e.g., `ko-content` and `custom/ko-content`),
 66: their patterns are merged and counts summed for that category.
 67: 
 68: Unknown categories (from custom packs not in the weight config) get default weight: **0.10**.
 69: 
 70: ---
 71: 
 72: ## 4. Category Weights
 73: 
 74: ### Korean (ko)
 75: 
 76: | Category | Weight | Patterns | Notes |
 77: |----------|--------|----------|-------|
 78: | content | 0.18 | 6 | |
 79: | language | 0.18 | 9 | |
 80: | style | 0.18 | 6 | |
 81: | communication | 0.13 | 4 | |
 82: | filler | 0.08 | 4 | |
 83: | structure | 0.15 | 5 | |
 84: | viral-hook | 0.10 | 9 | score-only (no rewrite) |
 85: | **Total** | **1.00** | **43** | |
 86: 
 87: ### English (en)
 88: 
 89: | Category | Weight | Patterns | Notes |
 90: |----------|--------|----------|-------|
 91: | content | 0.20 | 6 | |
 92: | language | 0.20 | 9 | |
 93: | style | 0.20 | 6 | |
 94: | communication | 0.12 | 4 | |
 95: | filler | 0.08 | 4 | |
 96: | structure | 0.10 | 5 | |
 97: | viral-hook | 0.10 | 9 | score-only (no rewrite) |
 98: | **Total** | **1.00** | **43** | |
 99: 
100: ### Chinese (zh)
101: 
102: | Category | Weight | Patterns | Notes |
103: |----------|--------|----------|-------|
104: | content | 0.18 | 6 | |
105: | language | 0.18 | 9 | |
106: | style | 0.18 | 6 | |
107: | communication | 0.13 | 4 | |
108: | filler | 0.08 | 4 | |
109: | structure | 0.15 | 5 | |
110: | viral-hook | 0.10 | 9 | score-only (no rewrite) |
111: | **Total** | **1.00** | **43** | |
112: 
113: ### Japanese (ja)
114: 
115: | Category | Weight | Patterns | Notes |
116: |----------|--------|----------|-------|
117: | content | 0.18 | 6 | |
118: | language | 0.18 | 9 | |
119: | style | 0.18 | 6 | |
120: | communication | 0.13 | 4 | |
121: | filler | 0.08 | 4 | |
122: | structure | 0.15 | 5 | |
123: | viral-hook | 0.10 | 9 | score-only (no rewrite) |
124: | **Total** | **1.00** | **43** | |
125: 
126: Weights are configurable via `ouroboros.category-weights.{lang}` in `.patina.yaml`.
127: 
128: **viral-hook weight review (issue #154):** kept at `0.10`. The pack remains score-only and the three added patterns improve the category denominator/granularity rather than broadening it enough to justify a larger score contribution. Rewrite, diff, and Ouroboros modes still skip the pack.
129: 
130: ---
131: 
132: ## 5. Profile Override Adjustments
133: 
134: Before summing severities, apply profile `pattern-overrides` modifiers:
135: 
136: | Override | Factor | Effect |
137: |----------|--------|--------|
138: | amplify | × 1.5 (cap at 3) | Increases severity contribution |
139: | reduce | × 0.5 | Decreases severity contribution |
140: | suppress | × 0.0 | Excludes pattern entirely |
141: | normal (default) | × 1.0 | No change |
142: 
143: Example: blog profile suppresses #14 (bold) → pattern #14 severity becomes 0,
144: excluded from ko-style category calculation.
145: 
146: ### Language-Scoped Overrides
147: 
148: `pattern-overrides` may be nested under a language code (`ko:`, `en:`) to avoid
149: cross-language number collisions (e.g., ko #8 is "~적 접미사" while en #8 is
150: "Copula Avoidance" — the same number refers to unrelated patterns in each language).
151: 
152: ```yaml
153: # Language-scoped format (recommended for multi-language profiles)
154: pattern-overrides:
155:   ko:
156:     8: amplify    # ko-language #8 (~적 접미사)
157:     14: suppress  # ko-style #14 (볼드체)
158:   en:
159:     8: amplify    # en-language #8 (Copula Avoidance)
160:     14: suppress  # en-style #14 (Boldface)
161: ```
162: 
163: **Resolution rule:** When the active language has a sub-section under `pattern-overrides`,
164: apply **only** that sub-section's overrides. Top-level (unscoped) overrides apply to all
165: languages and are merged before language-scoped ones (language-scoped wins on conflict).
166: 
167: ---
168: 
169: ## 6. Scoring Formula
170: 
171: ### Per-Category Score
172: 
173: ```
174: category_score = (sum of adjusted severities / (pattern_count × 3)) × 100
175: ```
176: 
177: - `sum of adjusted severities`: sum severity points for all detected patterns in category,
178:   after applying profile override factors
179: - `pattern_count × 3`: maximum possible score (all patterns detected at High severity)
180: - Result: 0-100 per category
181: 
182: ### Overall Score
183: 
184: ```
185: overall_score = Σ(category_score × category_weight) for all categories
186: ```
187: 
188: ### Worked Example (Korean, default profile)
189: 
190: Input text detected patterns:
191: 
192: | Pattern | Category | Raw Severity | Override | Adjusted |
193: |---------|----------|-------------|----------|----------|
194: | #1 과도한 중요성 부여 | content | High (3) | normal | 3 |
195: | #3 피상적 분석 | content | Medium (2) | normal | 2 |
196: | #5 모호한 출처 | content | Low (1) | normal | 1 |
197: | #8 ~적 접미사 | language | Medium (2) | normal | 2 |
198: | #14 볼드체 | style | High (3) | normal | 3 |
199: | #17 이모지 | style | Medium (2) | normal | 2 |
200: | #22 채움 표현 | filler | Low (1) | normal | 1 |
201: | #25 구조적 반복 | structure | High (3) | normal | 3 |
202: 
203: Category scores:
204: 
205: | Category | Detected | Sum | Max (count×3) | Score |
206: |----------|----------|-----|---------------|-------|
207: | content | 3/6 | 3+2+1=6 | 6×3=18 | 33.3 |
208: | language | 1/9 | 2 | 9×3=27 | 7.4 |
209: | style | 2/6 | 3+2=5 | 6×3=18 | 27.8 |
210: | communication | 0/4 | 0 | 4×3=12 | 0.0 |
211: | filler | 1/4 | 1 | 4×3=12 | 8.3 |
212: | structure | 1/5 | 3 | 5×3=15 | 20.0 |
213: | viral-hook | 0/9 | 0 | 9×3=27 | 0.0 |
214: 
215: Overall = 33.3×0.18 + 7.4×0.18 + 27.8×0.18 + 0.0×0.13 + 8.3×0.08 + 20.0×0.15 + 0.0×0.10
216:        = 6.0 + 1.3 + 5.0 + 0.0 + 0.7 + 3.0 + 0.0
217:        = **16.0**
218: 
219: (Weighted terms are computed from the unrounded category scores and shown to
220: one decimal; they sum exactly to the displayed total. Unrounded: 6.000 + 1.333
221: + 5.000 + 0.000 + 0.667 + 3.000 = 16.000 → 16.0.)
222: 
223: Interpretation: 16-30 range = "거의 사람다움" (Mostly human, minor traces)
224: 
225: ---
226: 
227: ## 7. Score Interpretation
228: 
229: | Range | Label | Meaning |
230: |-------|-------|---------|
231: | 0-15 | 사람다움 | Strongly human-like |
232: | 16-30 | 거의 사람다움 | Mostly human, minor AI traces |
233: | 31-50 | 혼재 | Mixed signals, noticeable AI patterns |
234: | 51-70 | AI 느낌 | Clearly AI-generated |
235: | 71-100 | AI 생성 | Heavily AI-generated |
236: 
237: > **Variance Note:** Scores have expected variance of **±8-10 points** between runs
238: > due to LLM severity assignment. Use score ranges, not exact numbers, for comparison.
239: > A score of 42 should be interpreted as "roughly 32-52 range" for decision-making.
240: 
241: ---
242: 
243: ## 8. Known Limitations
244: 
245: - **Custom pattern packs** are auto-discovered and scored. A new category
246:   (e.g., `custom/patterns/ko-domain.md` with `pack: ko-domain`) gets default weight 0.10.
247: - **LLM non-determinism** means the same text may score differently across runs.
248:   The formula is deterministic; the severity assignment is not.
249: - **Fidelity scoring** (meaning preservation vs original) is defined in §§ 9–13 below
250:   and integrated into `--score` and `--ouroboros` pipelines.
251: 
252: ### Short-text boost (v3.11 Phase 3.2)
253: 
254: For inputs ≤200 non-whitespace chars OR ≤3 non-empty paragraphs, register-
255: sensitive categories get a 1.5x severity multiplier (capped at 3 per detection):
256: 
257: - `language` — 종결어미, register cues, sentence form patterns
258: - `style` — connectors, transition fillers, formulaic openers
259: - `viral-hook` — shock numbers, clickbait closes, hyperbolic lexicon
260: 
261: Rationale: case-04 found that voice/register shifts (e.g., `~다` ↔ `~습니다`)
262: are clearly perceived by humans but barely move the standard formula because
263: short texts only accumulate 1–2 pattern detections. The boost surfaces those
264: shifts so single-paragraph score deltas align with reader intuition.
265: 
266: The boost is applied at severity-assignment time by the scoring LLM (per the
267: prompt instruction in `buildScoreInstructions`), not as a post-multiplier on
268: category scores. This keeps the formula in §6 unchanged.
269: 
270: ---
271: 
272: ## 9. Fidelity Scoring — Overview
273: 
274: AI-likeness (§§ 1–7) measures *how AI-like the output sounds*.
275: Fidelity measures *how faithfully the output preserves the original meaning*.
276: 
277: Both dimensions are necessary: aggressive humanization can achieve a low AI score
278: by deleting content or changing meaning entirely. Fidelity scoring guards against this.
279: 
280: Fidelity scoring is integrated into `--score` and `--ouroboros` pipelines.
281: See SKILL.md § 6 (score mode) for integration details.
282: 
283: ---
284: 
285: ## 10. Fidelity Criteria
286: 
287: Four criteria, each scored independently by the LLM comparing original → output:
288: 
289: ### 10.1 Claims Preserved
290: 
291: Every factual claim in the original appears (perhaps rephrased) in the output.
292: 
293: | Level | Points | Criteria |
294: |-------|--------|----------|
295: | High | 3 | All key claims preserved — no factual content lost |
296: | Medium | 2 | Minor claims omitted — supporting details or examples dropped, but core argument intact |
297: | Low | 1 | Significant claims missing — one or more central facts or arguments absent |
298: | Fail | 0 | Core meaning lost — the output says something fundamentally different |
299: 
300: ### 10.2 No Fabrication
301: 
302: The output does not add claims, facts, or specifics not present or implied by the original.
303: 
304: | Level | Points | Criteria |
305: |-------|--------|----------|
306: | High | 3 | No fabrication — every claim in the output traces to the original |
307: | Medium | 2 | Minor additions — a reasonable inference stated as fact, or an illustrative example added |
308: | Low | 1 | Noticeable fabrication — specific numbers, names, or claims not in the original |
309: | Fail | 0 | Significant fabrication — output contains substantial invented content |
310: 
311: ### 10.3 Tone Match
312: 
313: The output's register matches the original (or the profile's target register, if explicitly overridden).
314: 
315: | Level | Points | Criteria |
316: |-------|--------|----------|
317: | High | 3 | Tone matches — formality level, domain register, and audience consistent |
318: | Medium | 2 | Slight drift — somewhat more/less formal, but still appropriate for the context |
319: | Low | 1 | Noticeable mismatch — formal original made casual (or vice versa) without profile justification |
320: | Fail | 0 | Register violation — academic text made into slang, or casual text made into legalese |
321: 
322: **Profile exception:** When a profile explicitly shifts register (e.g., blog profile amplifies
323: informality), tone match is assessed against the *profile target*, not the original register.
324: 
325: ### 10.4 Length Ratio
326: 
327: Compares output length to original. Extreme changes suggest content loss or padding.
328: 
329: | Ratio | Points | Criteria |
330: |-------|--------|----------|
331: | 70–130% | 3 | Length preserved — natural variation within ±30% |
332: | 50–69% or 131–150% | 2 | Moderate change — some compression or expansion, likely acceptable |
333: | 30–49% or 151–200% | 1 | Significant change — substantial content probably lost or padded |
334: | < 30% or > 200% | 0 | Extreme change — content almost certainly lost or heavily padded |
335: 
336: **Calculation:** `length_ratio = len(output) / len(original) × 100`
337: 
338: Length is measured in characters (not words or tokens) for language-agnostic consistency.
339: 
340: ---
341: 
342: ## 11. Fidelity Severity Assignment Rubric
343: 
344: To reduce variance, apply these guidelines when scoring fidelity criteria:
345: 
346: ### Claims Preserved
347: - Count discrete factual claims in the original. If all appear (rephrased or not) → High.
348: - If only supporting details are dropped but the argument structure survives → Medium.
349: - If a numbered list loses items, a causal chain loses a step, or a key qualifier is dropped → Low.
350: 
351: ### No Fabrication
352: - Paraphrasing that changes word choice but not meaning → High.
353: - Adding a commonly-known context note ("Seoul, the capital of South Korea") → Medium.
354: - Inventing a statistic, date, or name not in the original → Low or Fail.
355: 
356: ### Tone Match
357: - Compare the first and last paragraphs of original vs. output for register cues.
358: - Profile-targeted register shifts are expected, not penalized.
359: - Mixed register (formal opening, casual middle) counts as Low.
360: 
361: ### Length Ratio
362: - This criterion is deterministic — compute the ratio and look up the table.
363: - No LLM judgment needed. Include the raw ratio in the score output.
364: 
365: ---
366: 
367: ## 12. Fidelity Scoring Formula
368: 
369: ### Per-Criterion Score
370: 
371: Each criterion is scored 0–3 (same as AI-likeness severity). The fidelity score normalizes
372: across all four criteria:
373: 
374: ```
375: fidelity_score = ((claims + fabrication + tone + length) / 12) × 100
376: ```
377: 
378: - Maximum: (3+3+3+3) / 12 × 100 = **100** (perfect fidelity)
379: - Minimum: (0+0+0+0) / 12 × 100 = **0** (total meaning loss)
380: 
381: ### Criterion Weighting (Optional)
382: 
383: For profiles that need non-uniform criterion importance, weights can be configured:
384: 
385: ```yaml
386: fidelity:
387:   weights:
388:     claims-preserved: 0.35
389:     no-fabrication: 0.30
390:     tone-match: 0.20
391:     length-ratio: 0.15
392: ```
393: 
394: When weights are configured:
395: 
396: ```
397: fidelity_score = Σ((criterion_points / 3) × criterion_weight) × 100
398: ```
399: 
400: Default weights (when not configured): equal at 0.25 each (equivalent to the simple formula).
401: 
402: ### Worked Example
403: 
404: Original: 4-paragraph academic article about climate change policy.
405: Output: Humanized version.
406: 
407: | Criterion | Score | Reasoning |
408: |-----------|-------|-----------|
409: | Claims preserved | 3 (High) | All policy recommendations and cited figures present |
410: | No fabrication | 2 (Medium) | Added "as widely reported" — minor inference stated as fact |
411: | Tone match | 3 (High) | Academic register maintained throughout |
412: | Length ratio | 2 (Moderate) | Output is 68% of original length (within 50-69% band) |
413: 
414: Fidelity = (3+2+3+2) / 12 × 100 = **83.3**
415: 
416: Interpretation: 76-90 range = "높은 충실도" (High fidelity, minor issues)
417: 
418: ### Fidelity Score Interpretation
419: 
420: | Range | Label | Meaning |
421: |-------|-------|---------|
422: | 91-100 | 완벽한 충실도 | Perfect fidelity — all meaning preserved |
423: | 76-90 | 높은 충실도 | High fidelity — minor omissions or additions |
424: | 51-75 | 보통 충실도 | Moderate fidelity — noticeable meaning changes |
425: | 26-50 | 낮은 충실도 | Low fidelity — significant meaning loss or fabrication |
426: | 0-25 | 의미 왜곡 | Meaning severely distorted or lost |
427: 
428: > **Note:** Unlike AI-likeness (lower = better), fidelity uses **higher = better**.
429: > A score of 83 means "roughly 73-93 range" given ±10 LLM variance —
430: > solidly in the "높은 충실도" band.
431: 
432: ---
433: 
434: ## 13. Combined Score
435: 
436: ### Formula
437: 
438: AI-likeness and fidelity compose into a combined score with configurable weighting:
439: 
440: ```
441: combined = (ai_likeness × ai_weight) + (fidelity_inverted × fidelity_weight)
442: ```
443: 
444: Where:
445: - `ai_likeness`: AI-likeness score from § 6 (0-100, lower = more human)
446: - `fidelity_inverted`: `100 - fidelity_score` (invert so both dimensions use "lower is better")
447: - `ai_weight + fidelity_weight = 1.0`
448: 
449: ### Default Weights
450: 
451: | Context | AI Weight | Fidelity Weight | Rationale |
452: |---------|-----------|-----------------|-----------|
453: | Default | 0.60 | 0.40 | Balanced — humanization is primary goal |
454: | Academic profile | 0.40 | 0.60 | Meaning preservation is critical in scholarly work |
455: | Blog profile | 0.70 | 0.30 | Creative rewriting tolerated |
456: | Technical profile | 0.35 | 0.65 | Accuracy is paramount in docs |
457: | Social profile | 0.75 | 0.25 | Tone transformation expected |
458: | Email profile | 0.50 | 0.50 | Equal importance |
459: | Legal profile | 0.35 | 0.65 | Legal precision must be preserved |
460: | Medical profile | 0.35 | 0.65 | Clinical accuracy is critical |
461: | Marketing profile | 0.65 | 0.35 | Tone transformation tolerated, creative rewriting expected |
462: | Namuwiki profile | 0.65 | 0.35 | Tone transformation tolerated for wiki-style cleanup |
463: 
464: Configurable via `ouroboros.combined-weights.{profile}` in `.patina.yaml`:
465: 
466: ```yaml
467: ouroboros:
468:   combined-weights:
469:     default:
470:       ai-likeness: 0.60
471:       fidelity: 0.40
472:     academic:
473:       ai-likeness: 0.40
474:       fidelity: 0.60
475: ```
476: 
477: ### Combined Score Interpretation
478: 
479: | Range | Label | Meaning |
480: |-------|-------|---------|
481: | 0-15 | 최적 | Excellent — human-like and faithful |
482: | 16-30 | 양호 | Good — minor issues in one or both dimensions |
483: | 31-50 | 보통 | Acceptable — noticeable trade-offs |
484: | 51-70 | 주의 | Caution — significant AI traces or meaning loss |
485: | 71-100 | 부적합 | Poor — heavy AI patterns and/or substantial meaning loss |
486: 
487: ### Ouroboros Termination
488: 
489: When used with `--ouroboros`, the loop terminates when:
490: - Combined score ≤ threshold (default: 30), OR
491: - Fidelity score drops below floor (default: 70) — **hard stop**, even if AI score improves
492: 
493: This prevents the ouroboros loop from "improving" AI score by destroying content.
494: 
495: ---
496: 
497: ## 14. MPS (Meaning Preservation Score) — Overview
498: 
499: AI-likeness (§§ 1–7) measures *how AI-like the output sounds*.
500: Fidelity (§§ 9–13) measures *how faithfully the output preserves overall meaning*.
501: MPS measures *whether specific semantic anchors survive the humanization pipeline*.
502: 
503: MPS is anchor-based: it tracks discrete meaning units (claims, polarity, causation, quantifiers, negations) extracted from the original text in SKILL.md Step 4.5, and checks whether each anchor is preserved after each pipeline phase.
504: 
505: MPS complements fidelity scoring — fidelity is a holistic LLM judgment, MPS is a structured anchor-by-anchor verification.
506: 
507: ---
508: 
509: ## 15. MPS Anchor Verification Criteria
510: 
511: Each anchor extracted in Step 4.5 is verified against the pipeline output. Verification produces one of three verdicts:
512: 
513: ### PASS
514: Anchor content is present in the output and its polarity is preserved. The anchor may be rephrased but its core assertion is unambiguously recoverable.
515: 
516: ### SOFT FAIL
517: Anchor content is present but weakened or made ambiguous. Examples:
518: - Specific claim became vague: "매출이 30% 증가" → "매출이 크게 증가"
519: - Quantifier lost precision: "p<0.05" → "통계적으로 유의미하다"
520: - Causal link became correlational: "A 때문에 B가 발생" → "A와 B는 관련이 있다"
521: - Definitive statement became hedged: "시스템이 실패했다" → "시스템에 문제가 있었을 수 있다"
522: 
523: ### HARD FAIL
524: Anchor content is deleted or its polarity is inverted. Examples:
525: - Claim removed entirely from output
526: - Negation dropped: "검증되지 않았다" → "검증되었다"
527: - Causation reversed: "A가 B를 야기했다" → "B가 A를 야기했다"
528: 
529: ### PASS vs SOFT FAIL Boundary
530: 
531: If a reader can **unambiguously recover** the original anchor's meaning from the rewritten version, it is PASS. If a reader could **reasonably interpret** the rewritten version differently from the original, it is SOFT FAIL.
532: 
533: ---
534: 
535: ## 16. MPS Scoring Formula
536: 
537: ### Base Formula
538: 
539: ```
540: anchor_pass_rate = PASS_count / total_anchor_count
541: polarity_preserved = polarity_PASS_count / total_polarity_anchor_count
542: 
543: MPS = (anchor_pass_rate × 0.6 + polarity_preserved × 0.4) × 100
544: ```
545: 
546: Where:
547: - `PASS_count`: anchors with PASS verdict after all remediation (including successful retries)
548: - `total_anchor_count`: all extracted anchors
549: - `polarity_PASS_count`: polarity-type anchors (Polarity + Negation) with PASS verdict
550: - `total_polarity_anchor_count`: all polarity-type anchors (Polarity + Negation)
551: 
552: ### Retry Counting Rule
553: 
554: If a SOFT FAIL anchor passes after alternative correction (retry), it counts as **PASS** in the formula. Only anchors that remain SOFT FAIL or HARD FAIL after all remediation are counted as failures.
555: 
556: ### Fallback (No Polarity Anchors)
557: 
558: If the text contains no Polarity or Negation anchors:
559: 
560: ```
561: MPS = anchor_pass_rate × 100
562: ```
563: 
564: ### Fallback (No Anchors Extracted)
565: 
566: When anchor extraction is skipped (text ≤1 paragraph and ≤2 sentences) or yields zero anchors:
567: 
568: ```
569: MPS = N/A (not applicable)
570: ```
571: 
572: When MPS = N/A:
573: - `--score` mode displays: `의미 보존 (MPS): N/A (앵커 없음)`
574: - Ouroboros loop: MPS floor는 우회되지 않는다. 결정론 루프의 `scoreMPS`는 숫자 mps를 반환하거나 스코어러 실패 시 `null`을 반환하며, `null`은 floor 위반으로 간주해 fail-closed로 직전 반복에 rollback한다 (아래 "Ouroboros Loop Gating" 및 `src/ouroboros.js` 참고). 앵커 N/A 표시는 SKILL 파이프라인 / `--score` 표시용이며 결정론 루프의 floor를 끄지 않는다.
575: 
576: ### MPS Interpretation
577: 
578: | Range | Label | Meaning |
579: |-------|-------|---------|
580: | 90–100 | 우수 (Excellent) | Full meaning preservation |
581: | 70–89 | 양호 (Good) | Minor weakening, acceptable |
582: | 50–69 | 주의 (Warning) | Significant anchor loss, review needed |
583: | < 50 | 위험 (Critical) | Severe meaning corruption |
584: 
585: ### Worked Example
586: 
587: Original text (3 paragraphs) with extracted anchors:
588: 
589: | # | Type | Content | Verdict | After Retry |
590: |---|------|---------|---------|-------------|
591: | 1 | Claim | "시스템이 실패했다" | SOFT FAIL | PASS (retry succeeded) |
592: | 2 | Polarity | "아직 검증되지 않았다" (negative) | PASS | — |
593: | 3 | Quantifier | "매출 30% 증가" | PASS | — |
594: | 4 | Causation | "A 때문에 B 발생" | HARD FAIL | — (original restored) |
595: | 5 | Negation | "불가능하다" | PASS | — |
596: 
597: After remediation:
598: - PASS: #1 (retry), #2, #3, #5 = 4
599: - HARD FAIL: #4 = 1 (original restored, so meaning is preserved in output but pattern not humanized)
600: - Total anchors: 5
601: - Polarity anchors (#2, #5): both PASS = 2/2
602: 
603: ```
604: anchor_pass_rate = 4/5 = 0.80
605: polarity_preserved = 2/2 = 1.00
606: MPS = (0.80 × 0.6 + 1.00 × 0.4) × 100 = (0.48 + 0.40) × 100 = 88
607: ```
608: 
609: Interpretation: 70–89 range = "양호" (Good, minor weakening)
610: 
611: > **Note:** Anchor #4 was a HARD FAIL, so its original sentence was restored in the output.
612: > The anchor counts as a failure in MPS (reducing the score), but the meaning IS preserved
613: > in the output because the original was kept. MPS reflects humanization success rate,
614: > not output meaning accuracy (which is always preserved via fallback).
615: 
616: ### MPS vs Fidelity: Complementary Metrics
617: 
618: MPS measures **humanization coverage** — what fraction of meaning anchors were successfully humanized while being preserved. A HARD FAIL anchor that was restored to its original wording counts as a humanization failure (the pattern wasn't removable without meaning loss), even though the final output's meaning is intact. Fidelity (§§ 9-13) measures **overall output meaning accuracy** against the original — restored sentences score perfectly on fidelity. Use both metrics together: high fidelity + low MPS means "meaning is safe but some AI patterns couldn't be removed."
619: 
620: ---
621: 
622: ## 17. MPS Integration Points
623: 
624: ### `--score` Mode Output
625: 
626: When `--score` is used with rewrite or ouroboros mode (original text available),
627: MPS is displayed alongside AI-likeness and Fidelity:
628: 
629: | 지표 | 점수 |
630: |------|------|
631: | AI 유사도 | 23/100 (낮을수록 좋음) |
632: | 충실도 | 87/100 (높을수록 좋음) |
633: | 의미 보존 (MPS) | 92/100 (높을수록 좋음) |
634: | 종합 | 25/100 (낮을수록 좋음) |
635: 
636: > **Note:** MPS is NOT included in the combined score formula (§13).
637: > Combined score uses fidelity (holistic) while MPS is a structural verification metric.
638: > Both are displayed for transparency but serve different purposes.
639: 
640: ### Ouroboros Loop Gating
641: 
642: MPS floor = 70 (default). Independent of fidelity floor.
643: 
644: Termination condition:
645: - MPS < mps-floor → terminate with reason: **의미 보존 하한 위반** → rollback to previous iteration
646: 
647: Both fidelity floor AND MPS floor must pass for an iteration to be accepted.
648: 
649: Configurable via `.patina.yaml`:
650: 
651: ```yaml
652: ouroboros:
653:   mps-floor: 70  # default
654: ```
````

## File: docs/research/2026-judge-calibration.md
````markdown
 1: # Do the study-series judges measure anything? — calibration results
 2: 
 3: Companion to `2026-judge-calibration-prereg.md` (registered 2026-07-13 before
 4: any data). Run: 2026-07-13 00:25–02:45 KST, post-midnight window, zero claude
 5: usage, fully separated from Study 3 (which was running concurrently on its
 6: own artifacts). Decision rules below were fixed in the registration; nothing
 7: moved after the numbers arrived.
 8: 
 9: - Corpus: 20 fresh human KO web documents (collected at registration time;
10:   sha256+URL dedupe against every store the deterministic layer was ever
11:   tuned, swept, or benchmarked on — 21,482 hashes / 94 URLs excluded) +
12:   24 fresh topic-paired AI documents (8 each gpt-5.5 / kimi-k2.5 / grok-4.5,
13:   title+register+length-band pairing, human text never in a prompt).
14: - Judging: 192/192 calls parsed (0 lost) — 132 main + 60 stability repeats.
15: - Headline metrics are cross-family (each judge never scored its own
16:   family's generations); self-family cells reported separately.
17: 
18: ## Verdicts (pre-set criteria: PASS = AUC ≥ 0.75 AND median repeat SD ≤ 12; DEMOTE = AUC < 0.65 OR SD > 20)
19: 
20: | judge | accuracy | AUC [95% CI] | bias human/AI | repeat SD (median) | self-pref | verdict |
21: |---|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---|
22: | judge-kimi (k2.5) | 0.75 | 0.83 [0.69, 0.95] | 36.0 / 71.1 | 4.5 | +6.0 | **PASS** |
23: | judge-gpt (gpt-5.5) | 0.92 | **1.00 [1.00, 1.00]** | 39.1 / 92.1 | 2.2 | −9.3 | **PASS** |
24: | judge-grok (grok-4.5) | 0.92 | 0.93 [0.83, 1.00] | 32.1 / 76.1 | 4.1 | +11.9 | **PASS** |
25: | **pooled panel (2-of-3 mean)** | — | **0.96 [0.90, 1.00]** | — | — | — | **PASS** (pre-set ≥ 0.80) |
26: 
27: **The panel is real.** All three judges discriminate certain-human from
28: certain-AI Korean documents well above chance, and repeat scoring is tight
29: (SD 2–5 points on a 0–100 scale).
30: 
31: **The kimi 35↔92 swing is explained.** Kimi's repeat variance is small
32: (SD 4.5), so its Study 3 swings are *document-driven disagreement*, not
33: sampling noise: kimi is the weakest discriminator (accuracy 0.75, CI dipping
34: to 0.69) with the strongest tendency to read human docs as AI-ish (bias 36.0).
35: Its dissents are real opinions of a mediocre judge — the 2-of-3 quorum design
36: is doing exactly the work it was added for.
37: 
38: **Self-preference exists but is small and mixed.** gpt flatters its own
39: family's generations by ~9 points (classic self-preference); grok is ~12
40: points *harsher* on its own family. The study series' cross-family judging
41: rule already neutralizes this.
42: 
43: ## Deterministic stylometry on the same corpus
44: 
45: | layer | AUC [95% CI] | mean score human/AI |
46: |---|---|---|
47: | prose-score `score` (lang ko) | **0.98 [0.93, 1.00]** | 17.7 / 78.1 |
48: 
49: - The deterministic score **matches or beats every judge** on this
50:   leakage-free corpus, at zero marginal cost and perfect reproducibility.
51: - Pre-registered promotion rule (deterministic AUC ≥ best judge − 0.05 =
52:   0.95) **fires** → a promotion-review decision is filed with the operator
53:   (promotion is an operator call, not this study's).
54: - Caveat that keeps this honest: the *binary* document verdict currently used
55:   in some surfaces ("any hot paragraph") scores only 0.55 accuracy here — the
56:   continuous score separates nearly perfectly, but the hot-paragraph trigger
57:   is miscalibrated at document length. Any promotion should promote the
58:   score/threshold, not the current binary rule.
59: 
60: ## Limitations (named, not hidden)
61: 
62: - n = 16 cross-family AI + 20 human per judge — CIs are wide (kimi's spans
63:   0.69–0.95). This is a calibration smoke test, not a benchmark claim.
64: - Register skew: human side is 14 blog + 4 technical-how-to + 2 chat-update
65:   (fresh-collection reality); no product-doc/academic register.
66: - gpt's perfect 1.00 may reflect an easy corpus ceiling (3-family generic
67:   generations); it does not certify gpt on harder, edited, or rewritten text.
68: - The corpus is certain-label by construction; humanized/edited-AI middle
69:   ground — the study series' actual subject — is out of scope here.
70: 
71: ## What this means for Study 3
72: 
73: Study 3's panel scores stand on measured ground: three PASS judges, panel
74: AUC 0.96, low repeat variance. Kimi dissents should be read as a lenient
75: judge's real opinion, absorbed by the quorum.
````

## File: docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy-study1.md
````markdown
  1: # Does patina rewrite reduce AI-likeness? — Study 1 (main study) results
  2: 
  3: Companion to `2026-rewrite-efficacy-prereg.md` ("Study 1" section, registered
  4: 2026-07-10 before any Study 1 data) and to the Study 0 pilot
  5: (`2026-rewrite-efficacy.md`). Decision rules were fixed up front; nothing below
  6: moved after the numbers arrived.
  7: 
  8: - Run: 2026-07-10. Rewriter: `claude-cli` (unchanged from the pilot).
  9: - Judge panel (fixed pre-run): `kimi` (moonshot, kimi-for-coding/k2.5) +
 10:   `codex` (gpt-5.5) + `grok` (xai, grok-4.5) — all cross-family to the
 11:   rewriter; 2-of-3 quorum. gemini was excluded **before the first call**
 12:   (monthly spend cap; recorded in the pre-registration).
 13: - Corpus generation models (Arm D AI side, family rotation):
 14:   gpt-5.5 / claude-sonnet-5 / kimi-k2.5 / grok-4.5.
 15: - Raw texts and judgments stay gitignored under
 16:   `artifacts/rewrite-efficacy-study1/`; only this synthesis is committed.
 17: - Final matrix: **576/576 panel ratings present, 0 rewrite failures,
 18:   0 unparseable cells** (61 ratings arrived via the top-up pass — see
 19:   Execution notes).
 20: 
 21: ## Design recap
 22: 
 23: Two document-length arms (snippet arms were measured in the pilot and not
 24: re-run):
 25: 
 26: | Arm | Language | Corpus | n (AI+human) |
 27: |---|---|---|---|
 28: | A1 | en | HAP-E paired on `prompt_id`, disjoint from pilot items | 25+25 (21+21 after `spok` exclusion) |
 29: | D | ko | **new**: 27 human documents (1.2–4.0k chars, 5 registers, 39 vetted public sources) + 27 topic-paired AI documents (public title + register + length band only — the human text never entered a prompt) | 27+27 |
 30: 
 31: Arm D length-band enforcement: 5/27 AI documents finished slightly under
 32: their paired band after 3 attempts (worst −20%; models chronically undershoot
 33: Korean char targets) and 1 slightly over (+2.8%); all recorded
 34: (`band_met:false`), none excluded.
 35: 
 36: ## RQ1 — construct validity: **PASS both arms**
 37: 
 38: | arm | units | Krippendorff α | mean pairwise ρ | mean abs gap |
 39: |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
 40: | A1 (en doc) | 84 | **0.751** | 0.845 | 14.1 |
 41: | D (ko doc) | 108 | **0.526** | 0.568 | 18.4 |
 42: 
 43: Both clear the pre-registered α ≥ 0.4 gate. Korean document agreement (0.526)
 44: is markedly lower than English (0.751) — "AI-likeness" is a noisier construct
 45: for Korean prose even at document length, consistent with the pilot's
 46: length-penalty finding but now visible *within* document-length stimuli.
 47: 
 48: ## RQ2 — perceptual efficacy: **supported in both arms, with a 4× language gap**
 49: 
 50: AI-likeness 0–100; negative Δ = reads less AI-like after rewrite.
 51: 
 52: | arm | class | n | before → after | Δ (95% CI) | Cliff's δ | "AI"-call |
 53: |---|---|---:|---|---|---:|---|
 54: | A1 | AI | 21 | 82.9 → 59.4 | **−23.4 [−30.8, −16.5]** | −0.79 | 92% → 65% |
 55: | A1 | human | 21 | 17.2 → 20.5 | +3.3 [−0.0, +7.0] | +0.17 | 2% → 5% |
 56: | D | AI | 27 | 83.6 → 77.6 | **−6.0 [−10.0, −3.4]** | −0.49 | 95% → 93% |
 57: | D | human | 27 | 45.4 → 41.9 | −3.5 [−8.1, +0.8] | −0.13 | 25% → 22% |
 58: 
 59: - **H2a: SUPPORTED in both arms** — every AI-text CI lies below zero.
 60: - **H2b (AI-call rate halved): FAILS in both arms**, decisively in Korean
 61:   (95% → 93%).
 62: - **The headline is the language gap.** On English documents the rewrite
 63:   removes ~23 points of perceived AI-likeness and drops a third of the
 64:   AI verdicts. On Korean documents it removes **six** — judges still call
 65:   93% of rewritten Korean AI documents "AI". At document length, patina's
 66:   Korean humanization is currently marginal.
 67: 
 68: ### Anti-circularity — no gaming signature, but watch Korean
 69: 
 70: Internal-score drop vs independent-judge drop: A1 −16.7 vs −23.4 (judges move
 71: *more* than the internal score — the healthy direction); D −13.4 vs −6.0 (the
 72: internal score falls **2.2× further than judges do**). The pre-registered
 73: trigger (internal falls, judges flat) does not fire, but the Korean gap is the
 74: direction it fires in; if ko pattern work optimizes the internal score without
 75: moving judges, this becomes the detector-gaming finding.
 76: 
 77: ## RQ5 — meaning and collateral
 78: 
 79: - **RQ5a: met** — safety-gate pass 95.8% (92/96) against the ≥95% target. All
 80:   4 failures are dropped-number cases, recorded with the emitted text kept.
 81: - **RQ5b: one flag.** Rewriting *human* English documents nudges them **toward**
 82:   AI (+3.3, CI [−0.0, +7.0], AI-call 2% → 5%). The CI touches zero, so this is
 83:   a borderline over-editing signal, not a verdict — but it is the same
 84:   direction as RQ4 below and deserves a targeted look before any "safe on
 85:   human text" claim. Korean human controls moved the other way (−3.5, n.s.).
 86: 
 87: ## H6 — structural-tell survival (pre-registered primary): **SUPPORTED in both arms**
 88: 
 89: Deterministic keyword rubric over judges' `strongest_cue` on rewritten AI text
 90: (rubric fixed in the harness before any data):
 91: 
 92: | arm | structure | lexical | specificity-absence | other |
 93: |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
 94: | A1 (63 cues) | **40%** | 11% | 11% | 38% |
 95: | D (81 cues) | **75%** | 9% | 0% | 16% |
 96: 
 97: Exploratory (not pre-registered) — restricted to judgments that still called
 98: the passage "ai": A1 49% structure; **D 81% structure**.
 99: 
100: The Korean surviving tells are overwhelmingly architectural, and the verbatim
101: cues name the exact shapes: "보고서식 문장", "균일한 설명 리듬과 체크리스트식
102: 포괄성", "너무 매끈한 문제-위기-교훈 서사", "rigid three-axis scaffolding plus
103: tidy triadic close", "uniform exhaustive tutorial cadence, no personal
104: fingerprint". Meanwhile, cues that flipped judges to "human" cite *specificity*
105: (lived edge cases, px values, Slack reactions, first-person asides). This is
106: the evidence base for a **ko structure pattern pack**: break uniform paragraph
107: rhythm, checklist exhaustiveness, and the tidy problem→crisis→lesson arc;
108: inject document-level irregularity rather than word-level substitutions.
109: 
110: ## RQ4 — humanizer fingerprint: **now significant in BOTH arms**
111: 
112: | arm | rewrite cohesion | human cohesion | gap | permutation p |
113: |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
114: | A1 | 0.9323 (n=25) | 0.8833 (n=25) | +0.049 | **0.029** |
115: | D | 0.9621 (n=27) | 0.9313 (n=27) | +0.031 | **0.045** |
116: 
117: The pilot's suggestive p = 0.078 was real: at n≈25, patina's rewrites are
118: measurably more alike than independently-authored human texts, in both
119: languages. patina removes the generator's fingerprint and leaves its own — a
120: second-order tell a per-text score cannot see, and a concrete work item
121: (voice diversification / persona-conditioned variation), not just a caveat.
122: 
123: ## Sensitivity — `spok` restored (Arm A1)
124: 
125: α 0.751 → 0.747; Δ −23.4 → −25.7 [−32.4, −19.2]. Both verdicts survive; the
126: headline does not rest on the exclusion.
127: 
128: ## Execution notes (transparency)
129: 
130: - **codex quota died mid-run again** — headroom was verified on a fresh reset
131:   before the first call (as pre-registered) and the recorded run still
132:   exhausted it. Unlike the pilot's Deviation 3, the pre-registered 3-judge /
133:   2-of-3 quorum absorbed it: every affected row kept a valid 2-judge quorum
134:   during the run, and all 33 missing gpt cells were repaired by the top-up
135:   pass after the next reset. **Limitation:** those gpt ratings were collected
136:   hours after their kimi/grok counterparts (judge × time confound on 33/576
137:   cells, D arm only).
138: - 13 Korean document rewrites exceeded the 300 s rewrite timeout in-run; all
139:   13 succeeded in the top-up pass at 600 s. Document-length Korean rewrite
140:   latency runs 4–10 minutes through the live CLI path — itself a product
141:   observation.
142: - Top-up total: 61/576 ratings (10.6%) repaired; 5 in-run retries; zero
143:   schema-drift keys (the pilot's Deviation-2 hardening held).
144: 
145: ## Verdict
146: 
147: Against the pre-registered rules: **RQ1 passes in both arms; H2a is supported
148: in both arms; H2b fails in both; anti-circularity does not trigger; H5a is
149: met; H6 (structure-modal surviving cues) is supported in both arms; H4 (house
150: style) is now significant in both arms.**
151: 
152: Honestly stated: **patina reliably makes English AI documents read much less
153: AI-like (−23, a third of AI verdicts flip) while preserving meaning — but on
154: Korean documents the perceptual gain is small (−6, AI verdicts nearly
155: unmoved), the surviving tells are overwhelmingly structural, and the rewriter
156: leaves a measurable house style in both languages.** The product implications
157: are ranked by this data: (1) a Korean **structure** pattern pack, (2) house-
158: style diversification, (3) an over-editing guard for human English input.
````

## File: docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy-study2.md
````markdown
  1: # Does the ko-doc-structure pack fix the Korean gap? — Study 2 results
  2: 
  3: Companion to `2026-rewrite-efficacy-prereg.md` ("Study 2" section, registered
  4: 2026-07-12 before any data) and to Study 1 (`2026-rewrite-efficacy-study1.md`).
  5: Study 1 found Korean document rewrites barely move perceived AI-likeness
  6: (Δ −6.0) and that 75–81% of surviving cues are structural. The
  7: `ko-doc-structure` pack (6 document-architecture patterns, authored from that
  8: cue evidence) was the designed response. This study measured it.
  9: 
 10: **Verdict up front: the pack does not work in its current form, and it harms
 11: two guard rails. It must not ship with any efficacy claim, and we have pulled
 12: it from the pro-pack manifest until a redesigned intervention passes.**
 13: 
 14: - Run: 2026-07-12. Same corpus (54 Study 1 Arm-D documents), same rewriter
 15:   (claude-cli), same fixed 3-judge panel (kimi / gpt-5.5 / grok-4.5, 2-of-3
 16:   quorum). Intervention: the pack installed via the shipping `patina pack`
 17:   path (sha `36f5491f…` recorded per row); its 6 patterns entered the live
 18:   rewrite prompt (verified in Study-1-era e2e).
 19: - Final matrix: 54/54 rewrites, 0 unparseable judge cells.
 20: 
 21: ## H-S2a (primary) — paired improvement on AI documents: **REJECTED**
 22: 
 23: Baseline = each document's own Study 1 panel scores (original + rewrite).
 24: 
 25: | | orig | rewrite (S1, no pack) | rewrite (S2, pack) | paired d = rw2−rw1 (95% CI) |
 26: |---|---:|---:|---:|---|
 27: | AI docs (n=27) | 83.6 | 77.6 | 76.9 | **−0.7 [−3.4, +1.8]** |
 28: 
 29: The CI includes 0. Adding the pack's pattern descriptions to the rewrite
 30: prompt produced no detectable perceptual improvement over the pack-less
 31: rewrite. AI-call rate: 93% (S1) → 88% (S2) — marginal.
 32: 
 33: ## H-S2b — structural-cue share: **REJECTED**
 34: 
 35: Among judgments that still called the S2 rewrite "ai": **82% structural cues**
 36: (Study 1: 81%; pre-registered target: <60%). The judges' complaints are the
 37: same complaints — uniform report-style paragraphs, checklist coverage, tidy
 38: arcs. The pack described these shapes to the rewriter; the rewriter did not
 39: dismantle them.
 40: 
 41: ## Guard rails — **both violated**
 42: 
 43: 1. **Meaning-safety gate (target ≥95% pass):** 87.0% (47/54), down from 92.6%
 44:    on the same 54 documents in Study 1. All 7 failures are dropped-numbers.
 45:    Structure-level editing drops facts: when the rewriter merges sections or
 46:    compresses checklists, numbers fall out. This is exactly why the gate
 47:    exists.
 48: 2. **Human-control over-editing:** paired d = **+5.1 [+0.3, +9.6]** — the
 49:    pack-on rewrite made human documents read MORE AI-like than the pack-less
 50:    rewrite did (41.9 → 47.0 vs original 45.4), and the CI excludes 0. AI-call
 51:    on human docs rose 22% → 32%. The over-editing failure mode Study 1 flagged
 52:    as borderline on English is now measured and significant on Korean, caused
 53:    by the intervention itself.
 54: 
 55: Mean rewrite/original length ratio 0.91 (the pack's compression pressure is
 56: visible but mild).
 57: 
 58: ## Why it failed (diagnosis, not spin)
 59: 
 60: The intervention was **pattern descriptions in the prompt** — the same
 61: mechanism that works for lexical patterns. Document architecture apparently
 62: does not yield to that mechanism: the rewriter edits sentence-by-sentence and
 63: paragraph-by-paragraph, so "break the parallel sections, make coverage
 64: asymmetric" degrades into local paraphrase plus occasional deletion (hence the
 65: dropped numbers) rather than actual reorganization. The one thing the pack
 66: reliably did — push toward compression — cost meaning without buying
 67: perception.
 68: 
 69: Design implication, carried to the next iteration: structural humanization
 70: needs a **structural mechanism**, not more prompt text. The candidate designs,
 71: in order of likely cost-effectiveness:
 72: 1. a pre-rewrite **structure plan step** (LLM produces a reorganization plan —
 73:    merge/split/reorder decisions with number-preservation constraints — then
 74:    the rewrite executes it);
 75: 2. deterministic structure transforms (section merge/split scaffolding) with
 76:    LLM infill;
 77: 3. persona-conditioned document templates per register.
 78: 
 79: ## Execution notes
 80: 
 81: - Two infrastructure failures interrupted the run (a duplicated config key
 82:   from the over-editing-guard commit; a claude CLI login expiry). Both
 83:   produced fail-soft recorded rows, were pruned, and re-ran cleanly; the
 84:   resume path (skip-by-original_sha) worked as designed. Neither affects
 85:   judged data: every kept row has a real rewrite and full panel coverage.
 86: - The new over-editing guard fired on exactly the human controls whose
 87:   deterministic signal was already clean (7 warnings, 0 on AI docs) — its
 88:   first live validation, consistent with its design.
 89: - One S2-specific confound stands as registered: rewriter/judges are
 90:   stochastic and S1 ran two days earlier. The paired design absorbs
 91:   document-level variance but not run-level drift; given d ≈ 0 with a tight
 92:   CI, the conclusion "no effect distinguishable from rerun variance" is the
 93:   honest reading — and the guard-rail violations are same-run internal
 94:   comparisons (gate) or CI-excluding-zero (human d), so they do not hinge on
 95:   the confound.
 96: 
 97: ## What ships
 98: 
 99: Per the pre-registered decision rule: the results are published as-is; the
100: pack carries **no efficacy claim**; `ko-doc-structure` is **removed from the
101: pro-pack manifest** (unpublished, kept in the repo history for iteration 2);
102: the next intervention targets the structural mechanism, not the pattern prose.
````

## File: docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy-study3.md
````markdown
