````markdown
  1: # Does a structure-plan step fix the Korean gap? — Study 3 results
  2: 
  3: Companion to `2026-rewrite-efficacy-prereg.md` ("Study 3" section, registered
  4: 2026-07-12 before any data), Study 1 (`2026-rewrite-efficacy-study1.md`), and
  5: Study 2 (`2026-rewrite-efficacy-study2.md`). Study 2 rejected the prompt-text
  6: mechanism; Study 3 tested its named successor — a two-stage **plan → execute**
  7: rewrite in which the model first emits a reorganization plan under a
  8: KEEP-verbatim number/entity contract, then carries it out.
  9: 
 10: **Verdict up front: the plan-step mechanism fails every pre-registered
 11: criterion, and is measurably WORSE than the plain single-pass rewrite. It
 12: must not ship in any form; the next iteration falls to candidate 2
 13: (deterministic structure transforms with LLM infill), pending priority
 14: reassessment.**
 15: 
 16: - Run: 2026-07-12 → 07-13 (18 supervisor passes across three claude
 17:   session-limit windows). Same corpus (54 Study 1 Arm-D documents), rewriter
 18:   model claude-sonnet-4-6 invoked exactly as patina's claude-cli backend, same
 19:   fixed 3-judge panel (kimi / gpt-5.5 / grok-4.5, 2-of-3 quorum). Stage
 20:   prompts fixed before the first row (sha `5c43a5f5…` / `d8dc4497…`, stamped
 21:   in every row); plans recorded per row as auditable intermediates.
 22: - Final matrix: **53/54 documents** (27 AI + 26 human), 0 unparseable judge
 23:   cells. One human document (`toss-front-2`) is data loss: three consecutive
 24:   900 s execute-stage timeouts (see execution notes).
 25: 
 26: ## H-S3a (primary) — paired improvement on AI documents: **REJECTED (direction reversed)**
 27: 
 28: Baseline = each document's own Study 1 panel scores.
 29: 
 30: | | orig | rewrite (S1, plain) | rewrite (S3, plan-step) | paired d = rw3−rw1 (95% CI) |
 31: |---|---:|---:|---:|---|
 32: | AI docs (n=27) | 83.6 | 77.6 | **80.0** | **+2.4 [−0.3, +4.9]** |
 33: 
 34: Pre-set support required CI excluding 0 below AND d ≤ −5. Instead the point
 35: estimate is **positive**: the plan-step rewrite reads MORE AI-like to the
 36: panel than the plain rewrite it was supposed to beat (descriptive vs Study 2's
 37: pack rewrite: +3.1). AI-call rate 93% → 94%. The judged-credibility ground for
 38: these scores is the calibration side study (`2026-judge-calibration.md`:
 39: panel AUC 0.96, per-judge repeat SD 2–5).
 40: 
 41: ## H-S3b — structural-cue share: **REJECTED**
 42: 
 43: Among judgments still calling the S3 rewrite "ai": **75% structural cues**
 44: (S1: 81%, S2: 82%; pre-registered target < 60%). The plan step nudged the
 45: number three points — the judges' complaints remain uniform paragraphs,
 46: tidy arcs, checklist coverage. Planning the reorganization did not produce
 47: perceived reorganization.
 48: 
 49: ## Guard rails — **both violated (same directions as Study 2, now stronger)**
 50: 
 51: 1. **Meaning-safety gate (pre-set ≥ 95%, ≥ 52/54):** 49/53 pass (92.5%).
 52:    Four dropped-numbers failures — one is the known corpus-artifact document
 53:    (generator length-preamble numbers), three are real content numbers lost
 54:    despite the plan explicitly enumerating KEEP-verbatim strings. **The
 55:    KEEP-verbatim contract does not survive execution**: the model writes a
 56:    compliant plan, then loses the numbers anyway while reorganizing.
 57: 2. **Human-control over-editing:** paired d = **+3.6 [+0.1, +9… 7.5]** — the
 58:    plan-step rewrite pushed human documents' AI-likeness back UP to original
 59:    levels (42.6 → 46.1 vs original 46.2), erasing the plain rewrite's small
 60:    gain; CI excludes 0. AI-call on human docs 23% → 27%. Structure-level
 61:    editing harms human text — the third study in a row to measure this
 62:    failure mode.
 63: 
 64: Mean rewrite/original length ratio: 0.93 (AI), 0.86 (human) — the compression
 65: pressure Study 2 flagged persists and is stronger on human docs.
 66: 
 67: ## Why it failed (diagnosis)
 68: 
 69: Study 2 showed *describing* target architecture doesn't change it. Study 3
 70: shows *planning* it doesn't either: the model produces plausible
 71: merge/split/reorder plans, but execution collapses back into
 72: paragraph-by-paragraph paraphrase — now with extra degrees of freedom that
 73: cost meaning (dropped numbers), cost length (0.86–0.93), and cost perception
 74: (+2.4). The failure is in the **execute step's fidelity to a structural
 75: plan**, not in plan quality. Two registered interventions have now failed in
 76: complementary ways; the honest conclusion is that current-generation LLM
 77: rewriting cannot be prompted or planned into document-architecture
 78: humanization. Candidate 2 (deterministic structure transforms — section
 79: merge/split scaffolding — with LLM infill confined to seams) is the remaining
 80: mechanism, and its cost/benefit should be reassessed against the measured
 81: ceiling before any build.
 82: 
 83: ## Execution notes
 84: 
 85: - Two stage-timeout raises were made mid-run as execution notes (never
 86:   touching prompts or criteria): execute 600 s → 900 s after two distinct
 87:   documents hit the wall (PR #634), following S1's 300→600 precedent. One
 88:   document (`toss-front-2`, 3.9k chars) timed out at 900 s three consecutive
 89:   times and is recorded as data loss rather than raising further.
 90: - The run crossed three claude session-limit windows; the supervisor's
 91:   prune-and-resume loop (18 passes) preserved every clean row. A host tmux
 92:   crash mid-run did not affect the detached runner.
 93: - Corpus artifact (known from the smoke row): 2/54 stored S1 originals carry
 94:   generator length-preamble meta-text; one of them accounts for one of the
 95:   four gate failures (same document failed S2's gate identically). Removing
 96:   it from the count (48/52 = 92.3%) does not change the guard-rail verdict.
 97: - Judge panel context: this is the last study on the kimi-inclusive panel
 98:   (subscription ends 07-17); panel v2 (`2026-panel-v2-design.md`) governs
 99:   subsequent studies.
100: 
101: ## What ships
102: 
103: Per the pre-registered decision rule: nothing. The results are published
104: as-is; no efficacy claim attaches to any structure-rewrite surface; the
105: plan-step mechanism is not productized. The next candidate (deterministic
106: transforms + LLM infill) is **not** started — Study 2's follow-up options are
107: held for operator priority reassessment, as directed.
````

## File: docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy.md
````markdown
  1: # Does patina rewrite reduce AI-likeness? — Study 0 (pilot) results
  2: 
  3: Companion to the pre-registration `2026-rewrite-efficacy-prereg.md`, which fixed
  4: these hypotheses, metrics, and decision rules **before** any data existed. Read
  5: that file's four dated deviations alongside this one — the pilot's main product
  6: turned out to be a hardened measurement instrument, not an effect size.
  7: 
  8: - Run: 2026-07-10. Rewriter: `claude-cli`. Independent judge panel:
  9:   `gemini` + `kimi` (Moonshot), both cross-family to the rewriter.
 10: - Raw generations and per-passage judgments are gitignored under
 11:   `artifacts/rewrite-efficacy-pilot/`; only this synthesis is committed.
 12: - **This is a pilot (n≈7–8 per cell).** Its job is to validate the instrument
 13:   and size variance for a main study — not to license a headline number. Every
 14:   interval below is wide; treat directions, not magnitudes, as the finding.
 15: 
 16: ## Design recap
 17: 
 18: Three arms, so stimulus length is a measured moderator instead of a confound
 19: (pre-reg Deviation 1):
 20: 
 21: | Arm | Language | Stimulus | Corpus |
 22: |---|---|---|---|
 23: | A | English | document (~3 000 ch) | HAP-E, human/AI paired on `prompt_id` |
 24: | B | English | same items truncated (~450 ch) | HAP-E |
 25: | C | Korean | snippet (~170 ch) | rebaseline intake + register-matched controls |
 26: 
 27: `spok` (HAP-E's degraded-ASR register) is excluded from the primary analysis and
 28: restored in a sensitivity check (Deviation 4).
 29: 
 30: ## RQ1 — is "AI-likeness" a stable construct? **PASS**
 31: 
 32: Inter-judge agreement (Krippendorff's α, interval):
 33: 
 34: | Arm | stimulus | α | Spearman ρ |
 35: |---|---|---:|---:|
 36: | A | document | **0.82** | 0.87 |
 37: | B | snippet (same docs) | 0.67 | 0.78 |
 38: | C | Korean snippet | 0.86 | 0.76 |
 39: 
 40: All well above the pre-registered α ≥ 0.4 gate, so the efficacy estimates are
 41: interpretable and the main study is cleared to proceed.
 42: 
 43: **The length moderator paid off.** Same documents, document-length vs
 44: truncated-to-450-chars: α falls 0.82 → 0.67. Shorter stimulus = noisier
 45: judgment, exactly as the smoke test's chaos (α ≈ 0.02 on 95–150-char Korean
 46: snippets) first suggested. But Arm C's Korean 170-char snippets still reach
 47: α 0.86 — so the smoke test's failure was **extreme** shortness plus a
 48: register-mismatched control set, not snippet length per se. That distinction only
 49: exists because Deviation 1 turned the flaw into a factor.
 50: 
 51: ## RQ2 — does rewrite reduce *perceived* AI-likeness? **Directionally yes, everywhere**
 52: 
 53: AI-likeness is 0–100; negative Δ = reads less AI-like after rewrite.
 54: 
 55: | Arm | class | n | judge before → after | Δ (95% CI) | Cliff's δ | "AI"-call rate |
 56: |---|---|---:|---|---|---:|---|
 57: | A | AI | 7 | 70.5 → 56.3 | −14.2 [−24.5, −5.7] | −0.43 | 77% → 62% |
 58: | B | AI | 7 | 66.1 → 43.9 | −22.1 [−35.2, −9.4] | −0.63 | 71% → 46% |
 59: | C | AI | 8 | 76.5 → 58.9 | −17.6 [−32.3, −6.4] | −0.73 | 88% → 69% |
 60: 
 61: **H2a is supported in all three arms** — every CI lies entirely below zero. The
 62: rewrite reliably moves AI text toward "human" on judges that never saw patina and
 63: score each passage blind.
 64: 
 65: But two cautions sit right next to that:
 66: 
 67: 1. **The effect shrinks with stimulus length.** At document length (Arm A) the
 68:    drop is smallest (−14) and the AI-call rate stays a majority (62%). A reader
 69:    asked "human or AI?" still says AI most of the time after rewriting a full
 70:    document. The rewrite reduces AI-likeness; it does not, at this size, cross
 71:    the line into "reads human."
 72: 2. **H2b (AI-call rate halved) is not met.** No arm halves the rate; the best is
 73:    C's 88 → 69%. The signal is a shift in degree, not a flip in classification.
 74: 
 75: ## Anti-circularity — is patina just gaming its own detector? **No**
 76: 
 77: The pre-registered failure mode: internal score craters while independent judges
 78: barely move. Observed (AI texts, per arm):
 79: 
 80: | Arm | independent-judge Δ | patina internal Δ |
 81: |---|---:|---:|
 82: | A | −14.2 | −28.5 |
 83: | B | −22.1 | −26.3 |
 84: | C | −17.6 | −16.0 |
 85: 
 86: The internal drop and the independent-judge drop move together — largest gap in
 87: Arm A, but never the decoupling the rule was written to catch. **The gains are
 88: real to outside raters, not an artifact of optimizing the home detector.** (Arm A
 89: carries the widest internal-vs-judge gap, worth watching at scale.)
 90: 
 91: ## RQ5 — meaning and collateral
 92: 
 93: - **RQ5a (meaning preserved): met.** The persona safety gate (MPS / fidelity /
 94:   dropped numbers) passed on 44/44 rewrites once the `spok` word-salad was
 95:   removed — and the harness only counts this honestly because a mid-pilot fix
 96:   stopped it from silently discarding gate-failing rewrites.
 97: - **RQ5b (over-editing human prose): mostly clean, one flag.** Rewriting human
 98:   controls barely moved their AI-likeness in A (−2.9) and C (−4.1) and never
 99:   raised a human text's AI-call rate. Arm B is the exception: human Δ −13.4 — but
100:   the humans there *dropped* toward more-human, and Arm B's human "before" score
101:   was oddly high (26.8), likely the truncation reading as abruptness. Worth a
102:   look at scale; not an over-editing alarm yet.
103: 
104: ## RQ4 — does patina leave its own house style? **No signal (underpowered)**
105: 
106: Rewrites cluster slightly tighter in style space than human controls (cohesion
107: 0.96 vs 0.91), but the permutation test is not significant (p = 0.078, n = 8).
108: Suggestive of a mild convergent voice, nowhere near conclusive at pilot size.
109: Flagged as a real question for the main study, not a finding.
110: 
111: ## Surviving cues (what still reads as AI after rewrite)
112: 
113: Judges' free-text "strongest cue" on rewritten AI text clustered on **structure**,
114: not vocabulary: "formulaic thematic structure," "generic corporate-essay
115: structure with topic-sentence paragraphs and abstract-noun stacking," "neatly
116: resolved thematic ending." Meanwhile the cues they named on *human* text were all
117: **specificity**: named institutions, idiosyncratic anecdotes, character-driven
118: dialogue. This is the most actionable pilot output: patina is scrubbing lexical
119: tells (the internal score falls hard) but leaving the **architectural** ones —
120: the tidy intro-body-resolution arc — largely intact. That points pattern-pack
121: work toward structure, and matches why the effect is weakest on long documents,
122: where structure has the most room to show.
123: 
124: ## Verdict
125: 
126: Against the pre-registered criteria: **RQ1 passes**, **RQ2/H2a is supported in
127: all arms**, the **anti-circularity rule does not trigger**, and **H5a is met** —
128: but **H2b (AI-call rate halved) fails**, and the effect **shrinks as text
129: lengthens**. So, honestly stated: on this pilot, patina makes AI text *read
130: meaningfully less AI-like to independent judges while preserving meaning*, and
131: the gain is not a home-detector artifact — but it *does not yet make
132: document-length AI text pass as human*, and the residual tells are structural.
133: 
134: ## What the pilot changed (instrument, not just estimate)
135: 
136: The pilot's durable output is a measurement rig that survived contact with reality
137: and four recorded deviations:
138: 1. corpus substrate swapped to document-length paired HAP-E after snippets proved
139:    unjudgeable (Dev 1);
140: 2. judge parser hardened against schema drift + silent loss (Dev 2);
141: 3. panel moved to gemini+kimi after codex exhausted its quota mid-run, with the
142:    partial codex ratings kept as a separate third rater (Dev 3);
143: 4. non-prose register excluded with a paired sensitivity analysis (Dev 4).
144: 
145: It also surfaced a patina bug unrelated to the numbers: exit code 4 (persona
146: safety gate) was undocumented, so tooling that treats non-zero as failure would
147: silently drop exactly the meaning-drifted rewrites (fixed; PR #609).
148: 
149: ## Go / no-go for the main study
150: 
151: **Go**, with these conditions carried from the pilot:
152: - fix the judge panel up front and verify quota headroom before the first call
153:   (codex died mid-pilot; two texts deterministically hang gemini — the main study
154:   needs a 3-judge panel with a 2-of-3 quorum so one backend stalling can't void a
155:   cell);
156: - power: pilot AI-text Δ SDs imply ~20–30 items per cell for a stable estimate;
157: - **a document-length Korean corpus does not exist and is the blocking
158:   prerequisite** for any Korean claim at the length where the effect is weakest;
159: - report structural-tell survival (RQ3/cues) as a primary outcome, since that is
160:   where the pilot says the humanization actually falls short.
````

## File: patterns/en-language.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: en-language
  3: language: en
  4: name: Language Patterns
  5: version: 1.1.0
  6: patterns: 9
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: dedupe-with:
 13:   - source: "en-language:7"
 14:     target: "en-filler:24"
 15:     owner: "en-filler:24"
 16:     reason: "The phrase 'stands as a testament' belongs to filler #24; language #7 keeps 'testament' only as one word in a broader AI-vocabulary cluster."
 17:   - source: "en-language:7"
 18:     target: "en-language:8"
 19:     owner: "en-language:8"
 20:     reason: "When 'testament' appears inside a copula-avoidance construction, pattern #8 owns the rewrite; pattern #7 only fires on a broader AI-vocabulary cluster."
 21:   - source: "en-language:8"
 22:     target: "en-filler:24"
 23:     owner: "en-filler:24"
 24:     reason: "The exact phrase 'stands as a testament' is a generic positive-conclusion marker when used as vague praise; pattern #8 still owns non-conclusion copula avoidance such as 'serves as'."
 25: ---
 26: 
 27: # Language Patterns
 28: 
 29: ### 7. AI Vocabulary Words
 30: 
 31: **Watch words:** delve, tapestry, landscape, multifaceted, comprehensive, pivotal, testament, intricate, nuanced, leverage, foster, crucial, moreover, furthermore, realm, robust, facilitate, endeavor, resonate, underscore, embark, myriad, paramount, encompass, holistic, synergy, elucidate, culminate, juxtapose, burgeoning
 32: 
 33: **Fire condition:** 3+ watch words appear in one paragraph.
 34: 
 35: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 36: **Preservation Note:** Replacing AI vocabulary words changes the specific connotations carried by words like "nuanced" or "multifaceted"; ensure the substituted terms preserve the intended meaning and register.
 37: 
 38: **Problem:** These words appear at dramatically higher rates in AI-generated text than in human writing. Individually they are normal English words; clustered together they are a telltale fingerprint.
 39: 
 40: **Before:**
 41: > This comprehensive report delves into the multifaceted landscape of renewable energy, leveraging nuanced insights to foster a robust understanding. Moreover, the intricate tapestry of stakeholder interests underscores the pivotal role of policy in this crucial endeavor.
 42: 
 43: **After:**
 44: > This report covers renewable energy policy from three angles: cost, grid reliability, and public opinion. Each section uses data from the EIA and interviews with six state utility commissioners.
 45: 
 46: ---
 47: 
 48: ### 8. Copula Avoidance ("serves as")
 49: 
 50: **Watch words:** serves as, acts as, functions as, stands as, operates as, works as, remains as, exists as
 51: 
 52: **Fire condition:** 2+ copula-avoidance constructions in the same paragraph, or a single instance where "is" would be shorter and clearer.
 53: 
 54: **Exclusion:** "Serves as" is acceptable when the subject has a formally designated role function (e.g., "the committee serves as an advisory board" — institutional role, not decorative usage).
 55: 
 56: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 57: **Preservation Note:** "Serves as" sometimes implies a functional or designated role distinct from mere identity; replacing with "is" may flatten a meaningful distinction about purpose or function.
 58: 
 59: **Problem:** AI avoids the simple verb "is" by using unnecessarily complex copula constructions. "The park serves as a gathering place" instead of "The park is a gathering place."
 60: 
 61: **Before:**
 62: > The library serves as a vital community hub, functioning as both an educational resource and a social gathering space. It also acts as a testament to the city's commitment to public access.
 63: 
 64: **After:**
 65: > The library is the neighborhood's main public space. People use it for study groups, ESL classes, and the Saturday morning story hour, which draws about 40 kids a week.
 66: 
 67: ---
 68: 
 69: ### 9. Negative Parallelisms
 70: 
 71: **Watch words:** not just...but, not merely...but also, not only...but, goes beyond...to, more than just...it is, transcends...to become, serial negation ("It wasn't X. It wasn't Y. It was Z."), "Not a X. Not a Y. A Z."
 72: 
 73: **Fire condition:** 2+ "not X but Y" structures in the same document, or a single instance where the positive statement alone would be simpler and equally clear. Also fires on a single serial-negation build-up (two or more consecutive "it wasn't/is not X" sentences resolving into "it was/is Z") — the negations are a runway, not content.
 74: 
 75: **Exclusion:** Genuine contrastive clarification correcting a misconception ("the event is not a conference but a workshop — no keynotes, only hands-on sessions") — the negative frame is doing real work here.
 76: 
 77: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 78: **Preservation Note:** The negative framing sometimes carries a genuine contrast or correction; collapsing "not X but Y" to just "Y" may lose the implicit rejection of a common assumption the author intended to address.
 79: 
 80: **Problem:** AI uses negative-then-positive constructions to make simple points sound profound. Instead of stating what something is, it first states what it is not.
 81: 
 82: **Before:**
 83: > This initiative is not just a policy change but a fundamental reimagining of urban planning. It goes beyond mere infrastructure investment to become a statement about the kind of city residents want to live in.
 84: 
 85: **After:**
 86: > The initiative rezones 12 blocks for mixed-use development and adds a bike lane network connecting the east and west sides of the river.
 87: 
 88: ---
 89: 
 90: ### 10. Rule of Three Overuse
 91: 
 92: **Fire condition:** 3-item lists appear 2+ times in the same document, or a triple-part sentence where the count is arbitrary and another count would be equally valid.
 93: 
 94: **Exclusion:** Naturally occurring triads in genuinely three-part processes (past/present/future; input/process/output; beginning/middle/end) are not this pattern.
 95: 
 96: **Burstiness note:** When rewriting, use the naturally correct count — one strong point if that is all there is, two contrasting items if that is the real structure, four if there are actually four. Varying list counts across a document signals human authorship.
 97: 
 98: **Problem:** AI defaults to triple-item lists and three-part structures when the natural count might be two, four, or one. This creates a rhythm that feels rehearsed rather than spontaneous.
 99: 
100: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
101: 
102: **Before:**
103: > The program fosters creativity, innovation, and collaboration. Participants gain inspiration, practical skills, and lasting connections. The result is a more dynamic, inclusive, and forward-thinking community.
104: 
105: **After:**
106: > The program pairs early-career designers with experienced mentors for a 10-week project. Last year's cohort produced two apps that are still in active use.
107: 
108: ---
109: 
110: ### 11. Elegant Variation (Synonym Cycling)
111: 
112: **Fire condition:** The same entity referred to by 3+ different names or synonyms within a single paragraph.
113: 
114: **Exclusion:** Legitimate disambiguation (using "the company" vs. "Microsoft" when distinguishing a parent from a subsidiary, or "the study" vs. "the 2023 Stanford paper" for precision) is not this pattern.
115: 
116: **Problem:** AI rotates synonyms for the same entity to avoid repeating a word, producing text that feels strangely evasive. A city becomes "the metropolis," then "the urban center," then "the municipality" — all within the same paragraph.
117: 
118: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
119: 
120: **Before:**
121: > Tokyo is the most populous city in Japan. The metropolis is known for its blend of tradition and modernity. The urban center attracts millions of tourists each year. The Japanese capital continues to grow.
122: 
123: **After:**
124: > Tokyo is the most populous city in Japan, with about 14 million residents. It draws roughly 20 million foreign tourists a year, a number that has doubled since 2015.
125: 
126: ---
127: 
128: ### 12. False Ranges
129: 
130: **Watch words:** from X to Y, ranging from...to, spanning...to, everything from...to, whether...or
131: 
132: **Fire condition:** A "from X to Y" construction appears where the two poles do not meaningfully bound a spectrum — the range is decorative rather than informative.
133: 
134: **Exclusion:** Genuine numeric or temporal ranges ("from 10 to 100 employees", "from January to March", "from $5 to $50") are not this pattern.
135: 
136: **Problem:** AI creates artificial ranges to sound comprehensive, often pairing two extremes that do not meaningfully define a spectrum.
137: 
138: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
139: **Preservation Note:** Removing a range construction may drop a genuine scope claim about audience or coverage; verify whether the range was intended to convey actual breadth before eliminating it.
140: 
141: **Before:**
142: > The festival offers something for everyone, from young children to seasoned professionals, spanning everything from traditional folk music to cutting-edge electronic performances.
143: 
144: **After:**
145: > The festival has three stages. The main stage books established acts (this year: Yo-Yo Ma, Thundercat). The tent stage is for local bands. There is also a children's area with instrument demos.
146: 
147: ---
148: 
149: ### 32. Comparison Adverb Overuse ("more" without target)
150: 
151: **Watch words:** more specific, more concrete, more efficient, more effective, more comprehensive, more robust, more seamless, more meaningful, more strategic, more impactful, more nuanced, more proactive, more sustainable, more scalable
152: 
153: **Fire condition:** 2+ "more + adjective/adverb" comparative phrases appear in one document without a clear target, baseline, or metric. A single instance can fire when it appears in the same paragraph as other formal AI markers such as "comprehensive", "strategic", "framework", "stakeholder", or "in-depth".
154: 
155: **Exclusion:** Do not fire when the comparison target is explicit ("more efficient than the old process"), when a before/after metric is provided ("reduced latency from 240 ms to 130 ms"), in ordinary quantifiers ("more than 10 users"), or in fixed phrases such as "more or less".
156: 
157: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
158: **Preservation Note:** Comparative language can carry a real improvement claim. Preserve the target, metric, or priority if one exists; otherwise replace the vague comparative with the concrete requirement or result the sentence is trying to name.
159: 
160: **Problem:** AI text often implies progress by stacking "more X" phrases while never saying compared with what. Human prose either names the baseline ("faster than last quarter's build") or drops the decorative comparison and states the concrete change.
161: 
162: **Burstiness note:** Do not convert every "more X" phrase into the same alternative. Mix direct adjectives, named metrics, shorter verbs, and one retained comparative when the contrast is real.
163: 
164: **Before:**
165: > The initiative will enable more specific milestones, more efficient resource allocation, and more comprehensive stakeholder alignment. Moving forward, the team should develop a more strategic framework for more meaningful collaboration.
166: 
167: **After:**
168: > The initiative needs dated milestones, named owners, and a budget review. The team also needs one shared plan for partner handoffs, because the current spreadsheet has three conflicting owners for the same launch tasks.
169: 
170: ---
171: 
172: ### 33. Definitional-Metaphor Equation ("X is the architecture of Z")
173: 
174: **Watch words:** is the signature of, is the shape of, is the language of, is the currency of, is the architecture of, is the backbone of, is the engine of, is the heartbeat of, is the DNA of, is the cornerstone of, is the lifeblood of
175: 
176: **Fire condition:** 2+ copula sentences of the form "X is the [abstract noun] of Z" appear in the same document/section without concrete support for the equation. A single instance is an audit hint only: do not rewrite unless the same inflated metaphor pattern recurs.
177: 
178: **Exclusion:** Literal or technical definitions ("water is the universal solvent"), established idioms and textbook metaphors ("the mitochondria is the powerhouse of the cell"), factual "X is the capital/center of Z" statements, and genuine equivalences backed by concrete support in the same passage are not this pattern.
179: 
180: **Disambiguation from #8 (Copula Avoidance):** #8 fires on text that *avoids* "is" ("serves as", "functions as" → rewrite to "is"). #33 is the opposite: the sentence already uses "is", but inflates it into an "is the [abstract noun] of [abstraction]" metaphor-equation to manufacture profundity. Do not conflate them — #8 wants the copula restored, #33 wants the empty metaphor-equation dismantled.
181: 
182: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
183: **Preservation Note:** The metaphor sometimes points at a real claim ("trust depends on consistent behavior"); when rewriting, recover and state that underlying claim concretely rather than deleting it, so a genuine point is not lost along with the inflated framing.
184: 
185: **Problem:** AI manufactures depth by equating one abstraction with another through a borrowed structural noun — "signature", "architecture", "currency". The sentence sounds like an insight but asserts nothing testable; swapping the abstract noun ("is the *language* of" → "is the *currency* of") barely changes the meaning, which exposes the equation as decorative rather than substantive.
186: 
187: **Before:**
188: > Symmetry is the architecture of trust. Cringe is the visible signature of moving along a gradient you chose. Consistency is the currency of every relationship that lasts.
189: 
190: **After:**
191: > People trust a process more when it behaves the same way every time, so they can predict it. That predictability is what makes a relationship hold up: you keep doing what you said you would, and the other side stops bracing for surprises.
192: 
193: ### 34. False Agency (Inanimate Actors)
194: 
195: **Watch words:** the data tells us, the numbers speak, the decision emerged, the culture shifted, the conversation moved toward, the market rewards, the market decides, the complaint becomes, the strategy demands, this unlocks, the moment calls for, the work speaks for itself
196: 
197: **Fire condition:** 2+ sentences in the same document where an inanimate subject performs a human action (deciding, telling, rewarding, demanding, moving a discussion) *and* the human actor is recoverable from context but never named. A single instance is an audit hint only.
198: 
199: **Exclusion:**
200: - Academic/report citation conventions ("the study finds", "the paper argues", "the data show a correlation") — standard metonymy for the authors.
201: - Genuine agentless processes ("the temperature rose", "the deadline passed", "the market crashed" as an aggregate event).
202: - Deliberate literary personification sustained as a device, and established idioms.
203: - Software/system subjects doing what software actually does ("the linter flags", "the pipeline retries").
204: 
205: **Disambiguation from #26/#27 (Passive/Nominalization):** passive voice hides the actor by grammar ("the decision was made"); false agency hides the actor by promoting an object to actor ("the decision emerged"). Both leave the same question — *who did it* — but this pattern fires on active-voice sentences, which passive detectors miss.
206: 
207: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
208: **Preservation Note:** Never invent an actor the source does not support. If the text implies the actor (a team, the author, "you"), name that; if no actor is recoverable, restructure around what is actually known ("someone on the team killed the project" → only if the source says so; otherwise "the project was killed within days" may have to survive as-is). Fabricating agency is a meaning violation, not a style fix.
209: 
210: **Problem:** AI systematically promotes abstractions into actors — decisions emerge, cultures shift, data tells, markets reward — because it lets the prose sound analytical while never committing to who did what. Human writing tends to name the person, or at least stand somewhere ("we", "you", "the team"). The tell is not one sentence but the accumulation: a paragraph where every event happens with no human in it.
211: 
212: **Before:**
213: > The feedback loop rewarded speed over quality, so the culture shifted within a quarter. Decisions emerged from Slack threads rather than meetings, and the roadmap bent toward whatever the metrics demanded.
214: 
215: **After:**
216: > Leadership paid out bonuses on shipping speed, not defect counts, so within a quarter engineers stopped polishing. People started making calls in Slack threads instead of meetings, and the PMs rewrote the roadmap around whatever number the dashboard showed that week.
````

````markdown

 17:   - source: "ja-language:7"

 21:   - source: "ja-language:7"

 25:   - source: "ja-language:8"
 26:     target: "ja-language:12"

 29: ---
 30: 
 31: # 言語・文法パターン
 32: 
 33: ### 7. AI特有の語彙の多用
 34: 
 35: **高頻度語彙：** 多様な、活発な、革新的な、画期的な、体系的な、持続的な、効果的な、包括的な、先進的な、有機的な、総合的な、主導的な、さらに、加えて、これにより、こうした中、推進する、促進する、最大化する
 36: 
 37: **問題：** これらの語彙は2023年以降のテキストで著しく高い頻度で出現する。複数がまとまって使われることが多い。
 38: 
 39: **発火条件：** 同一段落に高頻度語彙が3つ以上出現する場合。
 40: 
 41: **除外条件：**
 42: - 個々の単語が単独で1-2回使われている場合（通常の日本語でも出現する語彙のため）
 43: - allowlistに含まれる表現
 44: 
 45: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 46: **Preservation Note:** AI特有の語彙を言い換える際に、その語彙が示す具体的な属性や程度まで失わないよう注意
 47: 
 48: **修正前：**
 49: > さらに、多様な革新的技術が活発に開発されており、これにより体系的かつ効果的なソリューションを提供している。加えて、こうした持続的な取り組みは主導的な役割を果たしている。
 50: 
 51: **修正後：**
 52: > 今年出た技術の中では、バッテリー寿命を20%延ばした電力管理チップと、処理速度が従来の2倍になったNPUがある。
 53: 
 54: ---
 55: 
 56: ### 8. 〜的（てき）接尾辞の多用
 57: 
 58: **注意語彙：** 革新的、体系的、効果的、効率的、先進的、積極的、総合的、核心的、戦略的、実質的、根本的、画期的、抜本的
 59: 
 60: **問題：** 日本語AIの最も顕著なパターンの一つ。「〜的」漢語形容詞を一文に複数積み重ねる。韓国語の~적(jeok)パターンと並行。
 61: 
 62: **発火条件：** 一文に「〜的」形容詞が3つ以上、または連続2文にわたって4つ以上使用された場合。
 63: 
 64: **除外条件：**
 65: - 学術論文・報告書で定義上必要な専門用語（「体系的レビュー」「実効的税率」）
 66: - 単独1つの使用は通常の日本語なので補正対象外
 67: 
 68: **Burstiness指針：** 「〜的」形容詞を修正する際、すべてを機械的に言い換えない。一部は具体的記述に変え（「革新的な」→「従来と異なる」）、一部は削除し、一部は他の品詞に転換して文のリズムを変える。
 69: 
 70: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 71: **Preservation Note:** 「〜的」形容詞を置き換える際に、それが示す性質や程度の意味が希薄にならないよう注意
 72: 
 73: **修正前：**
 74: > 革新的かつ体系的なアプローチにより、効果的かつ効率的な成果を導き、先進的かつ積極的な姿勢で総合的な発展を推進している。
 75: 
 76: **修正後：**
 77: > チームは既存のプロセスを単純化し、不要な承認ステップを省いて、デプロイ時間を半分に短縮した。
 78: 
 79: ---
 80: 
 81: ### 9. 否定並列構造
 82: 
 83: **注意語彙：** 〜にとどまらず、〜のみならず〜も、単に〜だけでなく、〜を超えて
 84: 
 85: **問題：** 「単にAではなくBである」「AだけでなくBも」構造を過度に使用する。
 86: 
 87: **発火条件：** 同一文書に否定並列構造が2回以上出現、または肯定文だけでより簡潔に表現できる箇所に使用された場合。
 88: 
 89: **除外条件：**
 90: - 誤解を正すための実質的な対比（「このイベントはカンファレンスではなくワークショップだ――基調講演なし、実習のみ」）
 91: - 二つの要素の対照が論理的に不可欠な場合
 92: 
 93: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 94: **Preservation Note:** 否定並列構造を単純化する際に、否定によって示されていた対比関係や範囲の拡張が失われないよう注意
 95: 
 96: **修正前：**
 97: > これは単なる技術革新にとどまらず、社会全体にわたる根本的な変革を意味する。これは経済的側面のみならず、社会的・文化的側面においても重大な意味を持つ。
 98: 
 99: **修正後：**
100: > この技術は最初に製造工程に導入され、その後物流と顧客サービスにも使われ始めた。
101: 
102: ---
103: 
104: ### 10. 三の法則の多用
105: 
106: **問題：** AIは包括的に見せるため、アイデアを必ず3つにまとめたがる。
107: 
108: **発火条件：** 同一文書に3項目の列挙が2回以上出現、または3つにまとめた根拠が恣意的で他の数でも同等に成り立つ場合。
109: 
110: **除外条件：**
111: - 本質的に三要素の構造（過去/現在/未来、入力/処理/出力、序論/本論/結論）
112: - 実際に3つある項目を列挙する場合
113: 
114: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
115: **Preservation Note:** 3項目列挙を削減する際に、実質的に異なる意味を持つ項目まで省略しないよう注意
116: 
117: **修正前：**
118: > このプログラムは創造性、革新性、そして持続可能性を追求します。参加者はインスピレーション、実践的スキル、そして持続的なつながりを得ることができます。
119: 
120: **修正後：**
121: > このプログラムは実務プロジェクト中心で、参加者は8週間かけて実際の製品開発に取り組む。昨年のコホートが作った2つのアプリは今も使われている。
122: 
123: ---
124: 
125: ### 11. 類義語の循環
126: 
127: **問題：** AIは繰り返しを避けるため、同じ対象を次々と別の言い方で呼ぶ。
128: 
129: **発火条件：** 同一段落で同一対象が3つ以上の異なる名称・同義語で指称された場合。
130: 
131: **除外条件：**
132: - 意図的な区別（例：「トヨタ自動車」と「トヨタグループ」で親会社・子会社を区別）
133: - 精度の異なるバリエーション（「この研究」と「2023年の東大の論文」のような具体化）
134: 
135: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
136: **Preservation Note:** 同じ対象の呼称を統一する前に、本当に同一対象か確認する。親会社・子会社、組織・製品、役職の違いがある場合は区別を残す。
137: 
138: **修正前：**
139: > 東京は日本最大の都市である。この大都市は伝統と現代の融合で知られる。同都市圏には多くの観光客が訪れる。日本の首都は今も成長を続けている。
140: 
141: **修正後：**
142: > 東京の人口は約1,400万人で、日本最大の都市だ。外国人観光客は年間約2,000万人で、2015年から倍増した。
143: 
144: ---
145: 
146: ### 12. カタカナ外来語の多用
147: 
148: **注意語彙：** イノベーション、ソリューション、パフォーマンス、ガバナンス、コンセンサス、シナジー、モメンタム、マイルストーン、トリガー、スケールアップ、オンボーディング、フィードバックループ、ペインポイント、ディシジョンメイキング、エコシステム、レジリエンス、サステナビリティ
149: 
150: **問題：** AIは日本語の代替があるのにカタカナ外来語を多用する。専門用語として定着した外来語（コンピュータ、インターネット等）は問題ないが、日本語で十分表現できるものまで外来語にする。特にビジネス・経営の文脈でひどい。
151: 
152: **発火条件：** 同一段落に日本語の代替がある外来語が3つ以上出現する場合。業界で定着した専門用語（マーケティング、ブランディング等）は除外。
153: 
154: **除外条件：**
155: - IT・開発の専門用語として定着した外来語（API、サーバー、クラウド等）
156: - 固有名詞またはプロダクト名
157: - 日本語代替がかえって不自然な場合
158: 
159: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
160: **Preservation Note:** カタカナ語を日本語に置き換える際に、元の語が持つ特定のニュアンスや概念の範囲が変わらないよう注意
161: 
162: **修正前：**
163: > 今回のプロジェクトのインサイトをレバレッジし、シナジーを最大化して、サステナブルなモメンタムを確保することがキーマイルストーンだ。チームのパフォーマンスを向上させるため、オンボーディングプロセスを改善し、ペインポイントを解決するソリューションを導入する必要がある。
164: 
165: **修正後：**
166: > 今回のプロジェクトから得た教訓を活かして相乗効果を高め、推進力を維持するのが主な目標だ。チームの成果を上げるには、新入社員の受け入れ体制を改善し、現場の課題を解決する仕組みを作る必要がある。
167: 
168: ---
169: 
170: ### 32. 「より」比較副詞の濫用
171: 
172: **注意語：** より + 形容詞・副詞の形 — 「より具体的な」「より効率的な」「より深い」「より明確な」「より積極的な」「より体系的な」「より効果的な」「より慎重な」「より幅広い」「より良い」
173: 
174: **問題：** 日本語の比較副詞「より」は、日常会話ではほとんど使われない。「もっと」のほうが自然だ。AI はフォーマルかつ知的に聞こえようとして「より X な」を多用するが、一つの文書に複数回出ると即座に AI テキストと識別される。これは KO の「보다」過剰使用（Pattern 32 KO）と並行する現象で、JA でも同じく目立つ。
175: 
176: **発火条件：** 1つの文書に「より + 形容詞/副詞」の形が 2 回以上登場。または、1つの段落に「より + 形容詞」が 1 回出現し、同じ段落に他のフォーマルマーカー（「かと思われます」「であると考えます」「いたします」「ございます」など）が共起する場合。
177: 
178: **除外条件：**
179: - 「Aより B」形式の実際の比較 — 「東京より大阪が暖かい」のような格助詞「より」
180: - 「より良い」が 1 回限りで、フォーマルマーカーと共起していない場合
181: - 学術論文、法律文書、公式広報での意図的なフォーマル文体
182: - 慣用表現として固定化したもの（「より一層」など、ビジネス文書で広く受容されている表現）
183: 
184: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
185: **Preservation Note:** 「より X な」を機械的にすべて「もっと X な」に置き換えると新しい機械的パターンになる。文脈に応じて多様化する — 「もっと X な」「X な」「より深く」のような副詞句、または別の表現に書き換える。
186: 
187: **Burstiness ガイダンス：** 同じ文書内のすべての「より X」を同じ方法で変えない。一部は「もっと」、一部は形容詞のみ残し、一部は別の表現に変える。
188: 
189: **修正前：**
190: > プロジェクトのスケジュールに関しては、より具体的なマイルストーンの設定が必要かと思われます。予算配分についても、より効率的な運用方法の検討が必要であると考えます。可能であれば、来週中により深い議論を進められれば幸いです。
191: 
192: **修正後：**
193: > プロジェクトの日程は、もっと具体的なマイルストーンが必要そうです。予算の使い方についても、一度見直したほうがよさそうです。来週、もう少しじっくり話せる時間を取れたら助かります。
194: 
195: **このパターンではないもの：** 「より」が格助詞として比較を表す場合（「東京より大阪が暖かい」）→ 発火しない。「Aに比べて B」のような比較構文 → Pattern 26（翻訳調）で扱う。
196: 
197: ---
198: 
199: ### 33. 定義的メタファー等式（「XはZの〜だ」構文）
200: 
201: **注意語彙：**（コピュラ「〜だ／である」＋抽象名詞）署名、痕跡、シグナル、形、輪郭、言語、通貨、設計図、屋台骨、背骨、エンジン、原動力、心臓、鼓動、DNA、礎、要、生命線、土台、地図、文法、レンズ
202: 
203: **問題：** 「XはZの〔抽象名詞〕だ」という断定文で壮大なメタファー的等価を主張し、深遠さを人工的に演出する。具体的な裏付けがないまま「〜は〜の署名だ」「〜は〜の設計図だ」のように抽象名詞へ等号で結びつけると、思索の見かけだけが残り、検証可能な内容が抜け落ちる。SNSの格言調・ビジネス書のキャッチコピー・自己啓発系エッセイに頻出する。英語の "Cringe is the visible signature of moving along a gradient you chose."「Symmetry is the architecture of trust.」と同型の現象で、JAでも「〜は〜の署名だ／設計図だ／通貨だ」として現れる。
204: 
205: **発火条件：** 同一文書/セクション内で「XはZの〔抽象名詞〕だ／である」というコピュラ構文が2回以上現れ、どれも具体的な根拠・機序・例示に支えられていない場合。単独1回の出現は audit hint にとどめ、同型の膨らんだ等式が反復するときだけ rewrite 対象にする。
206: 
207: **除外条件：**
208: - 字義どおりの定義・専門的定義（「水は万能溶媒だ」「ミトコンドリアは細胞の発電所だ」「東京は日本の首都だ」のような事実陳述・定着した教科書的比喩）
209: - 慣用句・ことわざとして定着した表現（「時は金なり」など）
210: - 具体例・データ・機序で等価が実際に支えられている場合（「このAPIは認証基盤の心臓だ――全リクエストがここを通り、月3億回呼ばれる」のように直後で実体を示すもの）
211: - 比喩であることを明示し、その射程を限定している場合
212: 
213: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
214: **Preservation Note:** メタファー等式をほどく際に、書き手が本当に伝えたい主張（XがZにとって重要だ、という関係性）まで消さないよう注意。比喩を外したら、その比喩が指していた具体的な役割・機能・因果を平叙文で言い換えて補う。
215: 
216: **修正前：**
217: > 対称性は信頼の設計図だ。一貫性は、ブランドが顧客と交わす無言の通貨である。そしてデザインの細部こそ、その企業の価値観の署名にほかならない。
218: 
219: **修正後：**
220: > ボタンの位置や余白を画面ごとに揃えておくと、ユーザーは次の操作を迷わず予測できる。実際、レイアウトを統一したあとはサポートへの「どこを押せばいい？」という問い合わせが3割減った。
221: 
222: **このパターンではないもの：** コピュラ「だ／である」そのものを**避けて**「〜の役割を果たす」「〜として機能する」と書く回避癖 → Pattern 8（コピュラ回避）で扱う。#8 は「だ」を避ける癖を「だ」に戻す方向、#33 は逆に「Xは Z の〔抽象名詞〕だ」という**膨らんだメタファー等式**そのものを縮める方向で、両者を混同しない。字義どおり・専門的な定義文（「東京は日本の首都だ」）は #33 では発火しない。
223: 
224: ### 34. 無生物行為主（擬似的行為性）
225: 
226: **注意語彙:** データが物語っている, 数字が証明している, 決定が自然と生まれた, 文化が変わっていった, 議論が~へと動いた, 市場が報いる, 市場が選択する, このテクノロジーが~を可能にする, 戦略が要求する, 状況が語るように, 結果がすべてを物語る
227: 
228: **発火条件:** 同一文書内で、無生物主語が人間の行為（決定・判断・報酬・要求・議論の主導）を行う文が 2 回以上現れ、文脈から行為者を特定できるのに最後まで名前が出てこないとき。単独 1 回は audit ヒントのみ。
229: 
230: **除外条件:**
231: - 学術・報告書の引用慣行（「本研究は~を示す」「報告書は~を指摘する」）— 著者への慣用的な換喩
232: - 本当に行為者のない過程（「気温が上がった」「締め切りが過ぎた」「市場が暴落した」のような集合的事象）
233: - 文学的装置として一貫して維持される擬人法、定着した慣用句
234: - ソフトウェア/システムが実際に行う動作（「リンターが検出する」「パイプラインがリトライする」）
235: 
236: **受動態検出との区別:** 受動態は文法で行為者を隠す（「決定が下された」）。擬似的行為性は客体を主語に昇格させて隠す（「決定が自然と生まれた」）。残る問いは同じ——誰がやったのか——だが、このパターンは能動態の文で発火するため、受動態検出では捕まらない。もともと日本語は無生物主語構文を好まない言語なので、この構文の蓄積は翻訳調のシグナルとしても機能する。
237: 
238: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
239: **Preservation Note:** 原文が裏付けない行為者を捏造しない。文脈から行為者が復元できるなら（チーム、筆者、「あなた」）それを使い、復元できないなら既知の事実を中心に文を組み直す。行為者の捏造は文体修正ではなく意味違反である。
240: 
241: **問題:** AI は抽象物を行為者に昇格させる——決定が生まれ、文化が変わり、データが語り、市場が報いる。誰が何をしたかにコミットせずに分析的な響きを出せるからだ。人間の文章は行為者を名指すか、少なくともどこかに立つ（「私たち」「あなた」「チーム」）。手がかりは一文ではなく蓄積にある：段落全体で出来事は起きているのに、人がひとりも登場しない。
242: 
243: **修正前:**
244: > フィードバックループが品質より速度に報いた結果、四半期のうちに文化が変わっていった。意思決定は会議ではなく Slack スレッドから自然と生まれ、ロードマップは指標が要求する方向へ曲がっていった。
245: 
246: **修正後:**
247: > 経営陣がボーナスを不具合件数ではなくリリース速度に紐づけたので、四半期のうちにエンジニアは磨き込みをやめた。みんな会議ではなく Slack スレッドで決めるようになり、PM はその週のダッシュボードの数字に合わせてロードマップを書き直した。
````

## File: patterns/ko-language.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: ko-language
  3: language: ko
  4: name: 언어/문법 패턴
  5: version: 1.2.0
  6: patterns: 9
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: dedupe-with:
 13:   - source: "ko-language:7"
 14:     target: "ko-style:13"
 15:     owner: "ko-style:13"
 16:     reason: "연결 표현은 style #13이 canonical owner이고, language #7에서는 다른 AI 어휘와 함께 군집으로 나타날 때만 보조 신호로 본다."
 17:   - source: "ko-language:7"
 18:     target: "ko-language:8"
 19:     owner: "ko-language:8"
 20:     reason: "\"~적\" 한자어 형용사 연쇄는 language #8이 canonical owner이고, language #7은 다른 AI 고빈도 어휘와 함께 나타나는 넓은 군집 신호로만 본다."
 21: ---
 22: 
 23: # 언어/문법 패턴
 24: 
 25: ### 7. AI 특유 어휘 남발
 26: 
 27: **고빈도 AI 어휘:** 다양한, 활발한, 주목할 만한, 혁신적인, 체계적인, 지속적인, 효과적인, 적극적인, 심층적인, 유기적인, 종합적인, 선도적인, 아울러, 나아가, 이를 통해, 이러한 맥락에서, 도모하다, 촉진하다, 극대화하다
 28: 
 29: **문제:** 이 단어들은 2023년 이후 텍스트에서 훨씬 높은 빈도로 나타난다. 자주 함께 쓰인다.
 30: 
 31: **발화 조건:** 한 문단에 고빈도 AI 어휘가 3개 이상 등장하는 경우.
 32: 
 33: **제외 조건:**
 34: - 개별 단어가 단독으로 1-2회 사용된 경우(일반 한국어에서도 나타나는 단어들이므로)
 35: - allowlist에 포함된 표현
 36: 
 37: **의미 위험도:** LOW
 38: 
 39: **수정 전:**
 40: > 아울러, 다양한 혁신적인 기술들이 활발하게 개발되고 있으며, 이를 통해 체계적이고 효과적인 솔루션을 제공하고 있다. 나아가, 이러한 지속적인 노력은 주목할 만한 성과를 거두고 있다.
 41: 
 42: **수정 후:**
 43: > 올해 나온 기술 중에는 배터리 수명을 20% 늘린 전력 관리 칩과, 처리 속도가 기존보다 두 배 빠른 NPU가 있다.
 44: 
 45: ---
 46: 
 47: ### 8. ~적(的) 접미사 남발
 48: 
 49: **주의 어휘:** 혁신적, 체계적, 효과적, 효율적, 선도적, 진취적, 종합적, 핵심적, 전략적, 실질적, 근본적, 획기적
 50: 
 51: **문제:** 한국어 AI 글쓰기에서 가장 두드러지는 패턴 중 하나. "~적" 한자어 형용사를 한 문장에 여러 개 쌓아 올린다.
 52: 
 53: **발화 조건:** 한 문장에 "~적" 형용사가 3개 이상 등장하거나, 연속 2문장에 걸쳐 4개 이상 사용된 경우.
 54: 
 55: **제외 조건:**
 56: - 학술 논문·보고서에서 정의상 필요한 전문 용어("체계적 문헌 고찰", "효과적 세율")
 57: - 단독 1개 사용은 정상 한국어이므로 교정 대상 아님
 58: 
 59: **의미 위험도:** MEDIUM
 60: **보존 주의사항:** "~적" 형용사를 풀어쓸 때 원래 표현이 담고 있던 강도나 범위가 희석되지 않도록 주의. "근본적인 변화"를 단순히 "변화"로 축약하면 의미가 약해진다.
 61: 
 62: **Burstiness 지침:** "~적" 형용사를 교정할 때, 모든 형용사를 기계적으로 풀어쓰지 않는다. 일부는 구체적 서술로 바꾸고("혁신적인" → "기존과 다른"), 일부는 아예 삭제하고, 일부는 다른 품사로 전환("효율적인 처리" → "빠르게 처리")해서 문장 리듬을 다양하게 만든다.
 63: 
 64: **수정 전:**
 65: > 혁신적이고 체계적인 접근 방식을 통해, 효과적이고 효율적인 결과를 도출하며, 선도적이고 진취적인 자세로 종합적인 발전을 추구하고 있다.
 66: 
 67: **수정 후:**
 68: > 팀은 기존 프로세스를 단순화하고, 불필요한 승인 단계를 빼서 배포 시간을 절반으로 줄였다.
 69: 
 70: ---
 71: 
 72: ### 9. 부정 병렬구조
 73: 
 74: **주의 어휘:** ~에 그치지 않고, ~뿐만 아니라 ~도, 비단 ~뿐 아니라, ~을 넘어
 75: 
 76: **문제:** "단순히 A가 아니라 B이다", "A뿐만 아니라 B도" 구조를 과도하게 사용한다.
 77: 
 78: **발화 조건:** 같은 문서에 "~뿐만 아니라", "~에 그치지 않고" 등의 부정 병렬 구조가 2회 이상 등장하거나, 긍정 서술만으로 더 간결하게 표현할 수 있는 곳에 단일 사용된 경우.
 79: 
 80: **제외 조건:**
 81: - 오해를 바로잡기 위한 실질적 대비("이 행사는 컨퍼런스가 아니라 워크숍이다 — 기조연설 없이 실습만 진행")
 82: - 두 요소의 대조가 논리적으로 필수적인 경우
 83: 
 84: **의미 위험도:** MEDIUM
 85: **보존 주의사항:** 부정 병렬 구조를 긍정문으로 바꿀 때 원문이 강조하려던 A와 B의 대비 관계가 사라지지 않도록 주의. 특히 "A가 아니라 B"에서 A를 명시적으로 부정하는 것이 핵심인 경우 해당 부정 의미를 유지해야 한다.
 86: 
 87: **Burstiness 지침:** 부정 병렬 구조를 풀 때, 모든 문장을 "A이다. B도 있다." 식의 균일한 긍정문으로 바꾸지 않는다. 일부는 직접 서술하고, 일부는 사례로 보여주고, 일부는 문장 구조 자체를 바꿔서 단조로움을 피한다.
 88: 
 89: **수정 전:**
 90: > 이것은 단순한 기술 발전에 그치지 않고, 우리 사회 전반에 걸친 근본적인 변화를 의미한다. 이는 비단 경제적 측면뿐만 아니라, 사회적·문화적 측면에서도 중대한 함의를 지닌다.
 91: 
 92: **수정 후:**
 93: > 이 기술은 처음에 제조 공정에 적용됐고, 이후 물류와 고객 서비스에도 쓰이기 시작했다.
 94: 
 95: ---
 96: 
 97: ### 10. 3의 법칙 남발
 98: 
 99: **문제:** AI는 포괄적으로 보이려고 아이디어를 무조건 셋으로 묶는다.
100: 
101: **발화 조건:** 같은 문서에 3개 항목 나열이 2회 이상 등장하거나, 셋으로 묶은 근거가 자의적이어서 다른 개수도 동등하게 성립하는 경우.
102: 
103: **제외 조건:**
104: - 본질적으로 3요소인 구조(과거/현재/미래, 입력/처리/출력, 서론/본론/결론)
105: - 실제로 3개인 항목을 나열하는 경우
106: 
107: **의미 위험도:** MEDIUM
108: **보존 주의사항:** 3개 항목 나열을 줄일 때 삭제되는 항목이 실질적 내용을 담고 있는지 확인. 자의적으로 묶인 항목이라도 각각이 독립적 의미를 가진다면 모두 유지해야 한다.
109: 
110: **Burstiness 지침:** 수정 시 자연스러운 개수를 사용한다. 포인트가 하나뿐이면 하나만, 둘이면 둘, 넷이면 넷으로 쓴다. 문서 전체에서 목록 길이가 다양해야 사람이 쓴 글처럼 보인다.
111: 
112: **수정 전:**
113: > 이 프로그램은 창의성, 혁신성, 그리고 지속가능성을 추구합니다. 참가자들은 영감, 통찰, 그리고 실질적인 경험을 얻을 수 있습니다.
114: 
115: **수정 후:**
116: > 이 프로그램은 실무 프로젝트 중심으로 운영되며, 참가자들은 8주간 실제 제품 개발에 참여한다.
117: 
118: ---
119: 
120: ### 11. 유의어 순환
121: 
122: **문제:** AI는 반복을 피하려고 같은 대상을 계속 다른 말로 바꿔 부른다.
123: 
124: **발화 조건:** 같은 문단에서 동일 대상이 3개 이상의 다른 명칭·동의어로 지칭되는 경우.
125: 
126: **제외 조건:**
127: - 의도적 구분 목적(예: "삼성전자"와 "삼성그룹"을 모회사·자회사로 구별)
128: - 정밀도를 위한 변형("이 연구"와 "2023년 스탠퍼드 논문"처럼 구체성이 달라지는 경우)
129: 
130: **의미 위험도:** LOW
131: 
132: **수정 전:**
133: > 이 도시는 많은 관광객을 끌어모으고 있다. 이 지역은 다양한 문화 행사로 유명하다. 해당 지자체는 관광 인프라를 확충 중이다. 이곳은 연간 방문객이 100만 명에 달한다.
134: 
135: **수정 후:**
136: > 이 도시에는 연간 100만 명이 방문한다. 문화 행사가 많고, 시는 관광 인프라에 500억 원을 투자하고 있다.
137: 
138: ---
139: 
140: ### 12. ~에 있어서/~함에 있어 장황한 조사 사용
141: 
142: **주의 어휘:** ~에 있어서, ~함에 있어, ~(으)로서, ~(이)라는 점에서, ~의 관점에서 볼 때
143: 
144: **문제:** 한국어 AI는 불필요하게 장황한 조사와 연결 표현으로 문장을 늘린다.
145: 
146: **발화 조건:** 같은 문단에 장황한 조사 표현이 2개 이상 등장하거나, "~에서"로 충분한 곳에 "~에 있어서"가 사용된 경우.
147: 
148: **제외 조건:**
149: - 법률·계약 문서에서 관례적으로 사용되는 표현("~(으)로서의 의무")
150: - "~(으)로서"가 자격·역할을 명확히 구분하기 위해 필요한 경우("교사로서" vs "교사로")
151: 
152: **의미 위험도:** LOW
153: 
154: **수정 전:**
155: > 교육에 있어서 가장 중요한 것은 학생들의 참여를 이끌어 내는 것이며, 이를 달성함에 있어 교사의 역할이 핵심적이다. 학습 효과를 극대화함에 있어서는 개별화된 접근이 필요하다.
156: 
157: **수정 후:**
158: > 교육에서 가장 중요한 것은 학생 참여다. 교사들은 학생별로 다른 과제를 내는 방식을 시도하고 있다.
159: 
160: ---
161: 
162: ### 32. "보다" 비교부사 남용
163: 
164: **주의 어휘:** 보다 + 형용사/부사 형태 — "보다 구체적인", "보다 효율적인", "보다 심도 있는", "보다 다양한", "보다 명확한", "보다 적극적인", "보다 체계적인", "보다 효과적인", "보다 신중한", "보다 폭넓은"
165: 
166: **문제:** 한국어 비교부사 "보다"는 일상에서 거의 쓰지 않는다. "더"가 자연스럽다. AI는 격식적으로 들리려고 "보다 X한"을 남발하는데, 한 문서 안에 여러 번 나오면 즉시 AI 텍스트로 인식된다. 이는 일본어 "より"의 직역체에 영향받은 표현이기도 하다.
167: 
168: **발화 조건:** 한 문서 안에 "보다 [형용사/부사 + 한]" 형태가 2회 이상 등장. 또는 한 단락 안에 "보다 X" 표현이 1회 등장하면서 같은 단락에 다른 격식체 마커("~을 위한", "~에 있어서", "~사료됩니다", "~할 것으로 보입니다")가 함께 있는 경우.
169: 
170: **제외 조건:**
171: - "X보다 Y" 형태의 실제 비교 — "한국이 일본보다 빠르다" 같은 문장
172: - "보다"가 동사로 쓰인 경우 — "영화를 보다", "뉴스를 보다"
173: - 학술 논문, 법률 문서, 공식 보도자료에서 의도적인 격식체 사용
174: - 한 문서에 1회만 등장하는 단발 사용 (격식체가 아닌 다른 마커가 동반되지 않은 경우)
175: 
176: **의미 위험도:** LOW
177: **보존 주의사항:** "보다 X한"을 무조건 "더 X한"으로 단순 치환하면 또 다른 기계적 패턴이 된다. 문맥에 따라 다양화 — 일부는 "더 X한", 일부는 형용사만 남기고("X한"), 일부는 다른 표현으로 풀어 쓴다.
178: 
179: **Burstiness 지침:** 같은 문서 안의 모든 "보다 X" 표현을 동일한 방식으로 바꾸지 말 것. 첫 번째는 "더 X한", 두 번째는 "X한", 세 번째는 아예 다른 표현 식으로 변주.
180: 
181: **수정 전:**
182: > 프로젝트 일정과 관련하여 보다 구체적인 마일스톤 설정이 필요할 것으로 보입니다. 예산 배분에 있어서도 보다 효율적인 운영 방안에 대한 검토가 이루어져야 할 것으로 사료됩니다. 가능하시다면 다음 주 중 보다 심도 있는 논의를 진행하는 것이 어떨까 싶습니다.
183: 
184: **수정 후:**
185: > 프로젝트 일정에 더 구체적인 마일스톤이 있어야 할 것 같습니다. 예산 운영 방안도 한 번 점검이 필요해 보입니다. 다음 주에 자세히 이야기 나누면 좋겠습니다.
186: 
187: **이 패턴이 아닌 것:** "보다"가 동사인 경우(예: "오늘은 영화를 보다") → 발화하지 않음. "X에 비해 Y" 같은 비교 구문도 → 패턴 26(번역체)에서 처리.
188: 
189: ---
190: 
191: ### 33. 정의형 은유 등식
192: 
193: **주의 어휘:** X는 Z의 [추상명사]다 / X는 Z의 [추상명사]이다 — 추상명사 자리에 오는 신호어: 시그니처, 서명, 형태, 언어, 통화, 화폐, 건축물, 설계도, 골격, 척추, 엔진, 심장박동, 맥박, DNA, 초석, 주춧돌, 생명선, 혈류, 문법, 풍경
194: 
195: **문제:** "X는 Z의 시그니처다", "대칭은 신뢰의 건축물이다"처럼 계사("~다/~이다")로 추상적 은유 등식을 단언해 깊이 있어 보이게 만든다. 구체적 근거 없이 거창한 동일시만으로 통찰을 가장하는 AI 특유의 수사다. 비유 자체가 검증되지 않고, 어떤 명사에든 끼워 넣어도 그럴듯하게 들리기 때문에 실제 정보량은 거의 없다.
196: 
197: **발화 조건:** "A는 B의 [추상명사]다/이다" 형태의 계사 문장이 같은 글/섹션 안에 2회 이상 등장하고, 그 등식을 뒷받침하는 구체적 근거(데이터·사례·메커니즘)가 없는 경우. 단일 1회 사용은 audit hint로만 취급하고, 같은 부풀린 은유 등식이 반복될 때만 rewrite 대상으로 본다.
198: 
199: **제외 조건:**
200: - 사전적·기술적 정의 — "물은 만능 용매다", "미토콘드리아는 세포의 발전소다"처럼 교과서적으로 확립된 정의·관용 비유
201: - "서울은 한국의 수도다"처럼 지명·직함 등 사실 등식
202: - 구체적 근거로 뒷받침된 진짜 동일시 — 바로 뒤에 메커니즘·사례·데이터가 이어져 그 비유가 실제로 성립함을 보여주는 경우 ("재고 회전율은 이 회사의 심장박동이다 — 회전율이 2주만 늦어져도 현금이 마르고 발주가 멈춘다")
203: - 인용·대화체 안에서의 등장
204: 
205: **의미 위험도:** MEDIUM
206: **보존 주의사항:** 은유 등식을 풀어쓸 때 원문이 전달하려던 실제 주장(X와 Z가 어떤 관계라는 것)을 잃지 않도록 주의. 비유를 걷어내되 글쓴이가 말하려던 인과·기능 관계는 구체적 서술로 남긴다. 비유가 사실 아무 내용도 담고 있지 않다면 문장을 통째로 삭제하는 것이 가장 정직한 교정이다.
207: 
208: **이 패턴이 아닌 것:** 패턴 8(계사 회피)은 "~의 역할을 한다", "~로 기능한다"처럼 "~다/이다"를 **피하는** 것을 잡아 계사로 되돌린다. 반대로 패턴 33은 계사를 써서 만든 **부풀린 "X는 Z의 OO다" 은유 등식**을 잡는다. 둘은 정반대 방향이므로 혼동하지 말 것 — 8은 계사 복원, 33은 과장된 계사 등식 해체.
209: 
210: **수정 전:**
211: > 침묵은 권력의 언어다. 회의실에서 누가 말하지 않는가를 보면 조직의 진짜 위계가 드러난다. 신뢰는 모든 협업의 통화이며, 투명성은 그 신뢰의 건축물이다.
212: 
213: **수정 후:**
214: > 회의에서 누가 말하지 않는지를 보면 조직의 실제 위계가 보인다. 발언권은 대개 직급이 아니라 평가 권한을 쥔 사람에게 쏠린다. 협업이 굴러가려면 서로 정보를 숨기지 않아야 하고, 그건 진행 상황을 공개적으로 공유하는 습관에서 나온다.
215: 
216: ### 34. 무생물 행위주 (가짜 행위성)
217: 
218: **주의 어휘:** 데이터가 말해준다, 숫자가 증명한다, 결정이 내려지는 과정에서 자연스럽게, 문화가 바뀌었다, 논의가 ~쪽으로 흘러갔다, 시장이 보상한다, 시장이 선택한다, 이 기술이 ~을 가능하게 한다, 전략이 요구한다, 상황이 말해주듯, 결과가 스스로 증명한다
219: 
220: **발화 조건:** 한 문서에서 무생물 주어가 인간의 행위(결정, 판단, 보상, 요구, 논의 주도)를 수행하는 문장이 2회 이상 나타나고, 문맥상 행위자를 특정할 수 있는데도 끝까지 이름이 등장하지 않을 때. 단독 1회는 audit 힌트로만.
221: 
222: **제외 조건:**
223: - 학술·보고서 인용 관행 ("본 연구는 ~을 보여준다", "보고서는 ~을 지적한다") — 저자에 대한 관용적 환유
224: - 실제로 행위자 없는 과정 ("기온이 올랐다", "마감이 지났다", "시장이 폭락했다"처럼 집합적 사건)
225: - 의도적 의인법이 장치로 일관되게 유지되는 문학적 문체, 정착된 관용구
226: - 소프트웨어/시스템이 실제로 하는 동작 ("린터가 잡아낸다", "파이프라인이 재시도한다")
227: 
228: **#26(번역체)과의 구분:** #26은 영어 구문의 직역 흔적("~에 의해 ~되다")을 잡는다. #34는 번역체 여부와 무관하게 **행위자 은폐** 자체를 잡는다 — 문장이 자연스러운 한국어라도 "결정이 내려졌다, 문화가 바뀌었다"가 쌓이며 사람이 한 명도 등장하지 않으면 발화한다.
229: 
230: **의미 위험도:** HIGH
231: **보존 주의사항:** 원문이 뒷받침하지 않는 행위자를 지어내지 않는다. 문맥상 행위자가 복원 가능하면(팀, 저자, "여러분") 그것을 쓰고, 복원 불가능하면 알려진 사실 중심으로 재구성한다. 행위자 날조는 문체 교정이 아니라 의미 위반이다.
232: 
233: **문제:** AI는 추상물을 행위자로 승격시킨다 — 결정이 내려지고, 문화가 바뀌고, 데이터가 말하고, 시장이 보상한다. 누가 했는지 말하지 않고도 분석적으로 들리기 때문이다. 사람 글은 행위자를 짚거나 최소한 어딘가에 선다("우리", "여러분", "팀"). 티는 한 문장이 아니라 누적이다: 문단 전체에서 사건은 일어나는데 사람이 아무도 없다.
234: 
235: **수정 전:**
236: > 피드백 루프가 품질보다 속도를 보상하면서 분기 만에 조직 문화가 바뀌었다. 결정은 회의가 아니라 슬랙 스레드에서 자연스럽게 내려졌고, 로드맵은 지표가 요구하는 방향으로 휘어졌다.
237: 
238: **수정 후:**
239: > 경영진이 결함 수가 아니라 출시 속도에 보너스를 걸자, 분기 만에 엔지니어들은 다듬는 일을 그만뒀다. 사람들은 회의 대신 슬랙 스레드에서 결정을 내리기 시작했고, PM들은 그 주 대시보드 숫자에 맞춰 로드맵을 다시 썼다.
````

## File: patterns/zh-language.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: zh-language
  3: language: zh
  4: name: 语言/语法模式
  5: version: 1.1.0
  6: patterns: 9
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: dedupe-with:
 13:   - source: "zh-language:7"
 14:     target: "zh-language:33"
 15:     owner: "zh-language:33"
 16:     reason: "“底层逻辑”既是 #7 的AI高频堆砌词，又可充当 #33 “X 是 Z 的[抽象名词]”隐喻等式的尾词。出现在该判断句框架内时由 #33 负责拆解隐喻等式；作为独立堆砌的流行词时仍归 #7。"
 17: ---
 18: 
 19: # 语言/语法模式
 20: 
 21: ### 7. AI高频词汇堆砌
 22: 
 23: **高频词汇：** 赋能、助力、深耕、布局、打造、构建、引领、聚焦、链接、生态、维度、颗粒度、底层逻辑、顶层设计、全方位、多层次、宽领域、高质量发展、新质生产力
 24: 
 25: **问题：** 这些词在2023年后AI生成的中文文本中出现频率显著偏高。它们常常扎堆出现，成为AI文本的显著指纹。
 26: 
 27: **触发条件：** 同一段落中出现3个以上高频AI词汇。
 28: 
 29: **排除条件：**
 30: - 单个词独立使用1-2次（这些词在正常中文中也会出现）
 31: - allowlist中的表达
 32: 
 33: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 34: **Preservation Note:** 替换AI高频词时，必须保留原词承载的具体对象、技术含义或政策语境；若原词只是空泛包装，再改成具体事实或数据。
 35: 
 36: **修改前：**
 37: > 我们致力于深耕AI赛道，聚焦底层逻辑创新，全方位赋能千行百业，打造多层次的智能生态体系，助力高质量发展。
 38: 
 39: **修改后：**
 40: > 公司主要做两件事：训练行业专用模型，给制造业和物流企业做部署。去年签了47个客户，比前年多了一倍。
 41: 
 42: ---
 43: 
 44: ### 8. 四字成语/四字格过度使用
 45: 
 46: **高频词汇：** 蓬勃发展、与日俱增、日新月异、任重道远、砥砺前行、不遗余力、齐心协力、众志成城、凝心聚力、守正创新、踔厉奋发、勇毅前行
 47: 
 48: **问题：** AI过度使用四字成语和四字格式词组，使文章读起来像官方文件或领导讲话稿。在日常写作中，四字格堆砌是AI生成的强烈信号。
 49: 
 50: **触发条件：** 同一段落中出现3个以上四字成语/四字格词组，或连续2句都以四字格结尾。
 51: 
 52: **排除条件：**
 53: - 正式的政治文件、领导讲话稿（四字格是其文体特征）
 54: - 文学作品中有意使用的修辞手法
 55: - 单个四字成语的自然使用
 56: 
 57: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 58: **Preservation Note:** 删减四字格时，保留原文的评价方向、正式程度和情绪强度；若文体本来是正式讲话或公文，不要把必要的文体标记全部抹平。
 59: 
 60: **Burstiness 指引：** 替换四字格时，不要机械地用"普通话"逐个替换。有的直接删掉，有的用具体数据代替，有的换成口语化表达。让句子长短不一。
 61: 
 62: **修改前：**
 63: > 在全体员工齐心协力、砥砺前行的共同努力下，公司业务蓬勃发展、蒸蒸日上，各项指标与日俱增，展现出日新月异的发展态势。
 64: 
 65: **修改后：**
 66: > 公司去年营收增长了23%，员工数从120人扩到了190人。增长最快的是海外业务，占比从15%涨到了28%。
 67: 
 68: ---
 69: 
 70: ### 9. "的地得"和书面语语法过度规范化
 71: 
 72: **问题：** AI生成的中文往往在"的/地/得"的使用上过度规范，每个位置都精确区分。而实际的中文母语写作——特别是非学术写作——经常混用或省略。AI的"完美"反而不自然。另一个信号是句式过度书面化，日常写作中不会出现的文言语法。
 73: 
 74: **高频词汇：** 积极地、有效地、充分地、深入地、全面地、切实地、进一步地、使得、以至于、鉴于此、有鉴于此
 75: 
 76: **触发条件：** 同一段落中出现3个以上"X地+动词"结构（特别是"积极地"、"有效地"、"充分地"这类程度副词），或使用明显文言语法的书面化表达在日常文体中出现。
 77: 
 78: **排除条件：**
 79: - 学术论文、法律文件等需要严格语法规范的文体
 80: - 单个"X地+动词"结构（正常中文中也会出现）
 81: 
 82: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 83: **Preservation Note:** 简化“的/地/得”和书面语结构时，保留动作方式、程度和因果关系；不要因省略副词而改变行为是如何发生的。
 84: 
 85: **修改前：**
 86: > 团队积极地探索新方向，有效地整合了各方资源，充分地发挥了技术优势，切实地推动了项目进展。
 87: 
 88: **修改后：**
 89: > 团队换了个方向——把原来做通用模型的算力转去做垂直场景。三个月搞定了医疗影像的原型，现在已经进了两家三甲医院试用。
 90: 
 91: ---
 92: 
 93: ### 10. 排比句过度使用
 94: 
 95: **问题：** AI喜欢用排比句来增加语势和"文采"。三个或更多结构相同的分句并列排列。在正式演讲中排比是有效的修辞手段，但在日常写作中大量使用是AI的典型标记。
 96: 
 97: **触发条件：** 同一段落中出现2组以上排比结构，或单组排比中并列3个以上结构高度相似的分句且可以用更简练的方式表达。
 98: 
 99: **排除条件：**
100: - 演讲稿、政论文等排比为标准修辞的文体
101: - 本质上就是三项并列的内容（过去/现在/未来等天然三元结构）
102: 
103: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
104: **Preservation Note:** 拆解排比时，每个分句的独立主张、对象和顺序都要保留；只能去掉空泛重复，不能删掉实际并列事项。
105: 
106: **Burstiness 指引：** 拆解排比时，让改后的句子长度差异明显。有的一句话说完，有的展开说两三句，有的用数据代替空话。
107: 
108: **修改前：**
109: > 我们要坚持创新驱动发展，坚持人才优先战略，坚持开放合作共赢，坚持绿色可持续理念，努力开创各项事业发展新局面。
110: 
111: **修改后：**
112: > 明年的重点是招30个算法工程师，把研发投入从营收的12%提到18%。
113: 
114: ---
115: 
116: ### 11. 同义词循环替换
117: 
118: **问题：** AI为了避免重复，不断用不同的说法指代同一事物。一个公司在一段话里先后被称为"该企业"、"这家公司"、"该集团"、"该机构"。
119: 
120: **触发条件：** 同一段落中同一实体被3个以上不同的名称/同义词指代。
121: 
122: **排除条件：**
123: - 有意区分的场合（例："腾讯"和"腾讯控股"分别指子公司和母公司）
124: - 精确度不同的变体（"该研究"和"2023年清华论文"这种具体化引用）
125: 
126: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
127: **Preservation Note:** 统一同一实体称呼时，先确认不同称呼是否真的指向同一对象；若涉及母公司/子公司、机构/产品或不同身份，必须保留区分。
128: 
129: **修改前：**
130: > 华为在芯片领域取得了重大突破。该企业近年来持续加大研发投入。这家科技巨头的自研芯片已应用于多款产品。该公司表示将继续推进技术自主化进程。
131: 
132: **修改后：**
133: > 华为的麒麟9000S是用中芯国际的7纳米工艺造的。去年装在Mate 60上之后，这款手机国内销量超过了3500万台。
134: 
135: ---
136: 
137: ### 12. 冗长的介词结构
138: 
139: **高频词汇：** 在～的过程中、在～的背景下、在～的基础上、在～的推动下、在～的带领下、以～为目标、以～为契机、以～为抓手、对于～而言、从～的角度来看
140: 
141: **问题：** AI使用冗长的介词框架结构来填充句子。"在数字化转型的背景下"可以直接说"数字化转型中"甚至直接省略。
142: 
143: **触发条件：** 同一段落中出现2个以上冗长介词结构，或删除后完全不影响意思的介词结构被单独使用。
144: 
145: **排除条件：**
146: - 法律/合同文件中为了精确而必要的介词结构
147: - 删除后确实会影响语义的场合
148: 
149: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
150: **Preservation Note:** 删除介词框架时，若框架内含有背景条件或限定语境（如"在政策推动的基础上"暗示政策依赖关系），该条件信息须以更简洁的形式保留，而非直接删除。
151: 
152: **修改前：**
153: > 在数字化转型的大背景下，在政策大力推动的基础上，以提升核心竞争力为目标，我们以此次改革为契机，全面推进业务升级。
154: 
155: **修改后：**
156: > 公司今年把ERP系统换成了自研的，砍掉了三层审批流程。结果订单处理时间从4天缩到了1天半。
157: 
158: ---
159: 
160: ### 32. “更”比较副词滥用
161: 
162: **注意词汇：** 更具体、更高效、更全面、更深入、更清晰、更积极、更系统、更有效、更稳健、更广泛、更优质、更可持续、更具战略性
163: 
164: **问题：** AI中文常用“更X”来制造进步感，却不说明和什么相比。“更具体的方案”“更高效的机制”“更深入的讨论”连续出现时，句子像在承诺优化，却没有给出基准、对象或实际变化。
165: 
166: **触发条件：** 同一文档中出现2个以上“更 + 形容词/副词/抽象名词化短语”，且没有明确比较对象、基准或指标。若同一段落中只有1处“更X”，但同时出现“系统推进”“全面提升”“战略框架”“协同机制”等正式AI标记，也可以触发。
167: 
168: **排除条件：**
169: - 明确的真实比较，如“比去年更高效”“新版比旧版更稳定”
170: - 带有可验证指标的改善，如“处理时间从4天缩短到1天半”
171: - 数量表达，如“更多用户”“超过10人”
172: - 口语中自然的单次使用，且没有其他AI正式腔标记
173: 
174: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
175: **Preservation Note:** “更X”有时承载真实的改善方向。若原文含有比较对象、指标或优先级，必须保留；若没有，就把空泛的比较改成具体需求、动作或结果。
176: 
177: **Burstiness 指引：** 不要把所有“更X”机械替换成同一种表达。可以一处保留“更”，一处改成具体指标，一处直接删去比较副词，或用动作说明替代。
178: 
179: **修改前：**
180: > 下一阶段，我们将制定更具体的项目里程碑，建立更高效的资源配置机制，并通过更深入的沟通推动更全面的协同。相关团队也将形成更具战略性的执行框架。
181: 
182: **修改后：**
183: > 下一阶段先做三件事：把里程碑拆到每周，给每个任务指定负责人，重新核对预算表。产品、运营和法务每周二开30分钟会，专门处理跨部门卡住的事项。
184: 
185: ---
186: 
187: ### 33. 定义式隐喻等式（"X 是 Z 的[抽象名词]"）
188: 
189: **注意词汇（抽象名词标记）：** 签名、印记、注脚、形状、轮廓、语言、货币、架构、骨架、引擎、心跳、脉搏、命脉、底色、基石、基因/DNA、底层逻辑、本质、缩影
190: 
191: **问题：** AI 中文喜欢用一个系动词判断句把两个东西强行划等号——"X 是 Z 的[抽象名词]"——借宏大的隐喻制造深刻感。"对称是信任的架构""尴尬是你主动选择的那条梯度留下的可见印记"之类的句子听上去很有哲理，实则只是把一个抽象概念套在另一个上面，没有给出任何可验证的支撑。它常常单独成句，充当段落的"金句"。
192: 
193: **与 #8（系动词回避）的区分（务必不要混淆）：** #8 标记的是**回避"是"**——把"X 是 Y"写成"X 充当 Y""X 起到 Y 的作用""X 相当于 Y"，修改方向是**改回"是"**。#33 恰好相反：它标记的是已经用了"是"、却把"是"膨胀成"X 是 Z 的[抽象名词]"这种**夸张的隐喻等式**，修改方向是**拆掉这个等式、换成具体陈述**。一个是缺"是"，一个是"是"被滥用造句，两者不是同一回事。
194: 
195: **触发条件：** 同一文档/段落中出现 2 处以上“X 是 Z 的[抽象名词]”形式的判断句（抽象名词取自上方标记词或同类），这些等式依赖宏大隐喻而不是具体证据成立。单次出现只记作 audit 提示，不直接进入 rewrite。
196: 
197: **排除条件：**
198: - 字面或技术性定义（"水是万能溶剂""线粒体是细胞的能量工厂""哈希表是一种以键值映射为基础的数据结构"）
199: - 约定俗成的固定说法或事实性等同（"北京是中国的首都""黄河是中华民族的母亲河"作为既有惯用语）
200: - 后文用具体事实、数据或机制真正撑起这个等式的等同关系（不是空泛比喻，而是有论据的论断）
201: 
202: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
203: **Preservation Note:** 拆解隐喻等式时，先判断作者到底想说什么真实主张：是"Z 依赖 X"、"X 能预测 Z"，还是"X 是 Z 的前提条件"？保留这个真实关系和它的方向、强度，只去掉"是…的[抽象名词]"这层装饰；若原文除了比喻之外没有任何可保留的信息，就改成一个具体的事实、动作或观察。
204: 
205: **修改前：**
206: > 对称是信任的架构。在产品设计里，一致性是用户体验的命脉，而留白则是高级感的语言。
207: 
208: **修改后：**
209: > 界面左右对齐、控件间距统一时，用户更容易预判下一步操作，投诉里"找不到按钮"的比例下降了。我们在两版改版里都验证过这一点。
210: 
211: ### 34. 无生命施动者（虚假能动性）
212: 
213: **关注词:** 数据告诉我们, 数字证明了, 决定应运而生, 文化随之改变, 讨论逐渐转向, 市场会奖励, 市场做出了选择, 这项技术让我们能够, 战略要求我们, 形势决定了, 结果不言自明
214: 
215: **触发条件:** 同一文档中出现 2 处以上"无生命主语执行人类行为"（决定、判断、奖励、要求、主导讨论）的句子，且从上下文可以推断出行为者是谁，却始终没有点名。单独一处仅作为 audit 提示。
216: 
217: **排除条件:**
218: - 学术/报告引用惯例（"本研究表明""报告指出"）——对作者的约定俗成的转喻
219: - 确无行为者的过程（"气温上升了""截止日期过了""市场崩盘"这类聚合事件）
220: - 作为文学手法贯穿全文的拟人，以及固定习语
221: - 软件/系统确实在执行的动作（"linter 会标记""流水线会重试"）
222: 
223: **与被动句检测的区分:** 被动句用语法隐藏行为者（"决定被做出了"）；虚假能动性用"把客体提升为主语"隐藏行为者（"决定应运而生"）。两者留下的问题相同——是谁做的——但本模式针对的是主动语态句子，被动句检测抓不到它们。
224: 
225: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
226: **Preservation Note:** 不得虚构原文不支持的行为者。上下文能还原行为者时（团队、作者、"你"），就点名；无法还原时，围绕已知事实重组句子。捏造行为者是语义违规，不是文体修正。
227: 
228: **问题:** AI 系统性地把抽象物提升为行为者——决定诞生、文化改变、数据发声、市场奖励——因为这样既显得有分析感，又永远不用承诺是谁做了什么。人写的文字倾向于点名，或至少站在某个立场（"我们""你""团队"）。破绽不在单句，而在累积：整段都在发生事情，却没有一个人出现。
229: 
230: **修改前:**
231: > 反馈机制奖励速度而非质量，于是一个季度内文化随之改变。决定在 Slack 讨论串中应运而生，路线图朝着指标要求的方向弯曲。
232: 
233: **修改后:**
234: > 管理层把奖金和上线速度挂钩，而不是缺陷数量，于是一个季度内工程师们不再打磨细节。大家开始在 Slack 讨论串里拍板，产品经理每周照着仪表盘上的数字重写路线图。
````

## File: src/loader.js
````javascript
  1: import { existsSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';
  2: import { resolve, sep } from 'node:path';
  3: import yaml from 'js-yaml';
  4: import { validateProfileName } from './security.js';
  5: import { inputError, runtimeError } from './errors.js';
  6: 
  7: /**
  8:  * Read a UTF-8 text file.
  9:  *
 10:  * @param {string} path File path to read.
 11:  * @returns {string} File contents.
 12:  * @throws {Error} When the file cannot be read.
 13:  * @example
 14:  * const markdown = loadFile('README.md');
 15:  */
 16: export function loadFile(path) {
 17:   return readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
 18: }
 19: 
 20: /**
 21:  * Split Markdown-style YAML frontmatter from a document body.
 22:  *
 23:  * @param {string} content File contents.
 24:  * @returns {{frontmatter: object|null, body: string}} Parsed frontmatter and trimmed body.
 25:  * @throws {Error} When YAML frontmatter is invalid.
 26:  * @example
 27:  * const { frontmatter, body } = splitFrontmatter('---\ntitle: x\n---\nBody');
 28:  */
 29: export function splitFrontmatter(content) {
 30:   const match = content.match(/^---\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n---\s*\n([\s\S]*)$/);
 31:   if (!match) {
 32:     return { frontmatter: null, body: content };
 33:   }
 34:   return {
 35:     frontmatter: yaml.load(match[1]),
 36:     body: match[2].trim(),
 37:   };
 38: }
 39: 
 40: /**
 41:  * Load language-specific pattern packs from patterns/{lang}-*.md, plus any
 42:  * user or pro packs in custom/patterns/{lang}-*.md. On a filename collision
 43:  * the custom pack wins (same precedence the persona and lexicon loaders give
 44:  * custom/), so an installed pack can also override a built-in one.
 45:  *
 46:  * @param {string} repoRoot Repository root path.
 47:  * @param {string} lang Language code, such as ko, en, zh, or ja.
 48:  * @param {string[]} [skipPatterns=[]] Pack names to omit, without .md.
 49:  * @returns {Array<{file: string, frontmatter: object|null, body: string, isStructure: boolean, isScoreOnly: boolean}>} Pattern packs.
 50:  * @throws {Error} When the patterns directory or a pattern file cannot be read.
 51:  * @example
 52:  * const patterns = loadPatterns(getRepoRoot(), 'en');
 53:  */
 54: export function loadPatterns(repoRoot, lang, skipPatterns = []) {
 55:   const patternsDir = resolve(repoRoot, 'patterns');
 56:   const customDir = resolve(repoRoot, 'custom', 'patterns');
 57: 
 58:   const discover = (dir) => {
 59:     if (!existsSync(dir)) return [];
 60:     return readdirSync(dir)
 61:       .filter((f) => f.startsWith(`${lang}-`) && f.endsWith('.md'))
 62:       .filter((f) => !skipPatterns.includes(f.slice(0, -3)))
 63:       .map((f) => ({ file: f, path: resolve(dir, f) }));
 64:   };
 65: 
 66:   // custom/ entries shadow built-ins with the same filename.
 67:   const byFile = new Map();
 68:   for (const entry of discover(patternsDir)) byFile.set(entry.file, entry);
 69:   for (const entry of discover(customDir)) byFile.set(entry.file, entry);
 70:   const entries = [...byFile.values()].sort((a, b) => a.file.localeCompare(b.file));
 71: 
 72:   const packs = [];
 73:   for (const entry of entries) {
 74:     const content = loadFile(entry.path);
 75:     const { frontmatter, body } = splitFrontmatter(content);
 76:     packs.push({
 77:       file: entry.file,
 78:       frontmatter,
 79:       body,
 80:       isStructure: frontmatter?.phase === 'structure',
 81:       isScoreOnly: frontmatter?.score_only === true,
 82:     });
 83:   }
 84:   return packs;
 85: }
 86: 
 87: /**
 88:  * Load a named profile from profiles/{profileName}.md after path validation.
 89:  *
 90:  * @param {string} repoRoot Repository root path.
 91:  * @param {string} profileName Profile file stem.
 92:  * @returns {{frontmatter: object|null, body: string}} Parsed profile document.
 93:  * @throws {Error} When the profile name is invalid or the file cannot be read.
 94:  * @example
 95:  * const profile = loadProfile(getRepoRoot(), 'default');
 96:  */
 97: export function loadProfile(repoRoot, profileName) {
 98:   validateProfileName(profileName);
 99:   const profilesDir = resolve(repoRoot, 'profiles');
100:   const profilePath = resolve(profilesDir, `${profileName}.md`);
101:   if (!profilePath.startsWith(profilesDir + sep)) {
102:     // Defense-in-depth after validateProfileName; an escape here is an internal
103:     // invariant breach, not user input — keep it a typed runtime error (#449).
104:     throw runtimeError(
105:       'profile path escaped the profiles directory',
106:       `${profilePath} is outside ${profilesDir}.`,
107:       'This is an internal guard; report it if you see it with a normal --profile value.'
108:     );
109:   }
110:   const content = loadFile(profilePath);
111:   return splitFrontmatter(content);
112: }
113: 
114: /**
115:  * Strip the individual pattern sections a profile marks `suppress` from loaded
116:  * pattern packs, so the rewrite/audit/score prompt never carries those rules.
117:  *
118:  * `pattern-overrides` in a profile's frontmatter is keyed by language then
119:  * numeric pattern id, with action `suppress` or `reduce`. v1 honors `suppress`
120:  * deterministically (the LLM cannot flag a rule it was never given); `reduce`
121:  * has no weight knob yet and is intentionally left in place. Packs without a
122:  * matching override are returned unchanged (same object identity).
123:  *
124:  * @param {Array<{body: string}>} packs Loaded pattern packs from loadPatterns.
125:  * @param {{frontmatter: object|null}|null} profile Loaded profile (loadProfile).
126:  * @param {string} lang Active language code.
127:  * @returns {Array<{body: string}>} Packs with suppressed sections removed.
128:  * @example
129:  * const packs = applyProfilePatternOverrides(loadPatterns(root, 'ko'), loadProfile(root, 'legal'), 'ko');
130:  */
131: export function applyProfilePatternOverrides(packs, profile, lang) {
132:   const overrides = profile?.frontmatter?.['pattern-overrides']?.[lang];
133:   if (!overrides || typeof overrides !== 'object') return packs;
134:   const suppressIds = Object.entries(overrides)
135:     .filter(([, action]) => action === 'suppress')
136:     .map(([id]) => String(id).trim())
137:     .filter(Boolean);
138:   if (suppressIds.length === 0) return packs;
139:   return packs.map((pack) => {
140:     const body = stripPatternSections(pack.body, suppressIds);
141:     return body === pack.body ? pack : { ...pack, body };
142:   });
143: }
144: 
145: // Remove each "### <id>. …" section — heading through the body up to the next
146: // "### " heading or end of pack — including the blank/`---` separator that
147: // trails a removed section, then normalize the seams left behind.
148: function stripPatternSections(body, ids) {
149:   const idSet = new Set(ids.map((id) => String(id)));
150:   const kept = [];
151:   let skipping = false;
152:   for (const line of body.split('\n')) {
153:     const heading = line.match(/^###\s+(\d+)\./);
154:     if (heading) skipping = idSet.has(heading[1]);
155:     if (!skipping) kept.push(line);
156:   }
157:   return kept
158:     .join('\n')
159:     .replace(/\n{3,}/g, '\n\n')
160:     .replace(/\n+---\s*$/g, '')
161:     .trim();
162: }
163: 
164: /**
165:  * Load a Markdown file from the core/ directory.
166:  *
167:  * @param {string} repoRoot Repository root path.
168:  * @param {string} filename Core filename, such as scoring.md.
169:  * @returns {{frontmatter: object|null, body: string}} Parsed core document.
170:  * @throws {Error} When the file cannot be read or frontmatter is invalid.
171:  * @example
172:  * const scoring = loadCoreFile(getRepoRoot(), 'scoring.md');
173:  */
174: export function loadCoreFile(repoRoot, filename) {
175:   const path = resolve(repoRoot, 'core', filename);
176:   const content = loadFile(path);
177:   return splitFrontmatter(content);
178: }
179: 
180: /**
181:  * Maximum size (in bytes) of a single input file patina will read into memory.
182:  * Guards against accidental memory exhaustion on huge or binary inputs (#508 G1).
183:  */
184: export const MAX_INPUT_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024;
185: 
186: /**
187:  * Map a low-level fs error to a typed inputError that names the file path.
188:  *
189:  * @param {string} path File path that failed to read.
190:  * @param {NodeJS.ErrnoException} err Underlying fs error.
191:  * @returns {import('./errors.js').PatinaCliError} Typed input error (exit code 2).
192:  */
193: function mapInputReadError(path, err) {
194:   const byCode = {
195:     ENOENT: 'file not found',
196:     EACCES: 'permission denied',
197:     EISDIR: 'path is a directory',
198:   };
199:   const why = (err && byCode[err.code]) || (err && err.message) || 'unknown read error';
200:   return inputError(
201:     `cannot read input file: ${path}`,
202:     `${path}: ${why}.`,
203:     'Check the path, permissions, and that it points to a readable text file.'
204:   );
205: }
206: 
207: /**
208:  * Read user input text from disk.
209:  *
210:  * @param {string} path Input file path.
211:  * @param {number} [maxBytes=MAX_INPUT_BYTES] Reject files larger than this many bytes.
212:  * @returns {string} UTF-8 input text.
213:  * @throws {import('./errors.js').PatinaCliError} Typed inputError (exit 2) when the file is missing, unreadable, a directory, or over the size cap.
214:  * @example
215:  * const text = loadInputText('draft.md');
216:  */
217: export function loadInputText(path, maxBytes = MAX_INPUT_BYTES) {
218:   let stats;
219:   try {
220:     stats = statSync(path);
221:   } catch (err) {
222:     throw mapInputReadError(path, err);
223:   }
224:   if (stats.size > maxBytes) {
225:     const mb = (maxBytes / (1024 * 1024)).toFixed(0);
226:     throw inputError(
227:       `input file too large: ${path}`,
228:       `The file is ${stats.size} bytes, over the ${maxBytes}-byte (~${mb} MB) limit.`,
229:       'Split the document into smaller files or trim it before running patina.'
230:     );
231:   }
232:   try {
233:     return readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
234:   } catch (err) {
235:     throw mapInputReadError(path, err);
236:   }
237: }
````

## File: docs/research/2026-panel-v2-design.md
````markdown
 1: # Panel v2 design — judge panel reconstitution after kimi's exit
 2: 
 3: Adopted 2026-07-15, before any v2 study uses it. Trigger: the kimi
 4: subscription ends 2026-07-17 (operator-confirmed), removing `judge-kimi` from
 5: the fixed 3-judge panel used by Studies 0–3. The deterministic document scorer
 6: is promoted to chief judge under the 2026-07-14 calibration; gpt and grok remain
 7: the perceptual panel. Every judging run in studies registered after this date
 8: MUST import the executable policy in `scripts/research/panel-v2.mjs`. Study 3,
 9: registered on 2026-07-12, is unaffected.
10: 
11: Grounding: the judge-calibration side study
12: (`2026-judge-calibration.md`, registered design, 192/192 judgments) plus a
13: bridge analysis computed from its existing data — no new calls.
14: 
15: ## The panel
16: 
17: | seat | scorer | role |
18: |---|---|---|
19: | **chief: judge-det** | patina deterministic prose-score (lang-scoped) | primary document verdict (`score >= 35`) and continuous score |
20: | judge-gpt | codex CLI, gpt-5.5 | perceptual corroboration (calibrated AUC 1.00 [1.00, 1.00]) |
21: | judge-grok | xai API, grok-4.5 | perceptual corroboration (calibrated AUC 0.93 [0.83, 1.00]) |
22: 
23: - **Primary document metric:** judge-det's deterministic verdict and continuous
24:   score. The chief lane is auditable, free, and independent of expiring model
25:   subscriptions. Its threshold is fixed at 35; no study may tune it on outcome
26:   data.
27: - **Binding fresh-corpus gate:** before reporting det binary outcomes, each study
28:   MUST pass its own labeled fresh corpus to
29:   `requireFreshCorpusValidation()` in `scripts/research/panel-v2.mjs`.
30:   Accuracy below 0.85 throws and disables the binary verdict column. Continuous
31:   deterministic scores remain reportable. This preserves the 2026-07-14
32:   calibration's anti-overfit condition in executable form.
33: - **Perceptual corroboration:** report the gpt+grok mean and both individual
34:   scores beside the chief result. Both are required for claude-generated
35:   passages; a missing/unparseable required judge is data loss, never silently
36:   reduced to one judge.
37: - **No pooled score:** deterministic and LLM scales remain separate. The det
38:   human mean was 17.7 versus roughly 35 for the LLM judges, so averaging them
39:   would create a number with no stable interpretation.
40: - **Authorship-call metrics:** the reported primary call comes from judge-det
41:   after the fresh-corpus gate. LLM calls are labeled perceptual corroboration,
42:   not quorum votes.
43: 
44: ## Bridge to the old panel (computed from calibration data, n=44 docs)
45: 
46: | quantity | value |
47: |---|---|
48: | old panel (kimi+gpt+grok) AUC | 0.963 |
49: | new LLM panel (gpt+grok) AUC | **0.989** |
50: | per-document correlation old ↔ new | 0.96 |
51: | mean offset (new − old) | +2.0 points |
52: 
53: No measured quality loss — kimi was the panel's weakest discriminator
54: (AUC 0.83, human-doc bias 36.0), so removing it slightly *sharpens* the
55: panel. The +2.0 offset means absolute panel scores drift up slightly.
56: 
57: ## Comparability with the archived series (the important rule)
58: 
59: Whenever a v2-era study compares against stored Study 0–3 ratings, the stored
60: baseline MUST be recomputed as the gpt+grok mean from the archived per-judge
61: rows (they retain each judge's score, so this is exact, not estimated).
62: Never compare a 3-judge historical mean against a 2-judge v2 mean — the
63: kimi column simply drops out of both sides. Kimi's archived scores stay in
64: the artifacts untouched.
65: 
66: ## Corpus generation after kimi
67: 
68: kimi-k2.5 also leaves the generation rotation. Future fresh-corpus studies
69: rotate generator families over the available non-judge family (claude) plus
70: gpt/grok under the existing cross-family judging exclusion: a judge never
71: scores its own family's generations, so gpt/grok-generated documents are
72: scored by the other LLM judge + det lane; claude-generated documents are
73: cross-family for both LLM judges. Per-cell counts are reported as in the
74: calibration study.
75: 
76: ## Agreement statistics
77: 
78: Krippendorff α is computed over the two LLM raters (α over 2 raters is
79: defined and was the pilot's snippet-arm reality); the det lane's agreement
80: with the LLM panel is reported separately (Spearman ρ) as a drift monitor.
81: 
82: ## Effective date & audit trail
83: 
84: - Adopted by the operator's 2026-07-15 autonomous reconstitution order and
85:   binding for studies registered from that date; Study 3 remains archived under
86:   its original panel.
87: - The judge prompt, parser, retry policy, and invocation shapes remain
88:   byte-identical to the S2/S3 runners for the two LLM seats.
89: - Calibration evidence: document threshold 35 reached 0.955 accuracy on 44
90:   leakage-free KO documents. The same-corpus selection caveat is enforced by
91:   the per-study fresh-corpus accuracy floor of 0.85.
92: - Kimi's archived scores and old runners remain untouched for reproducibility;
93:   no new runner may import `judge-kimi`.
````

## File: .patina.default.yaml
````yaml
  1: version: "6.3.1"
  2: language: ko              # Korean (default) -- auto-loads all ko-*.md patterns
  3:                           # language: en      -- English -- auto-loads all en-*.md patterns
  4:                           # language: zh      -- Chinese -- auto-loads all zh-*.md patterns
  5:                           # language: ja      -- Japanese -- auto-loads all ja-*.md patterns
  6:                           # Override with CLI: --lang en / --lang zh / --lang ja
  7: profile: default
  8: output: rewrite           # rewrite | diff | audit | score
  9: 
 10: # Opt-in: request OpenAI-compatible structured output (response_format
 11: # json_object) for ouroboros LLM scoring. Default false; only the openai-http
 12: # backend forwards it (never sent to local CLI backends). Some OpenAI-compatible
 13: # endpoints reject response_format — leave false unless your endpoint supports it.
 14: structured-output: false
 15: 
 16: # Tone categorization (v3.10): register axis (casual/professional) for ko/en.
 17: # Resolution: explicit --tone > config tone > config profile.
 18: # auto = heuristic single-tone detection at SKILL.md Phase 4.5b.
 19: # Unset = profile-only legacy behavior (regression-safe default).
 20: tone:                       # one of: casual, professional, auto (register only)
 21:                             # Override with CLI: --tone <name>
 22:                             # zh/ja with explicit tone: warns and falls back to profile.
 23: 
 24: # Persona harness (v1): Korean rewrite defaults to the conservative preserve
 25: # persona. Non-rewrite and non-Korean surfaces do not apply persona implicitly.
 26: persona: preserve
 27: personas:
 28:   library: personas/ko
 29:   thresholds:
 30:     schema: patina.persona.thresholds.v1
 31:     # source stays `placeholder` because the advisory tuning below is informed by
 32:     # observation, not a formal 2-round promotion. The ENFORCING floors, however,
 33:     # are the product's core safety contract and do not depend on calibration.
 34:     source: placeholder
 35:     calibration_rounds_required: 2
 36:     # --- ENFORCING (safety gate; a miss sets a non-zero exit, non-destructive) ---
 37:     # Meaning + facts only. Enforced when evaluated (mps/fidelity need --verify or a
 38:     # backend-reported score); dropped source numbers always enforce deterministically.
 39:     mps_floor: 70
 40:     fidelity_floor: 70
 41:     # --- ADVISORY (warn only; never blocks or changes the exit code) ---
 42:     # persona_match: voice-quality signal. Observed real KO persona-match runs ~35-56,
 43:     # so 45 flags the low end without firing on every rewrite.
 44:     persona_match_min: 45
 45:     # churn: surface token change, NOT a meaning signal. Live KO humanizing rewrites
 46:     # churn ~0.5-0.85 while preserving meaning, so 0.9 flags only near-total rewrites.
 47:     churn_max: 0.9
 48: 
 49: # Supported languages: ko, en, zh, ja
 50: 
 51: # patterns 목록은 기본 언어(ko)의 팩을 문서화합니다.
 52: # 실제 로딩은 Glob patterns/{lang}-*.md로 자동 탐색합니다.
 53: # 특정 팩을 제외하려면 skip-patterns를 사용하세요.
 54: patterns:
 55:   - ko-structure
 56:   - ko-content
 57:   - ko-language
 58:   - ko-style
 59:   - ko-communication
 60:   - ko-filler
 61:   - ko-viral-hook    # score-only — detection without rewrite
 62: 
 63: # English packs (auto-discovered when language: en):
 64: #   - en-structure (5 patterns: #25-28, #30)
 65: #   - en-content
 66: #   - en-language
 67: #   - en-style
 68: #   - en-communication
 69: #   - en-filler (4 patterns: #22-24, #31)
 70: 
 71: # Chinese packs (auto-discovered when language: zh):
 72: #   - zh-structure (5 patterns: #25-28, #30)
 73: #   - zh-content
 74: #   - zh-language
 75: #   - zh-style
 76: #   - zh-communication
 77: #   - zh-filler (4 patterns: #22-24, #31)
 78: 
 79: # Japanese packs (auto-discovered when language: ja):
 80: #   - ja-structure (5 patterns: #25-28, #30)
 81: #   - ja-content
 82: #   - ja-language
 83: #   - ja-style
 84: #   - ja-communication
 85: #   - ja-filler (4 patterns: #22-24, #31)
 86: 
 87: skip-patterns: []          # additive merge across default/global/project configs
 88: blocklist: []              # additive merge: 추가로 감지할 어휘
 89: allowlist: []              # additive merge: 감지에서 제외할 어휘
 90: # Other arrays are replaced by the higher-precedence config so users can choose
 91: # an exact value instead of merging by accident.
 92: 
 93: # Over-editing guard: before a rewrite, if the deterministic layer finds
 94: # nothing to fix (0 hot paragraphs, near-zero signal, 3+ paragraphs), warn that
 95: # rewriting already-human text can ADD AI-likeness (measured on human documents
 96: # in docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy-study1.md). Advisory only — never
 97: # blocks. Set to false to silence.
 98: over-editing-guard: true
 99: 
100: # Scoring: LLM score remains canonical, with a deterministic shadow score
101: # from src/features/* for reproducible drift checks.
102: scoring:
103:   deterministic:
104:     enabled: true
105:     divergence-threshold: 20  # warn and prefer the more pessimistic score above this delta
106:     combined-weight: 0        # default off for combinedScore; users may opt in (e.g. 0.2)
107: 
108: # Ouroboros: iterative self-improvement loop
109: # Runs the full humanization pipeline repeatedly until the AI score converges.
110: # Enable via config or CLI flag: --ouroboros
111: ouroboros:
112:   enabled: false            # opt-in; --ouroboros CLI flag overrides to true
113:   target-score: 30          # stop when overall score <= this value (0-100)
114:   max-iterations: 3         # hard limit on loop iterations
115:   plateau-threshold: 10     # stop if score improves by less than this between iterations
116:   category-weights:
117:     ko:
118:       content: 0.18
119:       language: 0.18
120:       style: 0.18
121:       communication: 0.13
122:       filler: 0.08
123:       structure: 0.15
124:       viral-hook: 0.10  # score-only pack (excluded from rewrite)
````
